  452: and applying \Cref{thm:mixed-branch-realization} yields the required state $p$, reference $r$, and branch family. Hence \textup{(i)} holds.
  453: 
  454: The quotient formulas are those of \Cref{thm:mixed-branch-realization}(v). The global-section criterion follows from \Cref{thm:mixed-branch-realization}(iii), because
  455: \[
  456: K_\lambda=0
  457: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  458: \operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda)=0
  459: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  460: \phi_\lambda=0.
  461: \]
  462: For the maximal-collapse claim, take $\Lambda$ to be a singleton and $K_\omega=A$. Then the equivalence between \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)} says exactly that such a branch exists if and only if $A$ is generated by at most $\beta$ elements. The final sentence is the case $\beta=1$.
  463: \end{proof}
  464: 

 succeeded in 224ms:
    1: \subsection{Realization of prescribed branch data}
    2: 
    3: The preceding algebra isolates the branchwise and aggregate quotients attached to a given family of hidden subgroups. The next theorem gives a converse existence statement on connected finite nerves with vanishing first homology. It is internal to the fixed finite-nerve framework of the paper: the nerve, the constant band, and the comparison data are fixed in advance, and the theorem constructs local-object and prestack data realizing the prescribed images.
    4: 
    5: The realization proofs use the following explicit admissible-expansion
    6: interface. The site is not an arbitrary full subcategory of intersections.
    7: It is the formal \v{C}ech site of a finite good-cover presentation, so that
    8: the cohomological comparison used later is an actual Leray comparison rather
    9: than a declared comparison datum.
   10: 
   11: \begin{lemma}[Formal \v{C}ech site, Leray comparison, and one-state admissible expansion]\label{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}
   12: Let
   13: \[
   14: \mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
   15: \]
   16: be a finite cover whose nerve $N=N(\mathcal U)$ is realized by a good cover
   17: of a paracompact space $X\simeq |N|$. Let $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ be the
   18: formal \v{C}ech index site over this chosen good-cover realization, defined
   19: as follows. Its objects are $a$, one strict initial object $\emptyset$, and
   20: one object $U_J$ for each nonempty simplex $J\in N$. A realization functor
   21: from this formal category to open subsets of \(X\) sends \(a\) to \(X\),
   22: \(\emptyset\) to the empty open set, and \(U_J\) to the corresponding
   23: nonempty good-cover intersection. This is a pullback-complete formal site,
   24: not the full subcategory on only nonempty intersections. The same strict
   25: initial object represents every empty finite intersection. Thus the only
   26: finite subsets represented by named non-initial objects are the simplices
   27: of \(N\), but a finite subset \(J\subseteq I\) which is not a simplex is
   28: not discarded from the site-level pullback calculus; whenever such a
   29: subset arises as a pullback intersection, the pullback is the
   30: distinguished object \(\emptyset\). In formulas
   31: we write
   32: \[
   33: \widetilde U_J:=
   34: \begin{cases}
   35: U_J,& J\in N,\\
   36: \emptyset,& J\notin N.
   37: \end{cases}
   38: \]
   39: Thus empty intersections are not omitted from the site: all of them are
   40: identified with the same strict initial pullback object $\emptyset$, and
   41: the realization of \(\widetilde U_J\) is the actual intersection
   42: \(\bigcap_{j\in J}U_j\), possibly empty. The order is generated by
   43: \[
   44: \emptyset\to U_J\to U_K\to a
   45: \qquad (K\subseteq J,\; J,K\in N).
   46: \]
   47: There is a unique arrow \(\emptyset\to W\) for every object \(W\), and no
   48: non-initial object maps to \(\emptyset\).
   49: For any two arrows $U_J\to U_L$ and $U_K\to U_L$ with
   50: $L\subseteq J\cap K$, their fibre product is
   51: \[
   52: U_J\times_{U_L}U_K=\widetilde U_{J\cup K}.
   53: \]
   54: If $J\cup K\notin N$, this means that the fibre product is the strict
   55: initial object $\emptyset$. Any fibre product in which one factor is
   56: $\emptyset$ is also $\emptyset$. The pretopology is the smallest
   57: pretopology for which the vertex cover \(\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}\), all of
   58: its pullbacks along arrows of the formal category, all identity covers,
   59: and the empty cover of \(\emptyset\) are covers. Equivalently, it is
   60: obtained by repeatedly applying pullback stability and transitivity to
   61: the vertex cover inside the formal category just described. For example,
   62: the pullback of the vertex cover along \(U_J\to a\) is the family of
   63: arrows
   64: \[
   65: \{\widetilde U_{J\cup\{i\}}\to U_J\}_{i\in I},
   66: \]
   67: with the terms for non-simplices equal to \(\emptyset\). Repeated
   68: occurrences of \(\emptyset\) in such a pulled-back family may be
   69: identified with the same initial object. They are harmless in matching
   70: conditions because the coefficient sheaves used below have zero value at
   71: \(\emptyset\), but the corresponding pullback object is still present in
   72: the site. In particular, disjoint cover members \(U_i,U_j\to a\) have the
   73: pullback \(U_i\times_aU_j=\emptyset\) in the site. The empty object is
   74: omitted only from the nonempty \v{C}ech nerve and from the displayed
   75: nonzero cochain groups, never from the local-reference-site structure.
   76: Every pullback demanded by \Cref{def:local-reference-frame} is therefore
   77: an object of \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), including the empty pullback
   78: objects produced by disjoint members of a covering family.
   79: Thus closure under the pretopology axioms is part of the definition, not a
   80: consequence of omitting any empty pullback object. Then:
   81: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
   82: \item $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ is a pullback-based local reference site
   83: with terminal object $a$; in particular, for every pair of cover members
   84: $U_i,U_j\to a$, the fibre product is the formal object $U_{\{i,j\}}$ if
   85: $\{i,j\}\in N$ and is the strict initial object $\emptyset$ otherwise;
   86: \item the cover $\mathcal U$ remains a cover of $a$ in
   87: $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$, all pullbacks of members of this cover exist
   88: there, and its nonempty \v{C}ech nerve is the finite simplicial complex
   89: $N$;
   90: \item for every abelian group $A$, let $A_{\mathcal U}$ denote the genuine
   91: constant sheaf
   92: on \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\)
   93: \[
   94: A_{\mathcal U}(W)=\operatorname{LC}(|W|,A),
   95: \]
   96: where \(|W|\subseteq X\) is the open set represented by \(W\). This is the
   97: restriction of the genuine constant sheaf of locally constant
   98: \(A\)-valued functions on the chosen good-cover realization. It is this
   99: sheaf, not the identity-restriction constant presheaf on the abstract
  100: index category, that is used as the band. With these coefficients the
  101: \v{C}ech cochain complex of
  102: $\mathcal U$ with coefficients in $A_{\mathcal U}$ and the finite nerve
  103: presentation of any class in $H^2(N(\mathcal U),A)$ are the same finite
  104: cochain data after replacing $\mathcal C_a$ with
  105: $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$. This is a cochain-level identification because
  106: the cover is a chosen good cover: on connected nonempty basic
  107: intersections \(A_{\mathcal U}\) has value \(A\), while on the strict
  108: initial object it has value \(0\);
  109: \item let \(\mathcal B_X\) be any ordinary basis site of the chosen
  110: good-cover realization \(X\) which contains the members of
  111: \(\mathcal U\), their nonempty finite intersections, \(X\), and the empty
  112: open set. The formal site \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) is only the finite
  113: pullback site generated by the selected vertex cover, with the single strict
  114: initial object representing all empty intersections; it is not asserted to
  115: be a basis site for the topology of \(X\). The precise basis condition used
  116: below is internal to this finite site: the pretopology on
  117: \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), and on each slice over a nonempty formal
  118: intersection, is generated by the vertex cover and its iterated pullbacks.
  119: For the genuine constant sheaf \(A_{\mathcal U}\), this makes the vertex
  120: cover an \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-Leray cover in the finite-site sense. Hence the
  121: Cech-to-derived comparison for this generated cover gives a natural
  122: isomorphism
  123: \[
  124: H^n(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U},A_{\mathcal U})\cong \check H^n(\mathcal U,A_{\mathcal U})\cong H^n(N,A)
  125: \]
  126: for all $n$, and the \v{C}ech cocycle on the vertex cover maps to the
  127: corresponding derived cohomology class;
  128: \item for every contravariant functor
  129: \[
  130: F:\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}^{op}\to\mathsf{Set},
  131: \]
  132: there is a one-state $\mathbb L_2$-model $M_2$ with state $p$ and a
  133: distinguished admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ such that $[r]_p=a$, the
  134: local slice site of $r$ is exactly $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$, and the local
  135: object functor on that slice site is $F$; this model is the admissible
  136: $\mathbb L_2$-expansion supplied by
  137: \Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface};
  138: \item if, in addition, a category fibred in groupoids
  139: \[
  140: \mathfrak P\to\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}
  141: \]
  142: with abelian sheaf band $\Band$ satisfies
  143: \[
  144: \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)\cong F
  145: \]
  146: and the automorphism-sheaf condition of
  147: \Cref{def:realization-prestack}, then $M_2$ has an admissible
  148: $\mathbb L_3$-expansion $M_3$ in which $r$ has realization prestack
  149: $\mathfrak P$, band $\Band$, and stackification $a(\mathfrak P)$.
  150: \end{enumerate}
  151: \end{lemma}
  152: 
  153: \begin{proof}
  154: The formal index category is thin and has terminal object $a$. We first
  155: verify the pullbacks, since they are part of the local-reference-site
  156: structure and not merely a convention about the \v{C}ech cochain complex.
  157: Suppose that \(U_J\to U_L\) and \(U_K\to U_L\) are two maps, so
  158: \(L\subseteq J\cap K\). If \(J\cup K\in N\), then \(U_{J\cup K}\) maps to
  159: both \(U_J\) and \(U_K\), and any object mapping to both over \(U_L\) is
  160: indexed by a simplex containing \(J\cup K\), hence factors uniquely through
  161: \(U_{J\cup K}\). If \(J\cup K\notin N\), no nonempty simplex can contain the
  162: union, so the only object mapping to both over \(U_L\) is the strict initial
  163: object \(\emptyset\). Pullbacks involving \(\emptyset\) are \(\emptyset\) by
  164: strict initiality. Therefore every fibre product required by
  165: \Cref{def:local-reference-frame} is an object of \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\).
  166: In particular, for every pair in the covering family \(\{U_i\to a\}\) the
  167: pullback is \(U_{\{i,j\}}\) when \(\{i,j\}\in N\) and is \(\emptyset\)
  168: otherwise. This proves the pullback-object clause in \textup{(i)}; the
  169: identity, pullback-stability, and transitivity pretopology axioms hold by
  170: construction of the generated pretopology.
  171: 
  172: The covering family $\mathcal U$ is one of the generators of the
  173: pretopology on $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$. A finite set $J\subseteq I$
  174: spans a simplex precisely when the iterated pullback of the corresponding
  175: $U_i$ is a nonempty object $U_J$ rather than $\emptyset$. Hence the
  176: \v{C}ech nerve of the cover is $N$, proving \textup{(ii)}.
  177: 
  178: The sheaf \(A_{\mathcal U}\) is defined by restricting the genuine
  179: constant sheaf of locally constant \(A\)-valued functions on \(X\) along
  180: the realization functor \(W\mapsto |W|\). Equivalently,
  760: Hence
  761: \[
  762: \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(U)\cong\Lambda
  763: \]
  764: for every nonempty proper intersection object $U\to a$. Over $a$, the
  765: nonzero summands have empty fibre and the neutral summands indexed by
  766: \(\Lambda_0\) have one object, so
  767: \[
  768: \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\cong\Lambda_0.
  769: \]
  770: These identifications are compatible with the restriction maps, giving \textup{(iv)}.
  771: 
  772: By \Cref{lem:formal-label-sheafification}, applied to the displayed
  773: presheaf \(F\) and the subset \(\Lambda_0\subseteq\Lambda\), the
  774: sheafification of \(F\) has terminal sections indexed by \(\Lambda\). By
  775: \Cref{prop:stackification-components}, stackification identifies
  776: \[
  777: \pi_0(a(\mathfrak P))\cong aF,
  778: \]
  779: so the visible components over $a$ are indexed by $\Lambda$.
  780: 
  781: For a fixed $\lambda$, take on $U_i$ the object $x_i^{(\lambda,U_i)}$ if $\lambda\notin\Lambda_0$ and the distinguished constant object if $\lambda\in\Lambda_0$. On $U_{ij}$ take the morphism $(0,i,j)$ in the nonzero case and the identity in the zero case. The triple defect is
  782: \[
  783: (0,i,k)^{-1}\circ(0,j,k)\circ(0,i,j)
  784: =
  785: (\alpha_{\lambda,ijk},i,i)
  786: \]
  787: in the twisted case and is $0$ in the constant case. Under the band identification this is exactly $\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}$. Thus the Cech obstruction cocycle of the $\lambda$-component is $\alpha_\lambda$, proving \textup{(v)}.
  788: \end{proof}
  789: 
  790: \begin{theorem}[Admissible-expansion realization of prescribed branchwise hidden subgroups on a formal \v{C}ech site]\label{thm:prescribed-branch-realization}
  791: Let
  792: \[
  793: \mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
  794: \]
  795: be a finite good-cover presentation in a pullback-based slice site $\mathcal C_a$ whose nonempty \v{C}ech nerve
  796: \[
  797: N:=N(\mathcal U)
  798: \]
  799: is connected. Equivalently, the realization of the formal cover is a
  800: paracompact space homotopy equivalent to $|N|$ and all nonempty finite
  801: intersections of members of $\mathcal U$ are acyclic for constant
  802: coefficients. Let $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ be the formal \v{C}ech site of
  803: \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}; it includes the strict
  804: initial object $\emptyset$ for all empty intersections, so disjoint cover
  805: members have a pullback in the site even though they do not contribute a
  806: nonzero finite-nerve cochain. Let $A$ be a finite abelian group, and let
  807: $A_{\mathcal U}$ denote the genuine constant sheaf on
  808: $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ whose value on each nonempty basic intersection
  809: is identified with $A$. View $A_{\mathcal U}$ as the automorphism band of
  810: the realization prestack constructed below, and assume
  811: \[
  812: H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0.
  813: \]
  814: Use the Leray comparison isomorphism of
  815: \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(iv). Let $\Lambda$ be a finite nonempty set, and let
  816: \[
  817: \phi_\lambda:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A
  818: \]
  819: be a nonzero homomorphism for each $\lambda\in\Lambda$. Then, in the
  820: admissible-expansion model class generated by
  821: \Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}, the following typed data
  822: define a one-state $\mathbb L_2/\mathbb L_3$ model \(M\): the one-state
  823: \(\mathbb L_1\)-reduct, the formal site \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), the
  824: local object functor \(F_{p,s}\) constructed below, and the realization
  825: prestack
  826: \[
  827: \mathfrak P_r\to \mathcal C_{\mathcal U}
  828: \]
  829: with band \(A_{\mathcal U}\). In that generated model there are a state
  830: \(p\) and a distinguished admitted reference \(r:\mathsf{Ref}_s\) with
  831: \([r]_p=a\), local slice site \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), and
  832: \(\mathfrak L_r:=a(\mathfrak P_r)\), such that the following hold:
  833: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
  834: \item
  835: \[
  836: \Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \Lambda;
  837: \]
  838: \item under the identification in \textup{(i)}, for each $\lambda\in\Lambda$ the corresponding component gerbe $\mathfrak G_\lambda\subseteq \mathfrak L_r$ has branch obstruction class
  839: \[
  840: \omega_\lambda\in H^2(N,A)
  841: \]
  842: satisfying
  843: \[
  844: \mathrm{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\phi_\lambda,
  845: \qquad
  846: K_\lambda=\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda);
  847: \]
  848: \item $\mathfrak P_r$ is globally conservative at $a$ by the vacuous terminal-fibre case
  849: \[
  850: \mathfrak P_r(a)=\varnothing=\mathfrak L_r(a),
  851: \]
  852: and
  853: \[
  854: M,p\Vdash \Null^{\mathrm{glue}}_s(r);
  855: \]
  856: \item the aggregate visibility quotients satisfy
  857: \[
  858: A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{br}}(r)\cong A\Big/\bigcap_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda),
  859: \qquad
  860: A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)\cong A\Big/\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda).
  861: \]
  862: \end{enumerate}
  863: In particular, within the fixed finite-nerve and admissible-expansion
  864: framework above, every finite family of subgroups of $A$ arising as images of homomorphisms
  865: \[
  866: H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A
  867: \]
  868: is realized as the family of finite-nerve-class hidden subgroups of the visible branches of one admitted reference.
  869: \end{theorem}
  870: 
  871: \begin{proof}
  872: Since
  873: \[
  874: H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0,
  875: \]
  876: the universal coefficient sequence reduces to an isomorphism
  877: \[
  878: \eta_A:H^2(N,A)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A).
  879: \]
  880: For each $\lambda\in\Lambda$, choose the unique class
  881: \[
  882: \omega_\lambda\in H^2(N,A)
  883: \]
  884: with
  885: \[
  886: \eta_A(\omega_\lambda)=\phi_\lambda,
  887: \]
  888: and choose a normalized simplicial cocycle
  889: \[
  890: \alpha_\lambda\in Z^2(N,A)
  891: \]
  892: representing $\omega_\lambda$, with the alternating ordered-triple extension
  893: of \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model}.
  894: 
  895: By \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(i)--(iv), pass to the
  896: formal \v{C}ech site $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ appearing in the statement.
  897: The cover $\mathcal U$, its nerve, and the constant-band finite cochain
  898: data are the same, and Leray comparison identifies
  899: $H^2(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U},A_{\mathcal U})$ with $H^2(N,A)$. The derived branch class
  900: used below is therefore the actual derived class corresponding to the
  901: chosen finite-nerve cocycle.
  902: 
  903: Define the local object functor
  904: \[
  905: F_{p,s}:\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}^{op}\to\mathsf{Set}
  906: \]
  907: by
  908: \[
  909: F_{p,s}(U):=\Lambda
  910: \qquad
  911: \text{for every nonempty proper intersection object }U\to a,
  912: \]
  913: \[
  914: F_{p,s}(a):=\varnothing,
  915: \]
  916: and
  917: \[
  918: F_{p,s}(\emptyset):=\{\ast\}.
  919: \]
  920: Restrictions between nonempty proper intersection objects are identities,
  921: and every restriction to $\emptyset$ is the unique map. This is the
  922: presheaf \(F_{\Lambda_0}\) of
  923: \Cref{lem:formal-label-sheafification} with \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\).
  924: That lemma uses the plus--plus description of sheafification, not a
  925: one-step matching-family replacement, and it treats pullbacks equal to
  926: \(\emptyset\) as strict initial objects carrying only the unique section.
  927: Since the nonempty nerve is connected, it gives
  928: \[
  929: \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\cong \Lambda.
  930: \]
  931: 
  932: For each $\lambda\in\Lambda$, define a category fibred in groupoids
  933: \[
  934: \mathfrak P_\lambda\to \mathcal C_{\mathcal U}
  935: \]
  936: by the nonzero-summand construction of
  937: \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model}. Thus
  938: $\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\varnothing$; over every nonempty proper formal
  939: intersection object $W$ its objects are
  940: $x_i^{(\lambda,W)}$ for $i\in I(W):=\{i\in I\mid W\to U_i\}$; its morphism
  941: sets are the sheaf sections $A_{\mathcal U}(W)$, identified with \(A\) on
  942: connected nonempty \(W\); and composition is twisted by the normalized
  943: cocycle $\alpha_\lambda$ under this identification. Over $\emptyset$ the
  944: fibre is terminal because \(A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0\). The groupoid
  945: laws, strict functoriality, prestack
  946: condition, and genuine constant-band automorphism-sheaf condition are
  947: exactly \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model}(i)--(iii), whose banding
  948: uses \Cref{lem:formal-site-constant-sheaf-banding}.
  949: 
  950: Let
  951: \[
  952: \mathfrak P_r:=\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathfrak P_\lambda.
  953: \]
  954: As in \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model}, this is the fibrewise
  955: coproduct over nonempty objects with the shared terminal fibre over
  956: $\emptyset$.
  957: The verification that this is a fibred prestack, that its restrictions are strictly functorial, that its automorphism sheaves are the genuine constant band $A_{\mathcal U}$, and that the normalized cocycle identities give the groupoid laws is precisely \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model} with $\Lambda_0=\varnothing$. On every nonempty proper intersection object $U\to a$, each summand $\mathfrak P_\lambda(U)$ is connected, while there are no morphisms between distinct summands. Consequently,
  958: \[
  959: \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)(U)\cong \Lambda
  960: \qquad
  961: \text{for every nonempty proper }U\to a,
  962: \]
  963: and
  964: \[
  965: \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)(a)=\varnothing.
  966: \]
  967: Over $\emptyset$ the shared terminal fibre gives
  968: $\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)(\emptyset)\cong\{\ast\}=F_{p,s}(\emptyset)$.
  969: Hence
  970: \[
  971: \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)\cong F_{p,s}.
  972: \]
  973: By \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(v)--(vi), the functor
  974: $F_{p,s}$ and the prestack $\mathfrak P_r$ determine a one-state semantic
  975: model $M$ with admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at its unique state $p$,
  976: with reference class $[r]_p=a$, local slice site $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$,
  977: local object functor $F_{p,s}$, and realization prestack $\mathfrak P_r$ with
  978: genuine constant band $A_{\mathcal U}$. This admissibility is exactly the expansion convention of
  979: \Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}; no additional semantic
  980: closure axiom for freely adjoining local-object or prestack data is being used.
  981: At this point all semantic interfaces required by the statement have been checked: the admitted reference is $r$; the formal \v{C}ech site is $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$; the local object functor is the displayed $F_{p,s}$; the prestack band is the genuine constant sheaf $A_{\mathcal U}$; the cover $\mathcal U$ is compared with derived cohomology by the Leray isomorphism of \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(iv); the visible classes will be the sheafified terminal sections of $F_{p,s}$; and the forcing clauses for $\CompSec_s$, $\Sec_s$, and $\Null_s^{\mathrm{glue}}$ are read from these compatible-section and terminal-section data.
  982: By \Cref{prop:stackification-components},
  983: \[
  984: \pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)\cong a_{p,s}F_{p,s},
  985: \]
  986: so the visible value classes at $a$ are exactly
  987: \[
  988: \Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\cong \Lambda.
  989: \]
  990: This proves \textup{(i)}.
  991: 
  992: For each $\lambda\in\Lambda$, let $\mathfrak G_\lambda$ be the full substack of $\mathfrak L_r$ determined by the visible branch $\lambda$. On the cover $\mathcal U$, take the local objects
  993: \[
  994: x_i^{(\lambda)}:=x_i^{(\lambda,U_i)}
  995: \]
  996: and, on each overlap $U_{ij}$, take the distinguished isomorphism
  997: \[
  998: \phi_{ij}^{(\lambda)}:=(0,i,j):x_i^{(\lambda)}|_{U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim}x_j^{(\lambda)}|_{U_{ij}}.
  999: \]
 1000: Then on every triple overlap $U_{ijk}$,
 1001: \[
 1002: \bigl(\phi_{ik}^{(\lambda)}\bigr)^{-1}\circ
 1003: \phi_{jk}^{(\lambda)}\circ
 1004: \phi_{ij}^{(\lambda)}
 1005: =
 1006: (\alpha_{\lambda,ijk},i,i),
 1007: \]
 1008: which identifies with $\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}\in A$ under the automorphism-band identification. Therefore the \v{C}ech cocycle produced by \Cref{thm:cech-bridge-compatible-realizations} is exactly $\alpha_\lambda$, and the branch obstruction class of $\mathfrak G_\lambda$ is
 1009: \[
 1010: \omega_\lambda=[\alpha_\lambda].
 1011: \]
 1012: This is the stackification-component compatibility clause of \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model} written on the cover $\mathcal U$.
 1013: Thus the finite-nerve comparison uses exactly the fixed good cover
 1014: $\mathcal U$, the constant band $A$, and the normalized cocycle
 1015: $\alpha_\lambda$ supplied by the homomorphism $\phi_\lambda$; the passage
 1016: to derived $H^2(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U},A_{\mathcal U})$ is the Leray comparison of
 1017: \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(iv).
 1018: Since
 1019: \[
 1020: \mathrm{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\eta_A(\omega_\lambda)=\phi_\lambda,
 1021: \]
 1022: one gets
 1023: \[
 1024: K_\lambda=\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda).
 1025: \]
 1026: This proves \textup{(ii)}.
 1027: 
 1028: Because each $\phi_\lambda$ is nonzero and $\eta_A$ is an isomorphism, each class $\omega_\lambda$ is nonzero. By \Cref{prop:derived-branch-classification}, every component gerbe $\mathfrak G_\lambda$ is therefore non-neutral, and \Cref{lem:terminal-fibre-neutrality} gives
 1029: \[
 1030: \mathfrak G_\lambda(a)=\varnothing
 1031: \qquad
 1032: (\forall \lambda\in\Lambda).
 1033: \]
 1034: Hence
 1035: \[
 1036: \mathfrak L_r(a)=\varnothing.
 1037: \]
 1038: Since also
 1039: \[
 1040: \mathfrak P_r(a)=\varnothing,
 1041: \]
 1042: the stackification functor on the terminal fibre is
 1043: \[
 1044: (\iota_r)_a:\mathfrak P_r(a)\to \mathfrak L_r(a),
 1045: \]
 1046: that is, the empty functor $\varnothing\to\varnothing$, and it is essentially surjective. Thus $\mathfrak P_r$ is globally conservative at $a$.
 1047: 
 1048: Moreover,
 1049: \[
 1050: \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\cong \Lambda\neq\varnothing,
 1051: \]
 1052: and the cover $\mathcal U$ carries an explicit strict matching family:
 1053: choose any $\lambda\in\Lambda$ and put the section $\lambda\in
 1054: F_{p,s}(U_i)=\Lambda$ on every member $U_i$. Restrictions between
 1055: nonempty proper intersections are identities, and restrictions to
 1056: $\emptyset$ are unique, so the family agrees on every pullback overlap.
 1057: Hence \Cref{def:existence-levels}(ii) gives
 1058: \[
 1059: M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r).
 1060: \]
 1061: On the other hand,
 1062: \[
 1063: F_{p,s}(a)=\varnothing,
 1064: \]
 1065: so
 1066: \[
 1067: M,p\nVdash \Sec_s(r).
 1068: \]
 1069: Since $r$ is admitted by construction, it follows that
 1070: \[
 1071: M,p\Vdash \Null^{\mathrm{glue}}_s(r).
 1072: \]
 1073: This proves \textup{(iii)}.
 1074: 
 1075: Finally, \textup{(iv)} is \Cref{thm:branch-sensitive-visible-quotient,thm:branch-uniform-visible-quotient} applied to the family
 1076: \[
 1077: K_\lambda=\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda)
 1078: \qquad
 1079: (\lambda\in\Lambda).
 1080: \]
 1081: The last sentence is the same statement rewritten in terms of the images of the chosen homomorphisms.
 1082: \end{proof}
 1083: 
 1084: The preceding realization theorem forces every branch to be non-neutral. The next theorem removes that restriction and identifies the neutral branches exactly with the zero homomorphisms.
 1085:

So far, the bouquet theorem reduces to two standard facts: every prescribed \(H_2\to G\) can be installed as a chosen gerbe cocycle on a custom one-state model, and a finite abelian group splits into two nonzero \(\beta\)-generated summands exactly under the stated elementary condition. That makes the theorem formally nontrivial but mathematically much shallower than its semantic packaging suggests.

    1: \section{Comparison of branches and realization}\label{sec:branch-aggregation}
    2: 
    3: We compare the quotients attached to the different visible branches of a single admitted reference. Retaining the branch label leads to a branch-sensitive quotient, while forgetting it leads to a branch-uniform quotient. The comparison results below are formal once the branchwise intrinsic data have been fixed, whereas the realization theorems later in the section remain internal to explicitly chosen finite nerves and constant bands.
    4: 
    5: \begin{definition}[Branchwise intrinsic data]\label{def:branchwise-intrinsic-data}
    6: Fix an admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$, and assume:
    7: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
    8: \item the hypotheses of \Cref{def:cech-gluing-obstruction} hold;
    9: \item the set $\Vis_{p,s}(r)$ is finite and nonempty;
   10: \item every component gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ is banded by the same finite abelian group $A$ and admits a finite nerve presentation of its branch obstruction class $\omega_v$.
   11: \end{enumerate}
   12: For each visible branch $v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)$, write
   13: \[
   14: K_v:=K_{\omega_v},
   15: \qquad
   16: A_{\mathrm{vis}}^v:=A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\omega_v}=A/K_v,
   17: \qquad
   18: \pi_v:A\twoheadrightarrow A_{\mathrm{vis}}^v.
   19: \]
   20: \end{definition}
   21: 
   22: \begin{theorem}[Branch-sensitive visible quotient]\label{thm:branch-sensitive-visible-quotient}
   23: In the setting of \Cref{def:branchwise-intrinsic-data}, consider the diagonal map
   24: \[
   25: \Delta_{\mathrm{br}}:A\longrightarrow \prod_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} A_{\mathrm{vis}}^v,
   26: \qquad
   27: x\longmapsto (\pi_v(x))_v.
   28: \]
   29: Then
   30: \[
   31: \ker(\Delta_{\mathrm{br}})=\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v.
   32: \]
   33: Consequently, the quotient
   34: \[
   35: A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{br}}(r):=A\Big/\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v
   36: \]
   37: is canonically isomorphic to $\mathrm{Im}(\Delta_{\mathrm{br}})$. It is the maximal quotient of $A$ that remains visible once the branch label itself is allowed as part of the semantic context.
   38: \end{theorem}
   39: 
   40: \begin{proof}
   41: For $x\in A$ one has $\Delta_{\mathrm{br}}(x)=0$ if and only if $\pi_v(x)=0$ for every visible branch $v$, which is equivalent to $x\in K_v$ for every $v$. Hence
   42: \[
   43: \ker(\Delta_{\mathrm{br}})=\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v.
   44: \]
   45: The first isomorphism theorem therefore yields
   46: \[
   47: \mathrm{Im}(\Delta_{\mathrm{br}})
   48: \cong
   49: A\Big/\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v.
   50: \]
   51: Thus, once the visible branch is known, two elements of $A$ remain semantically indistinguishable exactly when they are indistinguishable on every individual branch quotient.
   52: \end{proof}
   53: 
   54: \begin{theorem}[Branch-uniform visible quotient]\label{thm:branch-uniform-visible-quotient}
   55: In the setting of \Cref{def:branchwise-intrinsic-data}, let
   56: \[
   57: X_v:=\{\chi\in \widehat A\mid \chi_*(\omega_v)=0\}
   58: \]
   59: for each visible branch $v$, and define the uniformly admissible characters by
   60: \[
   61: X^{\mathrm{unif}}(r):=\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} X_v.
   62: \]
   63: Then
   64: \[
   65: X^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)=\mathrm{Ann}\Bigl(\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr).
   66: \]
   67: Consequently, the quotient
   68: \[
   69: A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{unif}}(r):=A\Big/\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v
   70: \]
   71: is exactly the quotient detected by the characters that are admissible on every visible branch simultaneously.
   72: \end{theorem}
   73: 
   74: \begin{proof}
   75: By \Cref{thm:intrinsic-visible-quotient}, one has
   76: \[
   77: X_v=\mathrm{Ann}(K_v)
   78: \]
   79: for every $v$. Therefore
   80: \[
   81: X^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)
   82: =
   83: \bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} \mathrm{Ann}(K_v)
   84: =
   85: \mathrm{Ann}\Bigl(\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr),
   86: \]
   87: where the last identity is the standard annihilator formula for finite abelian groups \cite[Part~I, Chap.~2]{Terras1999}. Finite abelian duality then gives
   88: \[
   89: \bigcap_{\chi\in X^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)} \ker\chi
   90: =
   91: \sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v.
   92: \]
   93: Hence the quotient detected by all uniformly admissible characters is precisely
   94: \[
   95: A\Big/\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v.
   96: \qedhere
   97: \]
   98: \end{proof}
   99: 
  100: \begin{definition}[Branch comparison map and resolution kernel]\label{def:branch-comparison-map}
  101: In the setting of \Cref{def:branchwise-intrinsic-data}, let
  102: \[
  103: \pi_{\mathrm{br}}:A\twoheadrightarrow A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{br}}(r)
  104: \qquad\text{and}\qquad
  105: \pi_{\mathrm{unif}}:A\twoheadrightarrow A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)
  106: \]
  107: be the canonical quotient maps. Since
  108: \[
  109: \bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v
  110: \subseteq
  111: \sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v,
  112: \]
  113: there is a unique surjective homomorphism
  114: \[
  115: \beta_r:A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{br}}(r)\twoheadrightarrow A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)
  116: \]
  117: such that
  118: \[
  119: \beta_r\circ \pi_{\mathrm{br}}=\pi_{\mathrm{unif}}.
  120: \]
  121: Its kernel is denoted
  122: \[
  123: B_{\mathrm{res}}(r):=
  124: \ker(\beta_r)
  125: \cong
  126: \Bigl(\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr)\Big/\Bigl(\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr)
  127: \]
  128: and called the \emph{branch-resolution kernel}.
  129: \end{definition}
  130: 
  131: \begin{theorem}[Exact comparison of branch-sensitive and branch-uniform visibility]\label{thm:branch-comparison-exact-sequence}
  132: In the setting of \Cref{def:branch-comparison-map}, there is a short exact sequence
  133: \[
  134: 0\longrightarrow B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)\longrightarrow A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{br}}(r)\xrightarrow{\ \beta_r\ } A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)\longrightarrow 0.
  135: \]
  136: Equivalently,
  137: \[
  138: \ker(\beta_r)
  139: \cong
  140: \Bigl(\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr)\Big/\Bigl(\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr).
  141: \]
  142: Thus the precise gap between branch-sensitive and branch-uniform visibility is measured by the lattice interval from $\bigcap_v K_v$ to $\sum_v K_v$.
  143: \end{theorem}
  144: 
  145: \begin{proof}
  146: Existence and uniqueness of $\beta_r$ follow from the universal property of the quotient because
  147: \[
  148: \bigcap_v K_v\subseteq \sum_v K_v.
  149: \]
  150: Surjectivity is immediate from surjectivity of $\pi_{\mathrm{unif}}$. By the third isomorphism theorem,
  151: \[
  152: \ker(\beta_r)
  153: \cong
  154: \Bigl(\sum_v K_v\Bigr)\Big/\Bigl(\bigcap_v K_v\Bigr),
  155: \]
  156: which gives the claimed exact sequence.
  157: \end{proof}
  158: 
  159: \begin{corollary}[Criterion for the absence of branch gain]\label{cor:no-branch-gain-criterion}
  160: In the setting of \Cref{def:branch-comparison-map}, the following are equivalent:
  161: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
  162: \item $\beta_r$ is an isomorphism;
  163: \item $B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)=0$;
  164: \item
  165: \[
  166: \bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v
  167: =
  168: \sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v;
  169: \]
  170: \item the hidden subgroups are branch-constant:
  171: \[
  172: K_v=K_w
  173: \qquad
  174: (\forall v,w\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)).
  175: \]
  176: \end{enumerate}
  177: Hence branch sensitivity yields no additional visible information exactly when all visible branches have the same finite-nerve-class hidden subgroup.
  178: \end{corollary}
  179: 
  180: \begin{proof}
  181: The equivalence of \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)} is exactness of the sequence in \Cref{thm:branch-comparison-exact-sequence}. Condition \textup{(ii)} is equivalent to \textup{(iii)} by the quotient description of $B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)$.
  182: 
  183: If \textup{(iii)} holds, write the common subgroup as $H$. Then
  184: \[
  185: H=\bigcap_v K_v\subseteq K_v\subseteq \sum_v K_v=H
  186: \]
  187: for every visible branch $v$, so each $K_v=H$, proving \textup{(iv)}. Conversely, if all $K_v$ are equal to one subgroup $H$, then clearly
  188: \[
  189: \bigcap_v K_v=H=\sum_v K_v.
  190: \qedhere
  191: \]
  192: \end{proof}
  193: 
  194: \begin{corollary}[Dual character description of the branch-resolution kernel]\label{cor:branch-resolution-dual}
  195: In the setting of \Cref{def:branch-comparison-map},
  196: \[
  197: \widehat{A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{br}}(r)}
  198: \cong
  199: \sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} X_v,
  200: \qquad
  201: \widehat{A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)}
  202: \cong
  203: \bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} X_v.
  204: \]
  205: Moreover,
  206: \[
  207: \widehat{B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)}
  208: \cong
  209: \Bigl(\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} X_v\Bigr)\Big/\Bigl(\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} X_v\Bigr).
  210: \]
  211: Thus the character channels created by branch resolution are exactly the channels that belong to the subgroup generated by the branchwise admissible characters but fail to be uniformly admissible.
  212: \end{corollary}
  213: 
  214: \begin{proof}
  215: By \Cref{thm:intrinsic-visible-quotient}, one has
  216: \[
  217: X_v=\mathrm{Ann}(K_v)
  218: \]
  219: for every visible branch $v$. Finite abelian duality then gives
  220: \[
  221: \widehat{A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{br}}(r)}
  222: \cong
  223: \mathrm{Ann}\Bigl(\bigcap_v K_v\Bigr)
  224: =
  225: \sum_v \mathrm{Ann}(K_v)
  226: =
  227: \sum_v X_v
  228: \]
  229: and, by \Cref{thm:branch-uniform-visible-quotient},
  230: \[
  231: \widehat{A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)}
  232: \cong
  233: \bigcap_v X_v.
  234: \]
  235: Dualizing the short exact sequence of \Cref{thm:branch-comparison-exact-sequence} yields an exact sequence
  236: \[
  237: 0\longrightarrow \widehat{A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)}
  238: \longrightarrow \widehat{A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{br}}(r)}
  239: \longrightarrow \widehat{B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)}
  240: \longrightarrow 0,
  241: \]
  242: and substituting the two identifications above gives the claimed quotient description of $\widehat{B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)}$.
  243: \end{proof}
  244: 
  245: \begin{definition}[Branch-resolution code]\label{def:branch-resolution-code}
  246: In the setting of \Cref{def:branch-comparison-map}, a \emph{branch-resolution code} consists of a finite set $R$ and an injective map
  247: \[
  248: \iota_{\mathrm{res}}:A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{br}}(r)\hookrightarrow A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)\times R
  249: \]
  250: such that
  251: \[
  252: \mathrm{pr}_1\circ \iota_{\mathrm{res}}=\beta_r.
  253: \]
  254: \end{definition}
  255: 
  256: \begin{theorem}[Exact branch-resolution budget]\label{thm:branch-resolution-budget}
  257: Every branch-resolution code satisfies
  258: \[
  259: |R|\ge |B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)|
  260: =
  261: \left|\Bigl(\sum_v K_v\Bigr)\Big/\Bigl(\bigcap_v K_v\Bigr)\right|.
  262: \]
  263: Equivalently,
  264: \[
  265: \log_2|R|
  266: \ge
  267: \log_2|B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)|.
  268: \]
  269: This bound is sharp.
  270: \end{theorem}
  271: 
  272: \begin{proof}
  273: Since $\mathrm{pr}_1\circ \iota_{\mathrm{res}}=\beta_r$, every fiber of $\beta_r$ injects into $R$. By \Cref{thm:branch-comparison-exact-sequence}, each fiber of $\beta_r$ is a coset of $B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)$ and therefore has cardinality $|B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)|$. Hence
  274: \[
  275: |R|\ge |B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)|.
  276: \]
  277: 
  278: For sharpness, choose a finite set $R$ with
  279: \[
  280: |R|=|B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)|.
  281: \]
  282: Choose a set-theoretic section
  283: \[
  284: s:A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)\to A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{br}}(r)
  285: \]
  286: of $\beta_r$, and choose an injection
  287: \[
  288: j:B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)\hookrightarrow R.
  289: \]
  290: Define
  291: \[
  292: \iota_{\mathrm{res}}(x):=
  293: \bigl(\beta_r(x),j(x-s(\beta_r(x)))\bigr).
  294: \]
  295: Since $x-s(\beta_r(x))\in \ker(\beta_r)=B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)$, the map is well defined. If
  296: \[
  297: \iota_{\mathrm{res}}(x)=\iota_{\mathrm{res}}(y),
  298: \]
  299: then $\beta_r(x)=\beta_r(y)$ and
  300: \[
  301: j(x-s(\beta_r(x)))=j(y-s(\beta_r(y))).
  302: \]
  303: Injectivity of $j$ gives $x=y$, so $\iota_{\mathrm{res}}$ is injective and the bound is attained.
  304: \end{proof}
  305: 
  306: \begin{corollary}[Factorization of uniform hidden information]\label{cor:uniform-hidden-budget-factorization}
  307: In the setting of \Cref{def:branch-comparison-map},
  308: \[
  309: \left|\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\right|
  310: =
  311: |B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)|
  312: \cdot
  313: \left|\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\right|.
  314: \]
  315: Equivalently,
  316: \[
  317: \log_2\left|\sum_v K_v\right|
  318: =
  319: \log_2|B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)|
  320: +
  321: \log_2\left|\bigcap_v K_v\right|.
  322: \]
  323: Thus the hidden-state budget behind uniform visibility factors exactly into a branch-resolution term and a residual within-branch hidden term.
  324: \end{corollary}
  325: 
  326: \begin{proof}
  327: By \Cref{def:branch-comparison-map},
  328: \[
  329: |B_{\mathrm{res}}(r)|
  330: =
  331: \left|\Bigl(\sum_v K_v\Bigr)\Big/\Bigl(\bigcap_v K_v\Bigr)\right|
  332: =
  333: \frac{|\sum_v K_v|}{|\bigcap_v K_v|}.
  334: \]
  335: Rearranging gives the first displayed identity, and the second follows after taking base-$2$ logarithms.
  336: \end{proof}
  337: 
  338: \begin{definition}[Branch-preserving strictification code]\label{def:branch-preserving-strictification-code}
  339: In the setting of \Cref{def:branchwise-intrinsic-data}, a \emph{branch-preserving strictification code} consists of a finite set $R$ and, for each visible branch $v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)$, an injective map
  340: \[
  341: \iota_v:A\hookrightarrow A_{\mathrm{vis}}^v\times R
  342: \]
  343: such that
  344: \[
  345: \mathrm{pr}_1\circ \iota_v=\pi_v.
  346: \]
  347: \end{definition}
  348: 
  349: \begin{theorem}[Multi-branch strictification budget]\label{thm:multi-branch-strictification-budget}
  350: Every branch-preserving strictification code satisfies
  351: \[
  352: |R|\ge \max_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} |K_v|.
  353: \]
  354: Equivalently,
  355: \[
  356: \log_2|R|\ge \max_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} \log_2|K_v|.
  357: \]
  358: This bound is sharp.
  359: \end{theorem}
  360: 
  361: \begin{proof}
  362: Fix a visible branch $v$. Since $\mathrm{pr}_1\circ \iota_v=\pi_v$, every fiber of $\pi_v$ injects into $R$. By \Cref{def:intrinsic-visible-quotient}, every fiber of $\pi_v$ is a coset of $K_v$ and hence has cardinality $|K_v|$. Therefore $|R|\ge |K_v|$ for every $v$, which proves the lower bound.
  363: 
  364: For sharpness, choose a finite set $R$ with
  365: \[
  366: |R|=\max_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} |K_v|.
  367: \]
  368: For each $v$, choose an injection
  369: \[
  370: j_v:K_v\hookrightarrow R
  371: \]
  372: and a set-theoretic section
  373: \[
  374: s_v:A_{\mathrm{vis}}^v\to A
  375: \]
  376: of $\pi_v$. Define
  377: \[
  378: \iota_v(x):=\bigl(\pi_v(x),j_v(x-s_v(\pi_v(x)))\bigr).
  379: \]
  380: Since $x-s_v(\pi_v(x))\in K_v$, the formula is well defined. If $\iota_v(x)=\iota_v(y)$, then $\pi_v(x)=\pi_v(y)$ and
  381: \[
  382: j_v(x-s_v(\pi_v(x)))=j_v(y-s_v(\pi_v(y))).
  383: \]
  384: Injectivity of $j_v$ gives $x=y$. Thus each $\iota_v$ is injective, so the bound is attained.
  385: \end{proof}
  386: 
  387: \begin{corollary}[Single-branch reduction]\label{cor:single-branch-reduction}
  388: If
  389: \[
  390: \Vis_{p,s}(r)=\{v\},
  391: \]
  392: then
  393: \[
  394: A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{br}}(r)\cong A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\mathrm{unif}}(r)\cong A_{\mathrm{vis}}^v
  395: \]
  396: and the optimal branch-preserving strictification budget is exactly $|K_v|$.
  397: \end{corollary}
  398: 
  399: \begin{proof}
  400: If there is only one visible branch, then
  401: \[
  402: \bigcap_{u\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_u
  403: =
  404: \sum_{u\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_u
  405: =
  406: K_v.
  407: \]
  408: The quotient statements follow from \Cref{thm:branch-sensitive-visible-quotient,thm:branch-uniform-visible-quotient}, and the budget statement is \Cref{thm:multi-branch-strictification-budget}.
  409: \end{proof}
  410: 

 succeeded in 225ms:
  120: \[
  121: (\mathfrak E_r,\epsilon_r)
  122: \]
  123: consisting of such a stack together with an isomorphism of sheaves
  124: \[
  125: \epsilon_r:\pi_0(\mathfrak E_r)\xrightarrow{\sim} a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}
  126: \]
  127: on $\mathcal C_a$.
  128: 
  129: For a visible value class
  130: \[
  131: v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a),
  132: \]
  133: the \emph{$v$-component substack} of $\mathfrak E_r$ is the full substack
  134: \[
  135: \mathfrak E_r[v](U):=
  136: \bigl\{x\in \mathfrak E_r(U)\mid \epsilon_{r,U}([x])=v|_U\bigr\}
  137: \]
  138: for objects $U$ of $\mathcal C_a$, where $\epsilon_{r,U}$ denotes the component of $\epsilon_r$ at $U$.
  139: \end{definition}
  140: 
  141: \begin{lemma}[Equivalence data and component transport]\label{lem:extension-equivalence-component-transport}
  142: Let
  143: \[
  144: (\mathfrak E_r,\Band,\epsilon_r),
  145: \qquad
  146: (\mathfrak E'_r,\Band',\epsilon'_r)
  147: \]
  148: be banded realization-stack extensions on $\mathcal C_a$. Suppose an equivalence of stacks
  149: \[
  150: \Phi:\mathfrak E_r\to\mathfrak E'_r
  151: \]
  152: comes with a band isomorphism $\theta:\Band\xrightarrow{\sim}\Band'$ and satisfies
  153: \[
  154: \epsilon'_r\circ\pi_0(\Phi)=\epsilon_r.
  155: \]
  156: Then, for every visible value class $v$, $\Phi$ restricts to an equivalence of full component substacks
  157: \[
  158: \Phi_v:\mathfrak E_r[v]\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak E'_r[v],
  159: \]
  160: and this restricted equivalence is banded by $\theta$.
  161: \end{lemma}
  162: 
  163: \begin{proof}
  164: Let $U$ be an object of $\mathcal C_a$ and let $x\in\mathfrak E_r[v](U)$. By definition,
  165: \[
  166: \epsilon_{r,U}([x])=v|_U.
  167: \]
  168: The compatibility condition gives
  169: \[
  170: \epsilon'_{r,U}([\Phi(x)])
  171: =
  172: (\epsilon'_r\circ\pi_0(\Phi))_U([x])
  173: =
  174: \epsilon_{r,U}([x])
  175: =
  176: v|_U.
  177: \]
  178: Thus $\Phi(x)\in\mathfrak E'_r[v](U)$, so $\Phi$ restricts to a functor
  179: \[
  180: \Phi_v:\mathfrak E_r[v]\to\mathfrak E'_r[v].
  181: \]
  182: Since both component substacks are full, $\Phi_v$ is fully faithful because $\Phi$ is. For local essential surjectivity, take $z\in\mathfrak E'_r[v](U)$. A quasi-inverse $\Psi$ to $\Phi$ gives, after possibly refining $U$, an object $y\in\mathfrak E_r(U)$ with $\Phi(y)\cong z$. Applying $\epsilon'_r$ to this local isomorphism gives
  183: \[
  184: \epsilon_{r,U}([y])
  185: =
  186: \epsilon'_{r,U}([\Phi(y)])
  187: =
  188: \epsilon'_{r,U}([z])
  189: =
  190: v|_U,
  191: \]
  192: so $y$ lies locally in $\mathfrak E_r[v]$. Hence $\Phi_v$ is an equivalence of stacks. The automorphism-sheaf maps of $\Phi_v$ are the restrictions of the automorphism-sheaf maps of $\Phi$, so the band is transported by the same isomorphism $\theta$.
  193: \end{proof}
  194: 
  195: \begin{lemma}[Transport of Giraud classes under banded equivalence]\label{lem:giraud-class-transport}
  196: Let $\mathfrak G$ be a $\Band$-banded gerbe and let $\mathfrak G'$ be a $\Band'$-banded gerbe on the same site $\mathcal C$. Suppose
  197: \[
  198: \Phi:\mathfrak G\to\mathfrak G'
  199: \]
  200: is an equivalence of stacks whose induced maps on automorphism sheaves identify the bands through an isomorphism
  201: \[
  202: \theta:\Band\xrightarrow{\sim}\Band'.
  203: \]
  204: Then
  205: \[
  206: \theta_*[\mathfrak G]=[\mathfrak G']
  207: \qquad\text{in}\qquad
  208: H^2(\mathcal C,\Band').
  209: \]
  210: \end{lemma}
  211: 
  212: \begin{proof}
  213: Choose a cover $\{U_i\to U\}$ which locally trivializes $\mathfrak G$, objects $x_i\in\mathfrak G(U_i)$, and descent isomorphisms
  214: \[
  215: \varphi_{ij}:x_i|_{U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim}x_j|_{U_{ij}}.
  216: \]
  217: The usual Giraud cocycle $c_{ijk}\in\Band(U_{ijk})$ is defined by
  218: \[
  219: \varphi_{ik}^{-1}\varphi_{jk}\varphi_{ij}=c_{ijk}\in\operatorname{Aut}(x_i|_{U_{ijk}})\cong\Band(U_{ijk}).
  220: \]
  221: Applying $\Phi$ gives local objects $\Phi(x_i)$ of $\mathfrak G'$ and comparison isomorphisms $\Phi(\varphi_{ij})$. The corresponding defect is
  222: \[
  223: \Phi(\varphi_{ik})^{-1}\Phi(\varphi_{jk})\Phi(\varphi_{ij})
  224: =
  225: \Phi(c_{ijk}),
  226: \]
  227: which is identified with $\theta(c_{ijk})$ under the band isomorphism. Thus the transported local trivialization of $\mathfrak G'$ has cocycle $\theta_*(c)$. Refining the cover or changing the local objects changes both cocycles by the corresponding coboundary, exactly as in the standard local-trivialization classification of banded gerbes \cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}, \cite[Tag 06NY]{StacksProject}. Hence the cohomology class of $\mathfrak G'$ is $\theta_*[\mathfrak G]$.
  228: 
  229: The functoriality invoked here is the usual naturality of Giraud's classification with respect to a morphism of bands. Equivalently, the assignment
  230: \[
  231: (\mathfrak G,\Band)\longmapsto [\mathfrak G]\in H^2(\mathcal C,\Band)
  232: \]
  233: is functorial for banded equivalences: an equivalence of gerbes whose automorphism sheaves are identified by $\theta$ transports a local object--isomorphism--defect cocycle to its pointwise image under $\theta$, and this construction is independent of all choices after passage to cohomology.
  234: \end{proof}
  235: 
  236: \begin{theorem}[Canonical branch obstruction map under realization-stack extensions]\label{thm:intrinsic-branch-obstruction-extension}
  237: Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admitted at a state $p$, write
  238: \[
  239: a=[r]_p,
  240: \qquad
  241: \mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,
  242: \]
  243: and let
  244: \[
  245: (\mathfrak E_r,\Band,\epsilon_r)
  246: \]
  247: be a $\Band$-banded realization-stack extension in the sense of \Cref{def:realization-stack-extension}. Then the assignment
  248: \[
  249: \delta_{\mathfrak E_r}:\Vis_{p,s}(r)\longrightarrow H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band),
  250: \qquad
  251: v\longmapsto [\mathfrak E_r[v]]
  252: \]
  253: is canonically defined, where $[\mathfrak E_r[v]]$ denotes the Giraud class of the $v$-component substack. More precisely:
  254: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
  255: \item for every visible value class $v$, the full substack $\mathfrak E_r[v]$ is a $\Band$-banded gerbe on $\mathcal C_a$;
  256: \item for every visible value class $v$,
  257: \[
  258: \delta_{\mathfrak E_r}(v)=0
  259: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  260: \mathfrak E_r[v]\ \text{is neutral}
  261: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  262: \mathfrak E_r[v](a)\neq\varnothing;
  263: \]
  264: \item if
  265: \[
  266: (\mathfrak E'_r,\Band',\epsilon'_r)
  267: \]
  268: is another banded realization-stack extension and
  269: \[
  270: \Phi:\mathfrak E_r\to \mathfrak E'_r
  271: \]
  272: is an equivalence of stacks whose induced map on automorphism sheaves identifies the bands through an isomorphism
  273: \[
  274: \theta:\Band\xrightarrow{\sim}\Band'
  275: \]
  276: and whose map on connected components satisfies
  277: \[
  278: \epsilon'_r\circ \pi_0(\Phi)=\epsilon_r,
  279: \]
  280: then for every visible value class $v$ the functor $\Phi$ restricts to an equivalence
  281: \[
  282: \mathfrak E_r[v]\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak E'_r[v]
  283: \]
  284: and
  285: \[
  390: For \textup{(iv)}, let $\mathfrak E_r=\mathfrak L_r=a(\mathfrak P_r)$. By \Cref{prop:stackification-components}, the stackification carries a canonical identification
  391: \[
  392: \pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)\cong a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a},
  393: \]
  394: so $\mathfrak L_r$ is a banded realization-stack extension. The $v$-component substack defined above is then exactly the component gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ of \Cref{def:compatible-realization-stack}. On every cover
  395: \[
  396: \mathcal U\in \Cov_v^{\mathrm{acyc}}(a),
  397: \]
  398: \Cref{thm:cech-bridge-compatible-realizations} constructs the \v{C}ech class of this gerbe. The filtered colimit of those \v{C}ech classes is, by the standard classification of banded gerbes, the Giraud class of $\mathfrak G_v$, and hypothesis \textup{(H4)} identifies that colimit with the derived cohomology group
  399: \[
  400: H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band).
  401: \]
  402: By \Cref{def:cech-gluing-obstruction}, the image of that same filtered system is precisely $\omega_v$. Therefore
  403: \[
  404: \delta_{\mathfrak E_r}(v)=[\mathfrak G_v]=\omega_v.
  405: \]
  406: \end{proof}
  407: 
  408: \begin{theorem}[Supporting component gerbes of visible branches]\label{thm:component-gerbe-decomposition}
  409: In the pullback-based setting of \Cref{def:compatible-realization-stack}, and using only the realization-prestack and component data fixed there, every visible branch $v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)$ determines a full substack $\mathfrak G_v\subseteq \mathfrak L_r$ that is a banded $\Band$-gerbe over $\mathcal C_{p,s}/a$. In particular, neither global conservativity at $a$ nor the derived comparison hypothesis \textup{(H4)} is used here.
  410: \end{theorem}
  411: 
  412: \begin{proof}
  413: By \Cref{prop:stackification-components}, the stackification
  414: \[
  415: \mathfrak E_r:=\mathfrak L_r
  416: \]
  417: is a $\Band$-banded realization-stack extension of
  418: $a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$. The visible branch $v$ is a section of
  419: \[
  420: \pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a)\cong (a_{p,s}F_{p,s})(a),
  421: \]
  422: and the full substack
  423: \[
  424: \mathfrak G_v(U)=
  425: \{x\in\mathfrak L_r(U)\mid [x]\ \text{restricts to}\ v|_U\}
  426: \]
  427: is therefore exactly the $v$-component substack
  428: $\mathfrak E_r[v]$ of \Cref{def:realization-stack-extension}.
  429: 
  430: We recall why this is a gerbe. Because $\mathfrak L_r$ is a stack in
  431: groupoids, every descent datum for objects whose component lies over
  432: $v$ glues to an object whose component still lies over $v$; hence
  433: $\mathfrak G_v$ is a full substack. Local nonemptiness follows from the
  434: definition of a visible branch as a section of the sheaf of connected
  435: components: after passing to a cover of any object $U$, the section
  436: $v|_U$ is represented by objects of $\mathfrak L_r$. Local connectedness
  437: is the local form of equality in the component sheaf: if two objects of
  438: $\mathfrak G_v(U)$ have component $v|_U$, then they become isomorphic
  439: after a cover of $U$. Fullness is built into the definition of
  440: $\mathfrak G_v$, so no morphisms between objects in the component are
  441: discarded. Finally, for every object $x$ of $\mathfrak G_v(U)$, the
  442: automorphism sheaf of $x$ inside $\mathfrak G_v$ is the same as its
  443: automorphism sheaf inside $\mathfrak L_r$, and this sheaf is identified
  444: with $\Band|_{\mathcal C_a/U}$ by the banding of the realization
  445: prestack and its stackification. Thus $\mathfrak G_v$ is a
  446: $\Band$-banded gerbe.
  447: 
  448: This argument uses only the realization prestack, its stackification, and
  449: the component identification. Global conservativity at $a$ concerns
  450: whether terminal stack objects come from terminal prestack objects, and
  451: \textup{(H4)} concerns promotion of cover-level \v{C}ech classes to
  452: derived cohomology; neither condition enters the gerbe construction
  453: itself.
  454: \end{proof}
  455: 
  456: \begin{theorem}[\v{C}ech obstruction of a branch gerbe on an acyclic cover]\label{thm:cech-bridge-compatible-realizations}
  457: Assume that $\mathfrak G_v$ is a banded component gerbe with band $\Band$ in the sense of \Cref{def:compatible-realization-stack}. Using only this gerbe and a single acyclic cover of $a$, let
  458: \[
  459: \mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}\in \Cov_{p,s}(a)
  460: \]
  461: be any cover whose \v{C}ech nerve exists in $\mathcal C_{p,s}/a$, and write
  462: \[
  463: U_{i_0\cdots i_n}
  464: \]
  465: for the corresponding simplices. Assume that the cover is locally nonempty for the branch, so that objects
  466: \[
  467: x_i\in \mathfrak G_v(U_i)
  468: \]
  469: exist for all $i$, and assume
  470: \[
  471: H^1(U_{i_0\cdots i_n},\Band)=0
  472: \]
  473: for every nonempty intersection used below, including all members, pairwise overlaps, triple overlaps, and quadruple overlaps appearing in the descent calculation.
  474: Then:
  475: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
  476: \item for every pair $(i,j)$ there exists an isomorphism
  477: \[
  478: \theta_{ij}:x_i|_{U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim} x_j|_{U_{ij}};
  479: \]
  480: \item on each triple overlap $U_{ijk}$, the element
  481: \[
  482: c_{ijk}:=\theta_{ik}^{-1}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{jk}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{ij}|_{U_{ijk}}
  483: \in \Band(U_{ijk})
  484: \]
  485: defines a \v{C}ech $2$-cocycle $c=(c_{ijk})\in \check Z^2(\mathcal U,\Band)$;
  486: \item the class $[c]\in \check H^2(\mathcal U,\Band)$ depends only on the branch gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ and the chosen cover: changing the local objects or the comparison isomorphisms changes $c$ by a \v{C}ech coboundary, and refinements of acyclic covers pull this class back functorially;
  487: \item the class $[c]$ vanishes if and only if the branch gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ is neutral over $a$.
  488: \end{enumerate}
  489: \end{theorem}
  490: 
  491: \begin{proof}
  492: By local nonemptiness, fix objects $x_i\in \mathfrak G_v(U_i)$. For every pair $(i,j)$, the sheaf
  493: \[
  494: \mathrm{Isom}(x_i|_{U_{ij}},x_j|_{U_{ij}})
  495: \]
  496: is a $\Band|_{U_{ij}}$-torsor. The vanishing
  497: \[
  498: H^1(U_{ij},\Band)=0
  499: \]
  500: therefore trivializes this torsor \cite[Chap.~III]{MacLaneMoerdijk1994}, \cite[Sec.~11]{StacksProject}, so we may choose a section
  501: \[
  502: \theta_{ij}:x_i|_{U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim} x_j|_{U_{ij}}.
  503: \]
  504: This proves \textup{(i)}.
  505: 
  506: On a triple overlap $U_{ijk}$, the composite
  507: \[
  508: c_{ijk}:=\theta_{ik}^{-1}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{jk}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{ij}|_{U_{ijk}}
  509: \]
  510: is an automorphism of $x_i|_{U_{ijk}}$ and hence an element of $\Band(U_{ijk})$. On a quadruple overlap $U_{ijkl}$ one computes
  511: \[
  512: c_{jkl}\,c_{ijl}
  513: =
  514: \theta_{jl}^{-1}\theta_{kl}\theta_{jk}\,\theta_{il}^{-1}\theta_{jl}\theta_{ij}
  515: =
  516: \theta_{il}^{-1}\theta_{kl}\theta_{ik}^{-1}\theta_{ik}\theta_{jk}\theta_{ij}
  517: =
  518: c_{ikl}\,c_{ijk},
  519: \]
  520: where all restrictions to $U_{ijkl}$ are suppressed from the notation. This is exactly the \v{C}ech cocycle identity $\delta c=0$, so $c=(c_{ijk})$ lies in $\check Z^2(\mathcal U,\Band)$. This proves \textup{(ii)}.
  521: 
  522: For \textup{(iii)}, first keep the objects $x_i$ fixed and replace $\theta_{ij}$ by another choice $\theta'_{ij}$. Since $\mathrm{Isom}(x_i|_{U_{ij}},x_j|_{U_{ij}})$ is a $\Band|_{U_{ij}}$-torsor, there exist unique sections $h_{ij}\in \Band(U_{ij})$ with
  523: \[
  524: \theta'_{ij}=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}.
  525: \]
  526: A direct calculation on triple overlaps gives
  527: \[
  528: c'_{ijk}=h_{ik}\,c_{ijk}\,h_{jk}^{-1}h_{ij}^{-1},
  529: \]
  530: which is the multiplicative form of the statement that $c'$ and $c$ differ by the coboundary $\delta h$.
  531: 
  532: Next replace the objects $x_i$ by another family $x_i'\in \mathfrak G_v(U_i)$. Since $x_i$ and $x_i'$ lie in the same gerbe over $U_i$, the sheaf $\mathrm{Isom}(x_i,x_i')$ is a $\Band|_{U_i}$-torsor. The vanishing
  533: \[
  534: H^1(U_i,\Band)=0
  535: \]
  536: trivializes it, so choose isomorphisms
  537: \[
  538: \psi_i:x_i\xrightarrow{\sim} x_i'.
  539: \]
  540: Define
  541: \[
  542: \theta_{ij}':=\psi_j|_{U_{ij}}\circ \theta_{ij}\circ \psi_i^{-1}|_{U_{ij}}.
  543: \]
  544: Then the associated cocycle satisfies
  545: \[
  546: c'_{ijk}
  547: =
  548: \psi_i|_{U_{ijk}}\,c_{ijk}\,\psi_i^{-1}|_{U_{ijk}}
  549: =
  550: c_{ijk},
  551: \]
  552: under the band identification $\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}(x_i)\cong \Band|_{U_i}$. Thus changing local objects changes the cocycle only by the preliminary freedom in the choice of the $\psi_i$, hence only by a coboundary. If
  553: \[
  554: \lambda:\mathcal V\to\mathcal U
  555: \]
  556: is a refinement by another cover $\mathcal V\in\Cov_{p,s}(a)$ whose \v{C}ech nerve exists and whose relevant intersections satisfy the same $H^1$-vanishing hypothesis, then the same construction on $\mathcal V$ uses the pulled-back objects and isomorphisms, so the resulting cocycle is exactly $\lambda^*(c)$. Therefore the induced cohomology class is refinement compatible.
  557: 
  558: For \textup{(iv)}, suppose first that $\mathfrak G_v$ is neutral and choose a global object $x\in \mathfrak G_v(a)$. For each $i$, the sheaf $\mathrm{Isom}(x|_{U_i},x_i)$ is a $\Band|_{U_i}$-torsor and therefore trivial because $H^1(U_i,\Band)=0$. Choose
  559: \[
  560: \psi_i:x|_{U_i}\xrightarrow{\sim} x_i.
  561: \]
  562: If we set
  563: \[
  564: \theta_{ij}:=\psi_j|_{U_{ij}}\circ \psi_i^{-1}|_{U_{ij}},
  565: \]
  566: then the resulting cocycle is identically zero, so $[c]=0$.
  567: 
  568: Conversely, assume $[c]=0$. Then there is a $1$-cochain $h=(h_{ij})$ with
  569: \[
  570: c=\delta h.
  571: \]
  572: Replace $\theta_{ij}$ by
  573: \[
  574: \theta_{ij}':=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}.
  575: \]
  576: The defining equation $c=\delta h$ is exactly the statement that the $\theta_{ij}'$ satisfy the strict cocycle condition on triple overlaps. Hence $(x_i,\theta_{ij}')$ is a descent datum for the stack $\mathfrak G_v$, and since $\mathfrak G_v$ is a stack in groupoids, that descent datum is effective. The resulting glued object of $\mathfrak G_v(a)$ is a global object, so the gerbe is neutral.
  577: \end{proof}
  578: 
  579: \begin{lemma}[\v{C}ech calculus for branch gerbes]\label{lem:cech-branch-gerbe-calculus}
  580: In the setting of \Cref{thm:cech-bridge-compatible-realizations}, the following four operations have the indicated effects on the cocycle
  581: \[
  582: c_{ijk}:=\theta_{ik}^{-1}\theta_{jk}\theta_{ij}.
  583: \]
  584: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
  585: \item If the local objects $x_i$ are fixed and $\theta'_{ij}=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}$ for a $1$-cochain $h=(h_{ij})$ with values in $\Band$, then the new cocycle is
  586: \[
  587: c'_{ijk}=h_{ik}\,c_{ijk}\,h_{jk}^{-1}h_{ij}^{-1};
  588: \]
  589: in additive notation, $c'-c=\delta h$.
  590: \item If local objects $x_i$ are replaced by $x_i'$ and $\psi_i:x_i\xrightarrow{\sim}x_i'$ are chosen, then
  591: \[
  592: \theta'_{ij}:=\psi_j\theta_{ij}\psi_i^{-1}
  593: \]
  594: produces the same cocycle under the band identifications. Changing the $\psi_i$ modifies the result only by a coboundary of the kind in \textup{(i)}.
  595: \item If $\lambda:\mathcal V\to\mathcal U$ is a refinement satisfying the same acyclicity hypotheses, the cocycle constructed from the pulled-back objects and isomorphisms on $\mathcal V$ is $\lambda^*c$.
  596: \item The class $[c]$ is zero if and only if the descent datum may be adjusted so that the comparison isomorphisms satisfy the strict cocycle condition; by effective descent for the stack $\mathfrak G_v$, this is equivalent to a global object of $\mathfrak G_v(a)$.
  597: \end{enumerate}
  598: \end{lemma}
  599: 
  600: \begin{proof}
  601: For \textup{(i)}, the Isom sheaf on $U_{ij}$ is a $\Band$-torsor, so the ratio between $\theta_{ij}$ and $\theta'_{ij}$ is the unique element $h_{ij}^{-1}\in\Band(U_{ij})$ with
  602: \[
  603: \theta'_{ij}=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}.
  604: \]
  605: Substituting these three identities in
  606: \[
  607: c'_{ijk}=(\theta'_{ik})^{-1}\theta'_{jk}\theta'_{ij}
  608: \]
  609: and using that the band is abelian gives
  610: \[
  611: c'_{ijk}=h_{ik}\,c_{ijk}\,h_{jk}^{-1}h_{ij}^{-1}.
  612: \]
  613: This is precisely the multiplicative Cech coboundary formula.
  614: 
  615: For \textup{(ii)}, substituting
  616: \[
  617: \theta'_{ij}=\psi_j\theta_{ij}\psi_i^{-1}
  618: \]
  619: into the triple product gives
  620: \[
  621: (\theta'_{ik})^{-1}\theta'_{jk}\theta'_{ij}
  622: =
  623: \psi_i\theta_{ik}^{-1}\theta_{jk}\theta_{ij}\psi_i^{-1}.
  624: \]
  625: Under the band identification
  626: \[
  627: \underline{\mathrm{Aut}}(x_i)\cong\Band|_{U_i}\cong
  628: \underline{\mathrm{Aut}}(x_i'),
  629: \]
  630: conjugation by $\psi_i$ is the identity on $\Band$. If the isomorphisms $\psi_i$ are changed, the new choices differ from the old ones by a $0$-cochain in the automorphism sheaves, and the resulting comparison isomorphisms differ from the displayed $\theta'_{ij}$ by the coboundary described in \textup{(i)}.
  631: 
  632: For \textup{(iii)}, a refinement map $\lambda$ sends each member of $\mathcal V$ to one member of $\mathcal U$. Pulling back the objects $x_i$ and maps $\theta_{ij}$ along these assignments gives on each triple overlap of $\mathcal V$ exactly the restriction of $c_{\lambda(i)\lambda(j)\lambda(k)}$. This is the usual pullback cocycle $\lambda^*c$.
  633: 
  634: For \textup{(iv)}, if $[c]=0$, choose $h$ with $c=\delta h$ and replace $\theta_{ij}$ by $\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}$. By \textup{(i)} the new cocycle is zero, which says exactly that the modified $\theta_{ij}$ satisfy the strict descent condition on triple overlaps. Since $\mathfrak G_v$ is a stack, this descent datum is effective and glues to an object of $\mathfrak G_v(a)$. Conversely, a global object $x\in\mathfrak G_v(a)$ and local isomorphisms $x|_{U_i}\cong x_i$ produce comparison maps whose triple defect is zero; \textup{(ii)} and \textup{(i)} then show that the original cocycle class is zero.
  635: \end{proof}
  636: 
  637: \begin{proposition}[Derived branch classes detect neutrality]\label{prop:derived-branch-classification}
  638: Assume \textup{(H1)}--\textup{(H4)}. For every visible branch $v$,
  639: \[
  640: \omega_v=0\ \text{in}\ H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band)
  641: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  642: \mathfrak G_v\ \text{is neutral}.
  643: \]
  644: \end{proposition}
  645: 
  646: \begin{proof}
  647: By \Cref{def:cech-gluing-obstruction}, the derived class $\omega_v$ is the image of the refinement-compatible \v{C}ech classes supplied by \Cref{thm:cech-bridge-compatible-realizations}. The comparison isomorphism in \textup{(H4)} identifies this class with the standard Giraud class of the banded gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$. The vanishing of that derived class is equivalent to neutrality by the classification of banded gerbes by $H^2$ \cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}, \cite[Tag 06NY]{StacksProject}.
  648: \end{proof}
  649: 
  650: \begin{proposition}[Compatible local sections versus visible branches on separated slices]\label{prop:compsecs-vs-visible-branches}
  651: Assume that $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ is admitted at $p$, admits a realization prestack in the sense of \Cref{def:realization-prestack}, and that $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is slice-separated. Then visible branches detect strict compatible local realizability, without any global-conservativity or derived-comparison hypothesis:
  652: \[
  653: \Vis_{p,s}(r)\neq\varnothing
  654: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  655: M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r).
  656: \]
  657: \end{proposition}
  658: 
  659: \begin{proof}
  660: By \Cref{thm:visible-value-components},
  661: \[
  662: \Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a).
  663: \]
  664: The implication from strict compatible local sectionability to
  665: nonemptiness of this sheafification is
  666: \Cref{prop:compsec-to-sheafification}. Conversely, a visible branch gives
  667: an element of \((a_{p,s}F_{p,s})(a)\) by the same displayed
  668: identification. The slice-separated hypothesis is exactly the additional
  669: condition needed to strictify that sheafified section: applying
  670: \Cref{thm:sheafification-characterization} to
  671: \(F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\) produces a cover of \(a\) and a strict
  672: matching family of \(F_{p,s}\)-sections on that cover. By
  673: \Cref{def:existence-levels}(ii), this is
  674: \[
  675: M,p\Vdash\CompSec_s(r).
  676: \]
  677: \end{proof}
  678: 
  679: \begin{lemma}[Terminal-fibre nonemptiness and neutrality]\label{lem:terminal-fibre-neutrality}
  680: Let $v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)$. Then
  681: \[
  682: \mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing
  683: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  684: \mathfrak G_v\ \text{is neutral}.
  685: \]
  686: \leanverified{paper_gluing_failure_terminal_fibre_neutrality}
  687: \end{lemma}
  688: 
  689: \begin{proof}
  690: By definition of the gerbe notion used in this paper, neutrality means the existence of a global object over $a$. Therefore it is exactly the same as $\mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing$.
  691: \end{proof}
  692: 
  693: \begin{proposition}[Global sections versus neutral component gerbes]\label{prop:global-sections-vs-neutral-components}
  694: \leanverified{paper_gluing_failure_global_sections_vs_neutral_components}
  695: Assume that $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ is admitted at $p$, that it admits a realization prestack in the sense of \Cref{def:realization-prestack}, and that this prestack is globally conservative at $a$ in the sense of \Cref{def:global-conservativity}. Then a global realization exists exactly when some visible branch has a global object:
  696: \[
  697: M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)
  698: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  699: \exists v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)\ \text{such that}\ \mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing.
  700: \]
  701: Equivalently, by \Cref{lem:terminal-fibre-neutrality}, a global realization exists exactly when at least one visible branch gerbe is neutral.
  702: \end{proposition}
  703: 
  704: \begin{proof}
  705: From \Cref{prop:no-new-global-objects}, $M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)$ iff $\mathfrak L_r(a)\neq\varnothing$.
  706: 
  707: If $\mathfrak L_r(a)\neq\varnothing$, choose $x\in \mathfrak L_r(a)$ and let
  708: \[
  709: v=[x]\in \pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a)\cong \Vis_{p,s}(r).
  710: \]
  711: Then $x\in \mathfrak G_v(a)$, so $\mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing$.
  712: 
  713: Conversely, if $\mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing$ for some visible branch $v$, then $\mathfrak G_v(a)\subseteq \mathfrak L_r(a)$, so $\mathfrak L_r(a)\neq\varnothing$.
  714: \end{proof}
  715: 
  716: \begin{theorem}[Conditional branched gerbe semantics of gluing-level absence]\label{thm:gerbe-null-semantics}
  717: Assume \textup{(H1)}--\textup{(H3)} for an admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at $p$, and assume in addition that \(F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\) is slice-separated. Then the gluing-null condition is exactly the existence of at least one visible branch and the non-neutrality of every component gerbe:
  718: \[
  719: M,p\Vdash \Null^{\mathrm{glue}}_s(r)
  720: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  721: \Vis_{p,s}(r)\neq\varnothing
  722: \ \text{and}\
  723: \mathfrak G_v\ \text{is non-neutral for every }v\in\Vis_{p,s}(r).
  724: \]
  725: If, in addition, \textup{(H4)} holds, then this is equivalently
  726: \[
  727: M,p\Vdash \Null^{\mathrm{glue}}_s(r)
  728: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  729: \Vis_{p,s}(r)\neq\varnothing
  730: \ \text{and}\
  731: \omega_v\neq 0\ \text{in }H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band)
  732: \text{ for every }v\in\Vis_{p,s}(r).
  733: \]
  734: The companion theorem \cite[Theorem~5.20]{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure} is the meet-semilattice analogue of this pullback-site formulation.
  735: \end{theorem}
  736: 
  737: \begin{proof}
  738: Because $r$ is admitted, the definition of gluing-level nullity gives
  739: \[
  740: M,p\Vdash \Null^{\mathrm{glue}}_s(r)
  741: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  742: M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)\ \text{and}\ M,p\nVdash \Sec_s(r).
  743: \]
  744: By \Cref{prop:compsecs-vs-visible-branches}, the compatible-local clause is equivalent to
  745: \[
  746: \Vis_{p,s}(r)\neq\varnothing.
  747: \]
  748: By \Cref{prop:global-sections-vs-neutral-components}, using global conservativity at $a$ from \textup{(H3)}, the global-section clause is equivalent to the existence of a visible branch $v$ with
  749: \[
  750: \mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing.
  751: \]
  752: By \Cref{lem:terminal-fibre-neutrality}, this is equivalent to the existence of a neutral component gerbe. Therefore
  753: \[
  754: M,p\nVdash \Sec_s(r)
  755: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad

 succeeded in 217ms:
  730: would have to be stated with coherent cartesian isomorphisms, and the proof
  731: of descent below would require the corresponding triple-cocycle coherence
  732: instead of literal equalities of pulled-back representatives.
  733: \end{definition}
  734: 
  735: \begin{lemma}[Where slice-separatedness enters]\label{lem:unit-equality-on-cover-members}
  736: Let $\mathcal C$ be a site with pullbacks of covering families, let \(F\)
  737: be a presheaf of sets, let \(u:F\to aF\) be the sheafification unit, and
  738: let \(X\) be an object. Assume that \(F\) is separated at every object of
  739: the slice \(\mathcal C/X\). If
  740: \[
  741: \{X_i\to X\}_{i\in I}
  742: \]
  743: is a covering family, \(\sigma\in F(X)\), and
  744: \(\sigma_i\in F(X_i)\) are sections satisfying
  745: \[
  746: u_{X_i}\bigl(\res_{X_i}^{X}\sigma\bigr)=u_{X_i}(\sigma_i)
  747: \qquad(\forall i),
  748: \]
  749: then
  750: \[
  751: \res_{X_i}^{X}\sigma=\sigma_i
  752: \qquad(\forall i).
  753: \]
  754: \end{lemma}
  755: 
  756: \begin{proof}
  757: Fix \(i\). Equality in \((aF)(X_i)\) is only sheafified equality, so it
  758: does not by itself give equality in \(F(X_i)\). By
  759: \Cref{lem:sheafification-unit-equality-local}, there is a covering family
  760: \(\{Y_{ik}\to X_i\}_k\) such that
  761: \[
  762: \res_{Y_{ik}}^{X}\sigma=\res_{Y_{ik}}^{X_i}\sigma_i
  763: \qquad(\forall k)
  764: \]
  765: in \(F(Y_{ik})\). The object \(X_i\) lies in the slice \(\mathcal C/X\),
  766: and the hypothesis says that \(F\) is separated at this object. Therefore
  767: these equalities after a cover of \(X_i\) force equality over \(X_i\)
  768: itself:
  769: \[
  770: \res_{X_i}^{X}\sigma=\sigma_i.
  771: \]
  772: This proves the assertion for every \(i\).
  773: \end{proof}
  774: 
  775: \begin{lemma}[Bijective terminal unit and the terminal sheaf axiom]\label{lem:bijective-unit-terminal-sheaf-axiom}
  776: Let $\mathcal C$ be a site with pullbacks of covering families, let $F$
  777: be a presheaf of sets, let $X$ be an object, and let
  778: \[
  779: u:F\longrightarrow aF
  780: \]
  781: be the sheafification unit. Assume that \(F\) is separated at every object
  782: of the slice \(\mathcal C/X\). Then the following are equivalent:
  783: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
  784: \item the map \(u_X:F(X)\to(aF)(X)\) is bijective;
  785: \item \(F\) satisfies the sheaf axiom at \(X\): every strict matching
  786: family on every cover of \(X\) has a unique amalgamation in \(F(X)\).
  787: \end{enumerate}
  788: Here separatedness on the whole slice is essential.  In the direction from
  789: bijectivity of \(u_X\) to the sheaf axiom, the chosen preimage of the
  790: glued sheaf section has restrictions which agree with the prescribed
  791: local sections only after applying \(u\) over each cover member.  Equality
  792: in \((aF)(X_i)\) gives equality in \(F\) only after a further cover of
  793: \(X_i\), and separatedness at \(X_i\), not merely at \(X\), is what
  794: descends that refined equality back to \(X_i\).
  795: \end{lemma}
  796: 
  797: \begin{proof}
  798: Slice-separatedness at \(X\) implies injectivity of \(u_X\). Indeed, if
  799: \(u_X(\sigma)=u_X(\tau)\), then
  800: \Cref{lem:sheafification-unit-equality-local} gives a covering family
  801: \(\{X_i\to X\}_i\) on which the restrictions of \(\sigma\) and \(\tau\)
  802: are equal in \(F(X_i)\), and separatedness at \(X\) forces
  803: \(\sigma=\tau\).
  804: 
  805: Assume first that \(u_X\) is bijective. Let
  806: \[
  807: \bigl(\{X_i\to X\}_{i\in I},(\sigma_i)_{i\in I}\bigr)
  808: \]
  809: be a strict \(F\)-matching family. Applying \(u\) to the sections
  810: \(\sigma_i\) gives a matching family in the sheaf \(aF\), hence a unique
  811: section \(\xi\in(aF)(X)\). By surjectivity choose
  812: \(\sigma\in F(X)\) with \(u_X(\sigma)=\xi\). For each \(i\),
  813: \[
  814: u_{X_i}\bigl(\res_{X_i}^{X}\sigma\bigr)=u_{X_i}(\sigma_i)
  815: \]
  816: in \((aF)(X_i)\). This equality is not an equality in \(F(X_i)\). By
  817: \Cref{lem:unit-equality-on-cover-members}, applied to the cover
  818: \(\{X_i\to X\}_{i\in I}\), these sheafified equalities upgrade to
  819: \[
  820: \res_{X_i}^{X}\sigma=\sigma_i
  821: \qquad(\forall i).
  822: \]
  823: Thus \(\sigma\) is an amalgamation of the original matching family.
  824: Uniqueness of the amalgamation follows again from separatedness at \(X\).
  825: 
  826: Conversely, assume the sheaf axiom at \(X\). Let
  827: \(\xi\in(aF)(X)\). By the plus--plus normal form
  828: \Cref{lem:strict-sheafification-representatives}, \(\xi\) is represented
  829: by a cover \(\{X_i\to X\}_i\) and sections
  830: \(\sigma_i\in F(X_i)\) whose restrictions to each pullback overlap agree
  831: after a further covering of that overlap. Because every such overlap is an
  832: object of \(\mathcal C/X\), \Cref{lem:slice-separated-refined-equality}
  833: upgrades the refined equalities to a strict \(F\)-matching family. The
  834: sheaf axiom at \(X\) gives an amalgamation \(\sigma\in F(X)\). The
  835: sections \(u_X(\sigma)\) and \(\xi\) restrict to the same sections of
  836: \(aF\) on the cover \(\{X_i\to X\}_i\), so separatedness of the sheaf
  837: \(aF\) gives \(u_X(\sigma)=\xi\). Hence \(u_X\) is surjective, and it is
  838: injective by the first paragraph.
  839: \end{proof}
  840: 
  841: \begin{lemma}[Isomorphism torsor for a lifted component]\label{lem:canonical-lift-isom-torsor}
  842: Let $\mathfrak P_r$ be a representative-rigid realization prestack with band $\Band$ on $\mathcal C_a$, let
  843: \[
  844: \iota_r:\mathfrak P_r\to\mathfrak L_r:=a(\mathfrak P_r)
  845: \]
  846: be the stackification map, and let $x\in\mathfrak L_r(a)$ and $\sigma\in F_{p,s}(a)$ satisfy
  847: \[
  848: [\iota_r(s_a(\sigma))]=[x]
  849: \quad\text{in}\quad
  850: \pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a).
  851: \]
  852: Then the sheaf
  853: \[
  854: \mathcal I_{\sigma,x}:=
  855: \underline{\mathrm{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L_r}(\iota_r(s_a(\sigma)),x)
  856: \]
  857: is a $\Band$-torsor on $\mathcal C_a$. If $H^1(\mathcal C_a,\Band)=0$, then $\mathcal I_{\sigma,x}$ has a global section, equivalently
  858: \[
  859: \iota_r(s_a(\sigma))\cong x
  860: \quad\text{in}\quad
  861: \mathfrak L_r(a).
  862: \]
  863: \end{lemma}
  864: 
  865: \begin{proof}
  866: Write
  867: \[
  868: y:=s_a(\sigma).
  869: \]
  870: The equality of component classes in the sheaf $\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)$ means precisely that the two objects $\iota_r(y)$ and $x$ become isomorphic after passing to a cover of $a$. Hence there is a covering family
  871: \[
  872: \{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
  873: \]
  874: and isomorphisms
  875: \[
  876: \phi_i:\iota_r(y)|_{U_i}\xrightarrow{\sim}x|_{U_i}.
  877: \]
  878: Thus $\mathcal I_{\sigma,x}$ is locally nonempty.
  879: 
  880: For every object $U$ of $\mathcal C_a$ and every local isomorphism
  881: \[
  882: \phi:\iota_r(y)|_U\xrightarrow{\sim}x|_U,
  883: \]
  884: right composition gives a simply transitive action of
  885: \[
  886: \underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathfrak L_r}(\iota_r(y)|_U)
  887: \]
  888: on $\mathcal I_{\sigma,x}(U)$:
  889: \[
  890: \alpha\longmapsto \phi\circ\alpha.
  891: \]
  892: Because $\mathfrak L_r$ is the stackification of a $\Band$-banded prestack, the automorphism sheaf of $\iota_r(y)|_U$ is identified with $\Band|_U$; the band is abelian, so the same torsor is obtained from left composition through the automorphism sheaf of $x|_U$.
