    1: \section{Component gerbes and branch obstruction classes}\label{sec:gerbe-obstruction}
    2: 
    3: From this point on the argument proceeds branchwise relative to a chosen
    4: realization prestack. When derived obstruction classes are needed, the
    5: \v{C}ech--derived comparison package below is also imposed. No unconditional
    6: existence theorem for such data is sought here. This section reorganizes the
    7: gerbe-theoretic obstruction package of the companion paper
    8: \cite{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure} in the intrinsic
    9: realization-stack language used later. The first result of the section
   10: isolates the canonicity that is available once the sheafified local object
   11: functor has been lifted to a banded stack: the branch obstruction class is
   12: intrinsic to the resulting realization-stack extension, up to equivalence of
   13: extensions. Since a single reference may carry several visible value classes,
   14: the relevant obstruction objects are the corresponding component gerbes
   15: rather than a single global gerbe. The cocycle constructions used below are
   16: the standard local-trivialization constructions for gerbes
   17: \cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}, \cite[Sec.~5]{Breen2006}, together with their
   18: bundle-gerbe counterparts \cite{Murray1996,Brylinski1993}, applied
   19: branchwise in the present setting.
   20: 
   21: We begin by fixing the hypotheses used in the section. Individual statements invoke only the subcollection they require.
   22: 
   23: \begin{definition}[\v{C}ech--derived comparison package]\label{def:cech-derived-comparison-package}
   24: For an admitted reference \(r:\mathsf{Ref}_s\) at \(p\), a band \(\Band\)
   25: on \(\mathcal C_{p,s}/a\), and a visible branch \(v\), a
   26: \emph{\v{C}ech--derived comparison package} consists of a filtered cofinal
   27: family
   28: \[
   29: \Cov_v^{\mathrm{acyc}}(a)\subseteq \Cov_{p,s}(a)
   30: \]
   31: such that every cover \(\mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}\) in the family has a
   32: \v{C}ech nerve in the slice site, trivializes the band on all intersections
   33: used in degrees \(0,1,2\), and satisfies the following comparison condition:
   34: the canonical refinement map
   35: \[
   36: \varinjlim_{\mathcal U\in\Cov_v^{\mathrm{acyc}}(a)}
   37: \check H^2(\mathcal U,\Band)
   38: \longrightarrow
   39: H^2(\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,\Band)
   40: \]
   41: is an isomorphism. One way to verify this condition is the usual Leray
   42: hypothesis: all nonempty finite intersections appearing in the family are
   43: \(\Band\)-acyclic in every positive degree needed for the derived
   44: comparison. The definition itself, however, records the comparison
   45: isomorphism as the required assumption; the later theorems do not silently
   46: infer derived \(H^2\) from a single \v{C}ech cocycle unless this package, or
   47: the explicit finite good-cover Leray theorem of
   48: \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}, is in force.
   49: \end{definition}
   50: 
   51: \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(H\arabic*)},nosep]
   52: \item\label{hyp:admitted-prestack} An admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$ with $a=[r]_p$, equipped with a realization prestack $\mathfrak P_r \to \mathcal C_a$ and band $\Band$ in the sense of \Cref{def:realization-prestack}.
   53: \item\label{hyp:band-identification} The band $\Band$ is identified with a fixed abelian sheaf on the slice site $\mathcal C_{p,s}/a$.
   54: \item\label{hyp:global-conservativity} The realization prestack $\mathfrak P_r$ is globally conservative at $a$ in the sense of \Cref{def:global-conservativity}.
   55: \item\label{hyp:cofinal-gerbe-adapted} For every visible branch \(v\), a
   56: \v{C}ech--derived comparison package in the sense of
   57: \Cref{def:cech-derived-comparison-package} has been fixed.
   58: \end{enumerate}
   59: 
   60: When a statement compares visible branches with the strict predicate
   61: \(\CompSec_s\), or with \(\Null^{\mathrm{glue}}_s\) through that predicate,
   62: it separately assumes that \(F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\) is slice-separated.
   63: Without this separatedness assumption visible branches are only
   64: sheafified sections, and no strict compatible family is asserted.
   65: 
   66: \begin{remark}[\v{C}ech versus derived cohomology]\label{rem:cech-vs-derived}
   67: Hypothesis \ref{hyp:cofinal-gerbe-adapted}, equivalently the package of
   68: \Cref{def:cech-derived-comparison-package}, is the precise comparison
   69: assumption governing the passage between \v{C}ech and derived $H^2$. It
   70: requires the comparison isomorphism itself; acyclicity in positive degrees is
   71: one standard sufficient Leray condition, not a hidden extra assumption unless
   72: explicitly invoked. Without the package, the branch obstruction class lives in
   73: the filtered colimit
   74: \[
   75: \varinjlim_{\mathcal U\in \Cov_v^{\mathrm{acyc}}(a)} \check H^2(\mathcal U,\Band),
   76: \]
   77: and the non-neutrality statement in \Cref{thm:cech-bridge-compatible-realizations} is purely \v{C}ech-theoretic. With \ref{hyp:cofinal-gerbe-adapted}, this colimit is canonically identified with the derived $H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band)$, and the obstruction class $\omega_v$ is an element of derived cohomology. All theorems in this subsection state explicitly whether they use only the \v{C}ech-level data or additionally require the comparison isomorphism.
   78: \end{remark}
   79: 
   80: \begin{remark}[Relation to the companion paper]
   81: The companion paper \cite{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure} formulates branch obstructions relative to a chosen gluing-sensitive realization prestack and its stackification. The present section isolates the three extensions needed later: the promotion of cover-relative \v{C}ech classes to derived classes $\omega_v$ under \textup{(H4)}, the reformulation of the obstruction theory for arbitrary banded realization-stack extensions of the sheafified local-object functor, and the branchwise neutrality criteria stated directly in terms of component gerbes.
   82: \end{remark}
   83: 
   84: \begin{definition}[Component gerbes and branch obstruction classes]\label{def:compatible-realization-stack}\label{def:cech-gluing-obstruction}
   85: Fix an admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$, write
   86: \[
   87: a=[r]_p,
   88: \]
   89: and assume that $r$ admits a realization prestack $\mathfrak P_r$ with stackification $\mathfrak L_r$ and band $\Band$ as in \Cref{def:realization-prestack}. For a visible value class
   90: \[
   91: v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a),
   92: \]
   93: let $\mathfrak G_v(U)$ be the full subgroupoid of $\mathfrak L_r(U)$ on those objects whose connected-component class restricts to $v$ on $U$. When \Cref{thm:component-gerbe-decomposition} applies, we call $\mathfrak G_v$ the \emph{component gerbe} of the visible branch $v$.
   94: 
   95: Assume moreover that \textup{(H4)} holds. For each visible branch $v$ and each cover
   96: \[
   97: \mathcal U\in \Cov_v^{\mathrm{acyc}}(a),
   98: \]
   99: \Cref{thm:cech-bridge-compatible-realizations} produces a \v{C}ech obstruction class
  100: \[
  101: [g_{\mathcal U}]\in \check H^2(\mathcal U,\Band).
  102: \]
  103: Refinement compatibility identifies these classes in the filtered system, and the comparison isomorphism in \textup{(H4)} therefore determines a unique derived class
  104: \[
  105: \omega_v\in H^2(\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,\Band),
  106: \]
  107: called the \emph{branch obstruction class} of $v$.
  108: \end{definition}
  109: 
  110: \begin{definition}[Banded realization-stack extension]\label{def:realization-stack-extension}
  111: For an admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$, abbreviate
  112: \[
  113: a=[r]_p,
  114: \qquad
  115: \mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,
  116: \]
  117: and fix an abelian sheaf $\Band$ on $\mathcal C_a$. Let $\mathfrak E_r$ be a
  118: $\Band$-banded stack in groupoids on $\mathcal C_a$. A
  119: \emph{$\Band$-banded realization-stack extension} is a pair
  120: \[
  121: (\mathfrak E_r,\epsilon_r)
  122: \]
  123: consisting of such a stack together with an isomorphism of sheaves
  124: \[
  125: \epsilon_r:\pi_0(\mathfrak E_r)\xrightarrow{\sim} a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}
  126: \]
  127: on $\mathcal C_a$.
  128: 
  129: For a visible value class
  130: \[
  131: v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a),
  132: \]
  133: the \emph{$v$-component substack} of $\mathfrak E_r$ is the full substack
  134: \[
  135: \mathfrak E_r[v](U):=
  136: \bigl\{x\in \mathfrak E_r(U)\mid \epsilon_{r,U}([x])=v|_U\bigr\}
  137: \]
  138: for objects $U$ of $\mathcal C_a$, where $\epsilon_{r,U}$ denotes the component of $\epsilon_r$ at $U$.
  139: \end{definition}
  140: 
  440: $\mathfrak G_v$, so no morphisms between objects in the component are
  441: discarded. Finally, for every object $x$ of $\mathfrak G_v(U)$, the
  442: automorphism sheaf of $x$ inside $\mathfrak G_v$ is the same as its
  443: automorphism sheaf inside $\mathfrak L_r$, and this sheaf is identified
  444: with $\Band|_{\mathcal C_a/U}$ by the banding of the realization
  445: prestack and its stackification. Thus $\mathfrak G_v$ is a
  446: $\Band$-banded gerbe.
  447: 
  448: This argument uses only the realization prestack, its stackification, and
  449: the component identification. Global conservativity at $a$ concerns
  450: whether terminal stack objects come from terminal prestack objects, and
  451: \textup{(H4)} concerns promotion of cover-level \v{C}ech classes to
  452: derived cohomology; neither condition enters the gerbe construction
  453: itself.
  454: \end{proof}

  456: \begin{theorem}[\v{C}ech obstruction of a branch gerbe on an acyclic cover]\label{thm:cech-bridge-compatible-realizations}
  457: Assume that $\mathfrak G_v$ is a banded component gerbe with band $\Band$ in the sense of \Cref{def:compatible-realization-stack}. Using only this gerbe and a single acyclic cover of $a$, let
  458: \[
  459: \mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}\in \Cov_{p,s}(a)
  460: \]
  461: be any cover whose \v{C}ech nerve exists in $\mathcal C_{p,s}/a$, and write
  462: \[
  463: U_{i_0\cdots i_n}
  464: \]
  465: for the corresponding simplices. Assume that the cover is locally nonempty for the branch, so that objects
  466: \[
  467: x_i\in \mathfrak G_v(U_i)
  468: \]
  469: exist for all $i$, and assume
  470: \[
  471: H^1(U_{i_0\cdots i_n},\Band)=0
  472: \]
  473: for every nonempty intersection used below, including all members, pairwise overlaps, triple overlaps, and quadruple overlaps appearing in the descent calculation.
  474: Then:
  475: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
  476: \item for every pair $(i,j)$ there exists an isomorphism
  477: \[
  478: \theta_{ij}:x_i|_{U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim} x_j|_{U_{ij}};
  479: \]
  480: \item on each triple overlap $U_{ijk}$, the element
  481: \[
  482: c_{ijk}:=\theta_{ik}^{-1}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{jk}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{ij}|_{U_{ijk}}
  483: \in \Band(U_{ijk})
  484: \]
  485: defines a \v{C}ech $2$-cocycle $c=(c_{ijk})\in \check Z^2(\mathcal U,\Band)$;
  486: \item the class $[c]\in \check H^2(\mathcal U,\Band)$ depends only on the branch gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ and the chosen cover: changing the local objects or the comparison isomorphisms changes $c$ by a \v{C}ech coboundary, and refinements of acyclic covers pull this class back functorially;
  487: \item the class $[c]$ vanishes if and only if the branch gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ is neutral over $a$.
  488: \end{enumerate}
  489: \end{theorem}
  490: 
  491: \begin{proof}
  492: By local nonemptiness, fix objects $x_i\in \mathfrak G_v(U_i)$. For every pair $(i,j)$, the sheaf
  493: \[
  494: \mathrm{Isom}(x_i|_{U_{ij}},x_j|_{U_{ij}})
  495: \]
  496: is a $\Band|_{U_{ij}}$-torsor. The vanishing
  497: \[
  498: H^1(U_{ij},\Band)=0
  499: \]
  500: therefore trivializes this torsor \cite[Chap.~III]{MacLaneMoerdijk1994}, \cite[Sec.~11]{StacksProject}, so we may choose a section
  501: \[
  502: \theta_{ij}:x_i|_{U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim} x_j|_{U_{ij}}.
  503: \]
  504: This proves \textup{(i)}.
  505: 
  506: On a triple overlap $U_{ijk}$, the composite
  507: \[
  508: c_{ijk}:=\theta_{ik}^{-1}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{jk}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{ij}|_{U_{ijk}}
  509: \]
  510: is an automorphism of $x_i|_{U_{ijk}}$ and hence an element of $\Band(U_{ijk})$. On a quadruple overlap $U_{ijkl}$ one computes
  511: \[
  512: c_{jkl}\,c_{ijl}
  513: =
  514: \theta_{jl}^{-1}\theta_{kl}\theta_{jk}\,\theta_{il}^{-1}\theta_{jl}\theta_{ij}
  515: =
  516: \theta_{il}^{-1}\theta_{kl}\theta_{ik}^{-1}\theta_{ik}\theta_{jk}\theta_{ij}
  517: =
  518: c_{ikl}\,c_{ijk},
  519: \]
  520: where all restrictions to $U_{ijkl}$ are suppressed from the notation. This is exactly the \v{C}ech cocycle identity $\delta c=0$, so $c=(c_{ijk})$ lies in $\check Z^2(\mathcal U,\Band)$. This proves \textup{(ii)}.
  521: 
  522: For \textup{(iii)}, first keep the objects $x_i$ fixed and replace $\theta_{ij}$ by another choice $\theta'_{ij}$. Since $\mathrm{Isom}(x_i|_{U_{ij}},x_j|_{U_{ij}})$ is a $\Band|_{U_{ij}}$-torsor, there exist unique sections $h_{ij}\in \Band(U_{ij})$ with
  523: \[
  524: \theta'_{ij}=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}.
  525: \]
  526: A direct calculation on triple overlaps gives
  527: \[
  528: c'_{ijk}=h_{ik}\,c_{ijk}\,h_{jk}^{-1}h_{ij}^{-1},
  529: \]
  530: which is the multiplicative form of the statement that $c'$ and $c$ differ by the coboundary $\delta h$.
  531: 
  532: Next replace the objects $x_i$ by another family $x_i'\in \mathfrak G_v(U_i)$. Since $x_i$ and $x_i'$ lie in the same gerbe over $U_i$, the sheaf $\mathrm{Isom}(x_i,x_i')$ is a $\Band|_{U_i}$-torsor. The vanishing
  533: \[
  534: H^1(U_i,\Band)=0
  535: \]
  536: trivializes it, so choose isomorphisms
  537: \[
  538: \psi_i:x_i\xrightarrow{\sim} x_i'.
  539: \]
  540: Define
  541: \[
  542: \theta_{ij}':=\psi_j|_{U_{ij}}\circ \theta_{ij}\circ \psi_i^{-1}|_{U_{ij}}.
  543: \]
  544: Then the associated cocycle satisfies
  545: \[
  546: c'_{ijk}
  547: =
  548: \psi_i|_{U_{ijk}}\,c_{ijk}\,\psi_i^{-1}|_{U_{ijk}}
  549: =
  550: c_{ijk},
  551: \]
  552: under the band identification $\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}(x_i)\cong \Band|_{U_i}$. Thus changing local objects changes the cocycle only by the preliminary freedom in the choice of the $\psi_i$, hence only by a coboundary. If
  553: \[
  554: \lambda:\mathcal V\to\mathcal U
  555: \]
  556: is a refinement by another cover $\mathcal V\in\Cov_{p,s}(a)$ whose \v{C}ech nerve exists and whose relevant intersections satisfy the same $H^1$-vanishing hypothesis, then the same construction on $\mathcal V$ uses the pulled-back objects and isomorphisms, so the resulting cocycle is exactly $\lambda^*(c)$. Therefore the induced cohomology class is refinement compatible.
  557: 
  558: For \textup{(iv)}, suppose first that $\mathfrak G_v$ is neutral and choose a global object $x\in \mathfrak G_v(a)$. For each $i$, the sheaf $\mathrm{Isom}(x|_{U_i},x_i)$ is a $\Band|_{U_i}$-torsor and therefore trivial because $H^1(U_i,\Band)=0$. Choose
  559: \[
  560: \psi_i:x|_{U_i}\xrightarrow{\sim} x_i.
  561: \]
  562: If we set
  563: \[
  564: \theta_{ij}:=\psi_j|_{U_{ij}}\circ \psi_i^{-1}|_{U_{ij}},
  565: \]
  566: then the resulting cocycle is identically zero, so $[c]=0$.
  567: 
  568: Conversely, assume $[c]=0$. Then there is a $1$-cochain $h=(h_{ij})$ with
  569: \[
  570: c=\delta h.
  571: \]
  572: Replace $\theta_{ij}$ by
  573: \[
  574: \theta_{ij}':=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}.
  575: \]
  576: The defining equation $c=\delta h$ is exactly the statement that the $\theta_{ij}'$ satisfy the strict cocycle condition on triple overlaps. Hence $(x_i,\theta_{ij}')$ is a descent datum for the stack $\mathfrak G_v$, and since $\mathfrak G_v$ is a stack in groupoids, that descent datum is effective. The resulting glued object of $\mathfrak G_v(a)$ is a global object, so the gerbe is neutral.
  577: \end{proof}
  578: 
  579: \begin{lemma}[\v{C}ech calculus for branch gerbes]\label{lem:cech-branch-gerbe-calculus}
  580: In the setting of \Cref{thm:cech-bridge-compatible-realizations}, the following four operations have the indicated effects on the cocycle
  581: \[
  582: c_{ijk}:=\theta_{ik}^{-1}\theta_{jk}\theta_{ij}.
  583: \]
  584: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
  585: \item If the local objects $x_i$ are fixed and $\theta'_{ij}=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}$ for a $1$-cochain $h=(h_{ij})$ with values in $\Band$, then the new cocycle is
  586: \[
  587: c'_{ijk}=h_{ik}\,c_{ijk}\,h_{jk}^{-1}h_{ij}^{-1};
  588: \]
  589: in additive notation, $c'-c=\delta h$.
  590: \item If local objects $x_i$ are replaced by $x_i'$ and $\psi_i:x_i\xrightarrow{\sim}x_i'$ are chosen, then
  591: \[
  592: \theta'_{ij}:=\psi_j\theta_{ij}\psi_i^{-1}
  593: \]
  594: produces the same cocycle under the band identifications. Changing the $\psi_i$ modifies the result only by a coboundary of the kind in \textup{(i)}.
  595: \item If $\lambda:\mathcal V\to\mathcal U$ is a refinement satisfying the same acyclicity hypotheses, the cocycle constructed from the pulled-back objects and isomorphisms on $\mathcal V$ is $\lambda^*c$.
  596: \item The class $[c]$ is zero if and only if the descent datum may be adjusted so that the comparison isomorphisms satisfy the strict cocycle condition; by effective descent for the stack $\mathfrak G_v$, this is equivalent to a global object of $\mathfrak G_v(a)$.
  597: \end{enumerate}
  598: \end{lemma}
  599: 
  600: \begin{proof}
  601: For \textup{(i)}, the Isom sheaf on $U_{ij}$ is a $\Band$-torsor, so the ratio between $\theta_{ij}$ and $\theta'_{ij}$ is the unique element $h_{ij}^{-1}\in\Band(U_{ij})$ with
  602: \[
  603: \theta'_{ij}=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}.
  604: \]
  605: Substituting these three identities in
  606: \[
  607: c'_{ijk}=(\theta'_{ik})^{-1}\theta'_{jk}\theta'_{ij}
  608: \]
  609: and using that the band is abelian gives
  610: \[
  611: c'_{ijk}=h_{ik}\,c_{ijk}\,h_{jk}^{-1}h_{ij}^{-1}.
  612: \]
  613: This is precisely the multiplicative Cech coboundary formula.
  614: 
  615: For \textup{(ii)}, substituting
  616: \[
  617: \theta'_{ij}=\psi_j\theta_{ij}\psi_i^{-1}
  618: \]
  619: into the triple product gives
  620: \[
  621: (\theta'_{ik})^{-1}\theta'_{jk}\theta'_{ij}
  622: =
  623: \psi_i\theta_{ik}^{-1}\theta_{jk}\theta_{ij}\psi_i^{-1}.
  624: \]
  625: Under the band identification
  626: \[
  627: \underline{\mathrm{Aut}}(x_i)\cong\Band|_{U_i}\cong
  628: \underline{\mathrm{Aut}}(x_i'),
  629: \]
  630: conjugation by $\psi_i$ is the identity on $\Band$. If the isomorphisms $\psi_i$ are changed, the new choices differ from the old ones by a $0$-cochain in the automorphism sheaves, and the resulting comparison isomorphisms differ from the displayed $\theta'_{ij}$ by the coboundary described in \textup{(i)}.
  631: 
  632: For \textup{(iii)}, a refinement map $\lambda$ sends each member of $\mathcal V$ to one member of $\mathcal U$. Pulling back the objects $x_i$ and maps $\theta_{ij}$ along these assignments gives on each triple overlap of $\mathcal V$ exactly the restriction of $c_{\lambda(i)\lambda(j)\lambda(k)}$. This is the usual pullback cocycle $\lambda^*c$.
  633: 
  634: For \textup{(iv)}, if $[c]=0$, choose $h$ with $c=\delta h$ and replace $\theta_{ij}$ by $\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}$. By \textup{(i)} the new cocycle is zero, which says exactly that the modified $\theta_{ij}$ satisfy the strict descent condition on triple overlaps. Since $\mathfrak G_v$ is a stack, this descent datum is effective and glues to an object of $\mathfrak G_v(a)$. Conversely, a global object $x\in\mathfrak G_v(a)$ and local isomorphisms $x|_{U_i}\cong x_i$ produce comparison maps whose triple defect is zero; \textup{(ii)} and \textup{(i)} then show that the original cocycle class is zero.
  635: \end{proof}
  636: 
  637: \begin{proposition}[Derived branch classes detect neutrality]\label{prop:derived-branch-classification}
  638: Assume \textup{(H1)}--\textup{(H4)}. For every visible branch $v$,
  639: \[
  640: \omega_v=0\ \text{in}\ H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band)
  641: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  642: \mathfrak G_v\ \text{is neutral}.
  643: \]
  644: \end{proposition}
  645: 
  646: \begin{proof}
  647: By \Cref{def:cech-gluing-obstruction}, the derived class $\omega_v$ is the image of the refinement-compatible \v{C}ech classes supplied by \Cref{thm:cech-bridge-compatible-realizations}. The comparison isomorphism in \textup{(H4)} identifies this class with the standard Giraud class of the banded gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$. The vanishing of that derived class is equivalent to neutrality by the classification of banded gerbes by $H^2$ \cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}, \cite[Tag 06NY]{StacksProject}.
  648: \end{proof}
  649: 
  650: \begin{proposition}[Compatible local sections versus visible branches on separated slices]\label{prop:compsecs-vs-visible-branches}
  651: Assume that $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ is admitted at $p$, admits a realization prestack in the sense of \Cref{def:realization-prestack}, and that $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is slice-separated. Then visible branches detect strict compatible local realizability, without any global-conservativity or derived-comparison hypothesis:
  652: \[
  653: \Vis_{p,s}(r)\neq\varnothing
  654: \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  655: M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r).
  656: \]
  657: \end{proposition}
  658: 
  659: \begin{proof}
  660: By \Cref{thm:visible-value-components},
  661: \[
  662: \Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a).
  663: \]
  664: The implication from strict compatible local sectionability to
  665: nonemptiness of this sheafification is
  666: \Cref{prop:compsec-to-sheafification}. Conversely, a visible branch gives
  667: an element of \((a_{p,s}F_{p,s})(a)\) by the same displayed
  668: identification. The slice-separated hypothesis is exactly the additional
  669: condition needed to strictify that sheafified section: applying
  670: \Cref{thm:sheafification-characterization} to
  671: \(F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\) produces a cover of \(a\) and a strict
  672: matching family of \(F_{p,s}\)-sections on that cover. By
  673: \Cref{def:existence-levels}(ii), this is
  674: \[
  675: M,p\Vdash\CompSec_s(r).

 succeeded in 222ms:
    1: \subsection{Realization of prescribed branch data}
    2: 
    3: The preceding algebra isolates the branchwise and aggregate quotients attached to a given family of hidden subgroups. The next theorem gives a converse existence statement on connected finite nerves with vanishing first homology. It is internal to the fixed finite-nerve framework of the paper: the nerve, the constant band, and the comparison data are fixed in advance, and the theorem constructs local-object and prestack data realizing the prescribed images.
    4: 
    5: The realization proofs use the following explicit admissible-expansion
    6: interface. The site is not an arbitrary full subcategory of intersections.
    7: It is the formal \v{C}ech site of a finite good-cover presentation, so that
    8: the cohomological comparison used later is an actual Leray comparison rather
    9: than a declared comparison datum.
   10: 
   11: \begin{lemma}[Formal \v{C}ech site, Leray comparison, and one-state admissible expansion]\label{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}
   12: Let
   13: \[
   14: \mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
   15: \]
   16: be a finite cover whose nerve $N=N(\mathcal U)$ is realized by a good cover
   17: of a paracompact space $X\simeq |N|$. Let $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ be the
   18: formal \v{C}ech index site over this chosen good-cover realization, defined
   19: as follows. Its objects are $a$, one strict initial object $\emptyset$, and
   20: one object $U_J$ for each nonempty simplex $J\in N$. A realization functor
   21: from this formal category to open subsets of \(X\) sends \(a\) to \(X\),
   22: \(\emptyset\) to the empty open set, and \(U_J\) to the corresponding
   23: nonempty good-cover intersection. This is a pullback-complete formal site,
   24: not the full subcategory on only nonempty intersections. The same strict
   25: initial object represents every empty finite intersection. Thus the only
   26: finite subsets represented by named non-initial objects are the simplices
   27: of \(N\), but a finite subset \(J\subseteq I\) which is not a simplex is
   28: not discarded from the site-level pullback calculus; whenever such a
   29: subset arises as a pullback intersection, the pullback is the
   30: distinguished object \(\emptyset\). In formulas
   31: we write
   32: \[
   33: \widetilde U_J:=
   34: \begin{cases}
   35: U_J,& J\in N,\\
   36: \emptyset,& J\notin N.
   37: \end{cases}
   38: \]
   39: Thus empty intersections are not omitted from the site: all of them are
   40: identified with the same strict initial pullback object $\emptyset$, and
   41: the realization of \(\widetilde U_J\) is the actual intersection
   42: \(\bigcap_{j\in J}U_j\), possibly empty. The order is generated by
   43: \[
   44: \emptyset\to U_J\to U_K\to a
   45: \qquad (K\subseteq J,\; J,K\in N).
   46: \]
   47: There is a unique arrow \(\emptyset\to W\) for every object \(W\), and no
   48: non-initial object maps to \(\emptyset\).
   49: For any two arrows $U_J\to U_L$ and $U_K\to U_L$ with
   50: $L\subseteq J\cap K$, their fibre product is
   51: \[
   52: U_J\times_{U_L}U_K=\widetilde U_{J\cup K}.
   53: \]
   54: If $J\cup K\notin N$, this means that the fibre product is the strict
   55: initial object $\emptyset$. Any fibre product in which one factor is
   56: $\emptyset$ is also $\emptyset$. The pretopology is the smallest
   57: pretopology for which the vertex cover \(\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}\), all of
   58: its pullbacks along arrows of the formal category, all identity covers,
   59: and the empty cover of \(\emptyset\) are covers. Equivalently, it is
   60: obtained by repeatedly applying pullback stability and transitivity to
   61: the vertex cover inside the formal category just described. For example,
   62: the pullback of the vertex cover along \(U_J\to a\) is the family of
   63: arrows
   64: \[
   65: \{\widetilde U_{J\cup\{i\}}\to U_J\}_{i\in I},
   66: \]
   67: with the terms for non-simplices equal to \(\emptyset\). Repeated
   68: occurrences of \(\emptyset\) in such a pulled-back family may be
   69: identified with the same initial object. They are harmless in matching
   70: conditions because the coefficient sheaves used below have zero value at
   71: \(\emptyset\), but the corresponding pullback object is still present in
   72: the site. In particular, disjoint cover members \(U_i,U_j\to a\) have the
   73: pullback \(U_i\times_aU_j=\emptyset\) in the site. The empty object is
   74: omitted only from the nonempty \v{C}ech nerve and from the displayed
   75: nonzero cochain groups, never from the local-reference-site structure.
   76: Every pullback demanded by \Cref{def:local-reference-frame} is therefore
   77: an object of \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), including the empty pullback
   78: objects produced by disjoint members of a covering family.
   79: Thus closure under the pretopology axioms is part of the definition, not a
   80: consequence of omitting any empty pullback object. Then:
   81: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
   82: \item $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ is a pullback-based local reference site
   83: with terminal object $a$; in particular, for every pair of cover members
   84: $U_i,U_j\to a$, the fibre product is the formal object $U_{\{i,j\}}$ if
   85: $\{i,j\}\in N$ and is the strict initial object $\emptyset$ otherwise;
   86: \item the cover $\mathcal U$ remains a cover of $a$ in
   87: $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$, all pullbacks of members of this cover exist
   88: there, and its nonempty \v{C}ech nerve is the finite simplicial complex
   89: $N$;
   90: \item for every abelian group $A$, let $A_{\mathcal U}$ denote the genuine
   91: constant sheaf
   92: on \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\)
   93: \[
   94: A_{\mathcal U}(W)=\operatorname{LC}(|W|,A),
   95: \]
   96: where \(|W|\subseteq X\) is the open set represented by \(W\). This is the
   97: restriction of the genuine constant sheaf of locally constant
   98: \(A\)-valued functions on the chosen good-cover realization. It is this
   99: sheaf, not the identity-restriction constant presheaf on the abstract
  100: index category, that is used as the band. With these coefficients the
  101: \v{C}ech cochain complex of
  102: $\mathcal U$ with coefficients in $A_{\mathcal U}$ and the finite nerve
  103: presentation of any class in $H^2(N(\mathcal U),A)$ are the same finite
  104: cochain data after replacing $\mathcal C_a$ with
  105: $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$. This is a cochain-level identification because
  106: the cover is a chosen good cover: on connected nonempty basic
  107: intersections \(A_{\mathcal U}\) has value \(A\), while on the strict
  108: initial object it has value \(0\);
  109: \item let \(\mathcal B_X\) be any ordinary basis site of the chosen
  110: good-cover realization \(X\) which contains the members of
  111: \(\mathcal U\), their nonempty finite intersections, \(X\), and the empty
  112: open set. The formal site \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) is only the finite
  113: pullback site generated by the selected vertex cover, with the single strict
  114: initial object representing all empty intersections; it is not asserted to
  115: be a basis site for the topology of \(X\). The precise basis condition used
  116: below is internal to this finite site: the pretopology on
  117: \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), and on each slice over a nonempty formal
  118: intersection, is generated by the vertex cover and its iterated pullbacks.
  119: For the genuine constant sheaf \(A_{\mathcal U}\), this makes the vertex
  120: cover an \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-Leray cover in the finite-site sense. Hence the
  121: Cech-to-derived comparison for this generated cover gives a natural
  122: isomorphism
  123: \[
  124: H^n(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U},A_{\mathcal U})\cong \check H^n(\mathcal U,A_{\mathcal U})\cong H^n(N,A)
  125: \]
  126: for all $n$, and the \v{C}ech cocycle on the vertex cover maps to the
  127: corresponding derived cohomology class;
  128: \item for every contravariant functor
  129: \[
  130: F:\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}^{op}\to\mathsf{Set},
  131: \]
  132: there is a one-state $\mathbb L_2$-model $M_2$ with state $p$ and a
  133: distinguished admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ such that $[r]_p=a$, the
  134: local slice site of $r$ is exactly $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$, and the local
  135: object functor on that slice site is $F$; this model is the admissible
  136: $\mathbb L_2$-expansion supplied by
  137: \Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface};
  138: \item if, in addition, a category fibred in groupoids
  139: \[
  140: \mathfrak P\to\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}
  141: \]
  142: with abelian sheaf band $\Band$ satisfies
  143: \[
  144: \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)\cong F
  145: \]
  146: and the automorphism-sheaf condition of
  147: \Cref{def:realization-prestack}, then $M_2$ has an admissible
  148: $\mathbb L_3$-expansion $M_3$ in which $r$ has realization prestack
  149: $\mathfrak P$, band $\Band$, and stackification $a(\mathfrak P)$.
  150: \end{enumerate}
  151: \end{lemma}
  152: 
  153: \begin{proof}
  154: The formal index category is thin and has terminal object $a$. We first
  155: verify the pullbacks, since they are part of the local-reference-site
  156: structure and not merely a convention about the \v{C}ech cochain complex.
  157: Suppose that \(U_J\to U_L\) and \(U_K\to U_L\) are two maps, so
  158: \(L\subseteq J\cap K\). If \(J\cup K\in N\), then \(U_{J\cup K}\) maps to
  159: both \(U_J\) and \(U_K\), and any object mapping to both over \(U_L\) is
  160: indexed by a simplex containing \(J\cup K\), hence factors uniquely through
  161: \(U_{J\cup K}\). If \(J\cup K\notin N\), no nonempty simplex can contain the
  162: union, so the only object mapping to both over \(U_L\) is the strict initial
  163: object \(\emptyset\). Pullbacks involving \(\emptyset\) are \(\emptyset\) by
  164: strict initiality. Therefore every fibre product required by
  165: \Cref{def:local-reference-frame} is an object of \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\).
  166: In particular, for every pair in the covering family \(\{U_i\to a\}\) the
  167: pullback is \(U_{\{i,j\}}\) when \(\{i,j\}\in N\) and is \(\emptyset\)
  168: otherwise. This proves the pullback-object clause in \textup{(i)}; the
  169: identity, pullback-stability, and transitivity pretopology axioms hold by
  170: construction of the generated pretopology.
  171: 
  172: The covering family $\mathcal U$ is one of the generators of the
  173: pretopology on $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$. A finite set $J\subseteq I$
  174: spans a simplex precisely when the iterated pullback of the corresponding
  175: $U_i$ is a nonempty object $U_J$ rather than $\emptyset$. Hence the
  176: \v{C}ech nerve of the cover is $N$, proving \textup{(ii)}.
  177: 
  178: The sheaf \(A_{\mathcal U}\) is defined by restricting the genuine
  179: constant sheaf of locally constant \(A\)-valued functions on \(X\) along
  180: the realization functor \(W\mapsto |W|\). Equivalently,
  181: \(A_{\mathcal U}(W)=\operatorname{LC}(|W|,A)\), with the usual sheaf
  182: restriction maps for locally constant functions. Since every nonempty
  183: formal intersection \(U_J\) is contractible, hence connected, its sections
  184: are \(A\); over the strict initial object the section group is \(0\). This
  185: is a sheaf before it is used as a band: no sheaf condition is being
  186: inferred from the identity-restriction constant presheaf on the formal
  187: index category. More explicitly, if a generated cover of an object \(W\)
  188: contains several nonempty pieces, a matching family is a family of locally
  189: constant functions on the corresponding open subsets which agree on the
  190: nonempty realized overlaps and impose no condition on empty pullbacks,
  191: where the coefficient group is \(0\). These functions glue uniquely to a
  192: locally constant function on \(|W|\) because they are sections of the
  193: ambient constant sheaf restricted to the cover.
  194: Hence the \v{C}ech $n$-cochains on
  195: $\mathcal U$ are exactly functions from the set of nonempty
  196: $(n+1)$-fold intersections to $A$; empty pullback terms contribute only
  197: zero sections. Thus empty intersections are present as pullback objects
  198: in $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$, but their coefficient group is
  199: $A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0$, so omitting them from the finite cochain
  200: display changes neither cochains nor coboundaries. The preceding
  201: paragraph identifies that nonzero indexing set before and after passing
  202: to $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$, and the alternating coboundary is the same.
  203: Hence the \v{C}ech cochain complex, the simplicial cochain complex of
  204: $N(\mathcal U)$, and the
  205: universal-coefficient data used in the finite nerve presentation are
  206: identical. This proves \textup{(iii)}.
  207: 
  208: For \textup{(iv)}, choose an ordinary basis site \(\mathcal B_X\) of the
  209: good-cover realization containing \(X\), \(\emptyset\), and the members of
  210: \(\mathcal U\) with all their nonempty finite intersections. This basis site
  211: is used only to define the genuine constant sheaf of locally constant
  212: \(A\)-valued functions. The finite formal category
  213: \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) is not that basis site and is not a basis for all
  214: opens of \(X\): it remembers only the terminal object, the selected cover,
  215: their formal nonempty intersections, and the single strict initial object for
  216: empty intersections.
  217: 
  218: The site-theoretic condition needed for cohomology is the following finite
  219: cover-basis condition. By definition of the generated pretopology, every
  220: covering family in \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), and in every slice over a
  221: nonempty formal intersection \(U_J\), is refined by a family obtained from
  222: the vertex cover by finitely many pullbacks and transitivity. For such a
  223: slice the nonempty realized members are exactly finite intersections of the
  224: good-cover members inside the contractible open set \(|U_J|\); terms whose
  225: formal pullback is \(\emptyset\) contribute only the zero group
  226: \(A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0\). Thus the pulled-back vertex cover is a
  227: Leray cover for \(A_{\mathcal U}\) on each nonempty proper formal
  228: intersection. Equivalently,
  229: \[
  230: H^q(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/U_J,A_{\mathcal U})=0
  231: \qquad(q>0)
  232: \]
  233: for every nonempty proper intersection object \(U_J\). This is the acyclicity
  234: hypothesis in the Leray theorem for sites with a covering basis
  235: \cite{Bredon1997,Jardine2015,MacLaneMoerdijk1994}.
  236: 
  237: Applying that Leray theorem to the vertex cover of the terminal object \(a\)
  238: identifies the derived sheaf cohomology on the generated finite site with the
  239: \v{C}ech cohomology of this cover. By \textup{(iii)}, the resulting Cech
  240: cochain complex is the simplicial cochain complex of the nonempty nerve
  241: \(N\) with coefficients in \(A\): nonempty intersections contribute one copy
  242: of \(A\), and empty intersections contribute \(0\). Hence
  243: \[
  244: H^n(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U},A_{\mathcal U})
  245: \cong \check H^n(\mathcal U,A_{\mathcal U})
  246: \cong H^n(N,A)
  247: \]
  248: naturally in \(A\). Under this comparison a vertex-cover \v{C}ech
  249: \(2\)-cocycle represents exactly the corresponding derived class in
  250: \(H^2(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U},A_{\mathcal U})\). The argument uses only the
  251: generated finite cover-basis condition just stated; it does not assert an
  252: equivalence of topoi between \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) and the open-set
  253: site of \(X\).
  254: 
  255: For \textup{(v)}, build the lower structure explicitly. Let the state set be
  256: the singleton $\{p\}$. For each object $U$ of $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$, take a
  257: reference element $r_U$ of sort $\mathsf{Ref}_s$, interpret $\Adm_s$ at $p$ as
  258: the set of all these elements, and put $[r_U]_p=U$. This gives a surjective
  259: reference-class assignment onto the object set of $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$.
  260: Let $r:=r_a$ be the distinguished terminal reference. The refinement relation
  261: and pretopology on these reference classes are those of
  262: $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$; its identity, pullback-stability, transitivity, and
  263: pullback-object clauses were verified in \textup{(i)}. Thus the slice site over
  264: $[r]_p=a$ is exactly $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$. For reference sorts not used in
  265: the construction, take empty reference sets or the inherited background
  266: interpretation. Assign the given functor $F$ as the local object functor on
  267: that slice site. These data are exactly the semantic data required by
  268: \Cref{def:reference-classes,def:local-reference-frame,def:local-object-functor};
  269: the lower $\mathbb L_1$-reduct is the same one-state information model with
  270: the added site and local-object symbols forgotten. Hence they define the
  271: claimed $\mathbb L_2$-model by the local-object expansion case of
  272: \Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}. Thus the existence
  273: assertions in this subsection are relative to the deliberately broad
  274: well-typed admissible-expansion semantics of that proposition, not to an
  275: independently axiomatized smaller class of semantic models.
  276: 
  277: For \textup{(vi)}, expand this $\mathbb L_2$-model by the supplied fibred
  278: groupoid $\mathfrak P$, the band $\Band$, and the stackification
  279: $a(\mathfrak P)$. The displayed identification
  280: $\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)\cong F$ gives clause \textup{(i)} of
  281: \Cref{def:realization-prestack}, and the assumed automorphism-sheaf condition
  282: gives clause \textup{(ii)}. Thus $\mathfrak P$ is a realization prestack for
  283: $r$ at $p$ over the local slice site $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$, and adding it
  284: is precisely the $\mathbb L_2\preceq\mathbb L_3$ expansion described in
  285: \Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface} and instantiated in
  286: \Cref{prop:chain-instantiation}. This proves \textup{(v)} and \textup{(vi)}.
  287: \end{proof}
  288: 
  289: \begin{lemma}[Genuine constant sheaf and isomorphism sheaves on the formal site]\label{lem:formal-site-constant-sheaf-banding}
  290: In the formal \v{C}ech site $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ of
  291: \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}, let $A_{\mathcal U}$ be the
  292: genuine constant sheaf associated with an abelian group $A$ on the chosen
  293: good-cover realization, restricted to the formal site. This is part of the
  294: input supplied by \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}; it is not
  295: deduced from the identity-restriction constant presheaf on the formal
  296: index category. Equivalently,
  297: \[
  298: A_{\mathcal U}(W)=\operatorname{LC}(|W|,A)
  299: \]
  300: for the open subset \(|W|\subseteq X\) represented by \(W\), with
  301: \(A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0\); on connected nonempty intersections this
  302: is identified with \(A\), but that identification is a consequence of
  303: connectedness of the chosen good-cover pieces, not the definition of a
  304: constant presheaf on the index category. For every object \(W\), all sheaf
  305: assertions below are made on the slice topology
  306: \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W\), whose covers include the pullbacks
  307: generated by the strict initial object for empty overlaps.
  308: Then:
  309: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
  310: \item for every nonempty basic intersection object $U_J$,
  311: $A_{\mathcal U}(U_J)=\operatorname{LC}(|U_J|,A)\cong A$, while
  312: $A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0$; at the terminal object \(a\), the value is
  313: the group of locally constant functions on the whole good-cover
  314: realization, namely \(A^{\pi_0(|N|)}\), and this is \(A\) in the
  315: connected-nerve applications below;
  316: \item the restriction of $A_{\mathcal U}$ to every slice
  317: $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W$ is again a sheaf. Its value on an object
  318: \(V\to W\) is always \(\operatorname{LC}(|V|,A)\): this is \(A\) for a
  319: connected nonempty basic intersection, \(0\) for \(\emptyset\), and the
  320: locally constant functions on \(|V|\) for any possibly disconnected
  321: realized open set;
  322: \item for every generated covering family
  323: \[
  324: \{V_\ell\to V\}_{\ell\in L}
  325: \]
  326: in a slice, a family \(a_\ell\in A_{\mathcal U}(V_\ell)\) is a matching
  327: family exactly when the corresponding locally constant functions agree on
  328: each nonempty pullback \(V_\ell\times_VV_m\), while pullbacks equal to
  329: \(\emptyset\) impose only the unique equality in
  330: \(A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0\). Such matching families glue uniquely in
  331: \(A_{\mathcal U}(V)\);
  332: \item if \(x,y\) are two objects of a fibred groupoid over \(W\) whose
  333: isomorphism presheaf is defined on each \(V\to W\) by an explicit
  334: identification
  335: \[
  336: \underline{\mathrm{Isom}}(x,y)(V)=A_{\mathcal U}(V)
  337: \]
  338: with restrictions those of \(A_{\mathcal U}\), then
  339: \(\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}(x,y)\) is a sheaf on the slice
  340: \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W\). In particular, automorphism presheaves
  341: defined in this way are genuine sheaves banded by \(A_{\mathcal U}\).
  342: \end{enumerate}
  343: \end{lemma}
  344: 
  345: \begin{proof}
  346: The sheaf \(A_{\mathcal U}\) has already been defined as the restriction
  347: of the genuine constant sheaf on the chosen good-cover realization. Thus
  348: the sheaf property is inherited before any calculation of values on the
  349: formal index category is made. Each nonempty object $U_J$ is represented
  350: by a nonempty finite intersection of good-cover members, hence by a
  351: connected contractible space, so locally constant $A$-valued functions on
  352: it are just constant functions with value in $A$. The strict initial
  353: object represents the empty open set and has the zero group of sections.
  354: The terminal object \(a\) represents the whole good-cover realization, so
  355: its sections are locally constant functions on \(|N|\), i.e.
  356: \(A^{\pi_0(|N|)}\). In the realization theorems that use this lemma the
  357: nerve is connected, and this terminal value is a single copy of \(A\).
  358: This proves \textup{(i)}.
  359: 
  360: Restriction of a sheaf to a slice site is a sheaf, so
  361: $A_{\mathcal U}|_{\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W}$ is a sheaf on every slice.
  362: Equivalently, if
  363: \[
  364: \{V_\ell\to V\}_{\ell\in L}
  365: \]
  366: is any covering family in the generated pretopology of
  367: \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W\), a matching family of sections of
  368: $A_{\mathcal U}$ over the \(V_\ell\) glues uniquely because those sections
  369: are locally constant functions on the corresponding good-cover
  370: intersections. If \(|V|\) is connected and nonempty, the glued section is a
  371: single element of \(A\), and compatibility along nonempty pullback
  372: overlaps is exactly equality of those local constants. If \(|V|\) is
  373: disconnected, the same sheaf axiom glues independently on the connected
  374: components of \(|V|\), giving \(\operatorname{LC}(|V|,A)\) rather than a
  375: single copy of \(A\).
  376: Cover members whose pullback is \(\emptyset\) impose no equality because
  377: \(A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0\); when \(V=\emptyset\), the only covering
  378: is refined by the empty cover and the unique section is \(0\). This proves
  379: \textup{(ii)} without invoking the identity-restriction constant
  380: presheaf, and the same paragraph proves the explicit matching-family
  381: description in \textup{(iii)}.
  382: 
  383: For \textup{(iv)}, the displayed identification is an isomorphism
  384: \[
  385: \underline{\mathrm{Isom}}(x,y)\cong
  386: A_{\mathcal U}|_{\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W}
  387: \]
  388: of presheaves on the slice; the right-hand side is a sheaf by
  389: \textup{(ii)}. This is the automorphism-sheaf condition used below. Thus
  390: any later notation that writes the coefficient group as $A$ on nonempty
  391: basic objects abbreviates sections of the sheaf $A_{\mathcal U}$, not the
  392: constant presheaf with identity restrictions on an arbitrary site.
  393: \end{proof}
  394: 
  395: \begin{lemma}[Slice \(H^1\)-vanishing and neutral one-object stacks]\label{lem:formal-site-neutral-stack-h1}
  396: Let \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) be the formal \v{C}ech site of
  397: \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}, and assume that every
  398: nonempty good-cover intersection \(|U_J|\) is contractible and that
  399: \[
  400: H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)=0.
  401: \]
  402: Let
  403: \(A_{\mathcal U}\) be the genuine constant sheaf associated with an abelian
  404: group \(A\) on the chosen good-cover realization. Then for every object
  405: \(W\in\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\),
  406: \[
  407: H^1(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W,A_{\mathcal U})=0.
  408: \]
  409: Moreover, on every slice \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W\), the prestack
  410: \(P^0_W\) with one object \(*_V\) over each \(V\to W\), automorphism group
  411: \(\operatorname{Aut}(*_V)=A_{\mathcal U}(V)\), and restriction functors
  412: given by the sheaf restrictions of \(A_{\mathcal U}\), is a stack.
  413: \end{lemma}
  414: 
  415: \begin{proof}
  416: If \(W=\emptyset\), the slice has a terminal initial object with only the
  417: empty-cover contribution and \(A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0\), so the
  418: cohomology group is zero. If \(W=U_J\) is nonempty, the slice
  419: \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W\) is the formal good-cover site obtained by
  420: pulling the chosen good cover back to the contractible intersection
  421: \(|U_J|\). All nonempty finite intersections in that pulled-back cover are
  422: again contractible, and the strict initial object accounts for empty
  423: intersections. Leray's theorem for this acyclic cover identifies
  424: \[
  425: H^1(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W,A_{\mathcal U})
  426: \cong
  427: \check H^1(\mathcal U|_W,A_{\mathcal U})
  428: \cong
  429: H^1(|W|,A).
  430: \]
  431: The last group is zero because \(|W|\) is contractible. If \(W=a\), the
  432: same comparison gives
  433: \[
  434: H^1(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/a,A_{\mathcal U})
  435: \cong H^1(|N|,A).
  436: \]
  437: In the realization theorems that use neutral summands we assume
  438: \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\); the universal coefficient theorem then gives
  439: \[
  440: H^1(|N|,A)\cong \operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)=0
  441: \]
  442: for every abelian group \(A\). Thus the displayed vanishing holds on every
  443: relevant slice.
  444: 
  445: It remains to spell out why this vanishing, not merely the sheaf condition
  446: for automorphisms, makes the neutral one-object prestack a stack. Let
  447: \(\{V_i\to V\}\) be a cover in \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W\). A descent datum
  448: for objects of \(P^0_W\) consists of the unique local objects together with
  449: transition automorphisms
  450: \[
  451: g_{ij}\in A_{\mathcal U}(V_i\times_VV_j)
  452: \]
  453: satisfying \(g_{jk}+g_{ij}=g_{ik}\) on triple overlaps. This is precisely an
  454: \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-torsor cocycle on the slice over \(V\). Since
  455: \[
  456: H^1(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/V,A_{\mathcal U})=0,
  457: \]
  458: there are sections \(h_i\in A_{\mathcal U}(V_i)\) with
  459: \[
  460: g_{ij}=h_j-h_i.
  461: \]
  462: After changing the local identifications by the \(h_i\), the descent datum
  463: is equivalent to the trivial descent datum, which glues to the object
  464: \(*_V\). Descent for morphisms is exactly the sheaf condition for
  465: \(A_{\mathcal U}\), already proved in
  466: \Cref{lem:formal-site-constant-sheaf-banding}. Hence \(P^0_W\) is a stack.
  467: \end{proof}
  468: 
  469: \begin{lemma}[Nonempty refinements in the formal \v{C}ech site]\label{lem:formal-site-nonempty-refinements}
  470: Let \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) be the formal \v{C}ech site of
  471: \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}. If \(W\ne\emptyset\), then
  472: every covering family of \(W\) has a nonempty member. Consequently,
  473: pulling any covering family of \(a\) back to a vertex object \(U_i\)
  474: produces a cover of \(U_i\) with at least one nonempty member.
  475: \end{lemma}
  476: 
  477: \begin{proof}
  478: The pretopology on \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) is generated by the vertex
  479: cover, its pullbacks, identity covers, transitivity, and the empty cover of
  480: \(\emptyset\). The only generator with no nonempty member is the empty
  481: cover of the strict initial object. Pullback stability does not turn a
  482: cover of a nonempty object into a family consisting only of
  483: \(\emptyset\): if \(W=U_J\ne\emptyset\), the pullback of the vertex cover
  484: contains the term \(\widetilde U_{J\cup\{j\}}=U_J\) for every \(j\in J\).
  485: Identity covers of nonempty objects are nonempty, and transitivity
  486: preserves the existence of a nonempty member because a cover of a
  487: nonempty intermediate object again has a nonempty member by the same
  488: induction on the generation of covers. Thus every generated cover of
  489: \(W\ne\emptyset\) has at least one nonempty member. The final assertion is
  490: the case \(W=U_i\) after pullback.
  491: \end{proof}
  492: 
  493: \begin{lemma}[Sheafification of the formal label presheaf]\label{lem:formal-label-sheafification}
  494: Let \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) be the formal \v{C}ech site of
  495: \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}, and assume that the
  496: nonempty nerve \(N(\mathcal U)\) is connected. Let \(\Lambda\) be a set and
  497: \(\Lambda_0\subseteq\Lambda\). Define a presheaf \(F_{\Lambda_0}\) on
  498: \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) by
  499: \[
  500: F_{\Lambda_0}(W)=
  501: \begin{cases}
  502: \Lambda,& W\to a\text{ a nonempty proper formal intersection object},\\
  503: \Lambda_0,& W=a,\\
  504: \{\ast\},& W=\emptyset,
  505: \end{cases}
  506: \]
  507: with identity restrictions among nonempty proper intersection objects,
  508: the inclusion \(\Lambda_0\hookrightarrow\Lambda\) from \(a\) to nonempty
  509: proper objects, and the unique restriction to \(\emptyset\). Then
  510: \[
  511: \bigl(aF_{\Lambda_0}\bigr)(a)\cong\Lambda.
  512: \]
  513: Under this bijection, a label \(\lambda\in\Lambda\) is represented by the
  514: strict matching family that assigns \(\lambda\) to every vertex object
  515: \(U_i\).
  516: \end{lemma}
  517: 
  518: \begin{proof}
  519: For every \(\lambda\in\Lambda\), the constant vertex assignment
  520: \(\lambda_i=\lambda\in F_{\Lambda_0}(U_i)\) is a strict matching family:
  521: on a nonempty pairwise overlap the two restrictions are the same label,
  522: and on an empty pairwise overlap both restrictions are the unique element
  523: of \(F_{\Lambda_0}(\emptyset)\). This gives a section of
  524: \((aF_{\Lambda_0})(a)\).
  525: 
  526: Conversely, let \(\xi\in(aF_{\Lambda_0})(a)\). By
  527: \Cref{lem:strict-sheafification-representatives}, after replacing its
  528: plus--plus representative by a composite cover, \(\xi\) is represented by
  529: sections of \(F_{\Lambda_0}\) whose restrictions to each pullback overlap
  530: agree after a further cover of that overlap. Pull this cover back along
  531: the vertex cover \(\{U_i\to a\}\). For each vertex \(i\), the resulting
  532: cover of \(U_i\) has a nonempty member by
  533: \Cref{lem:formal-site-nonempty-refinements}; choose one such member and
  534: call the label carried by its section \(\lambda_i\in\Lambda\). This label
  535: is independent of the chosen nonempty member of the pulled-back cover of
  536: \(U_i\): two such members meet after a common covering refinement in the
  537: plus--plus representative, and on every nonempty refinement all
  538: restriction maps among nonempty proper objects are identities on
  539: \(\Lambda\), while refinements through \(\emptyset\) carry no label.
  540: 
  541: If two vertices \(i\) and \(j\) are joined by an edge of \(N\), the
  542: pullback \(U_{ij}=U_i\times_aU_j\) is a nonempty object. Pulling the
  543: representative further to \(U_{ij}\) and using
  544: \Cref{lem:formal-site-nonempty-refinements} gives a nonempty refinement on
  545: which the two restrictions agree in \(F_{\Lambda_0}\). Since the
  546: restrictions from \(U_i\) and \(U_j\) to any nonempty refinement of
  547: \(U_{ij}\) are identity maps on \(\Lambda\), this equality forces
  548: \(\lambda_i=\lambda_j\). The nerve is connected, so equality propagates
  549: along edge paths and all vertices carry one common label
  550: \(\lambda\in\Lambda\). Every nonempty piece of the composite
  551: representative maps to some vertex and therefore carries this same label.
  552: Thus \(\xi\) is the section represented by the constant vertex family with
  553: label \(\lambda\).
  554: 
  555: It remains only to check that distinct labels do not become equivalent.
  556: If the constant families labelled \(\lambda\) and \(\mu\) are equivalent
  557: in the plus--plus construction, then after a common covering refinement
  558: their restrictions agree on every nonempty member of that refinement.
  559: Pulling the common refinement back to any vertex object \(U_i\) gives a
  560: cover of \(U_i\). By \Cref{lem:formal-site-nonempty-refinements}, this
  561: cover has a nonempty member. On that member all restrictions among
  562: nonempty proper objects are identities on \(\Lambda\), hence
  563: \(\lambda=\mu\). Thus the displayed map
  564: \(\Lambda\to(aF_{\Lambda_0})(a)\) is bijective.
  565: \end{proof}
  566: 
  567: \begin{lemma}[Twisted branch prestack model]\label{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model}
  568: Let $\mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}$ be a finite cover whose nerve $N$ is connected, let $A$ be a finite abelian group, let $\Lambda$ be a finite nonempty label set, and let
  569: \[
  570: \alpha_\lambda\in Z^2(N,A)

\section{Preliminaries on the extension chain}\label{sec:preliminaries}

We fix the semantic framework used throughout. In particular, we distinguish conservative extension from the weaker singleton embedding that links the pointwise layer to information-state forcing.

\subsection*{Standing notation}
States are denoted by $p,q,u$, and admitted references by $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ with associated class $a=[r]_p$. The local reference site at a state and sort is $\mathcal C_{p,s}$, the local object functor is $F_{p,s}$, the visible value classes of a reference are $\Vis_{p,s}(r)$, and branch obstruction classes are denoted by $\omega$ or $\omega_v$. Subscripts such as $\mathrm{vis}$, $\mathrm{br}$, and $\mathrm{unif}$ consistently indicate visible, branch-sensitive, and branch-uniform quotients.

\begin{definition}[Logic layer]\label{def:logic-layer}
A \emph{logic layer} is a tuple
\[
\mathbb{L}=(\Sigma,\mathrm{Form},\Mod,\St,\Vdash,\models),
\]
where $\Sigma$ is a signature, $\mathrm{Form}$ is the class of well-formed $\Sigma$-formulas, $\Mod$ is a class of admissible models, $\St$ assigns to every $M\in\Mod$ a nonempty set $\St(M)$ of states, $\Vdash$ is a forcing relation on model-state pairs, and $\models$ is the induced consequence relation defined by
\[
\Gamma\models\varphi
\quad:\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\forall M\in\Mod\;\forall p\in\St(M)\,
\bigl(M,p\Vdash\Gamma \Rightarrow M,p\Vdash\varphi\bigr).
\]
\end{definition}

Forcing is the primitive notion; consequence is derived from it.

\begin{definition}[Conservative extension]\label{def:conservative-extension}
Let
\[
\mathbb{L}_m=(\Sigma_m,\mathrm{Form}_m,\Mod_m,\St_m,\Vdash_m,\models_m)
\quad\text{and}\quad
\mathbb{L}_n=(\Sigma_n,\mathrm{Form}_n,\Mod_n,\St_n,\Vdash_n,\models_n)
\]
with $m<n$. We say that $\mathbb{L}_n$ is a \emph{conservative extension} of $\mathbb{L}_m$, written $\mathbb{L}_m\preceq\mathbb{L}_n$, if there exist
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item a language embedding $e_{m,n}:\Sigma_m\hookrightarrow\Sigma_n$,
\item a forgetful functor $U_{n,m}:\Mod_n\to\Mod_m$,
\item for every $M\in\Mod_n$, a state projection
\[
\pi^M_{n,m}:\St_n(M)\to \St_m\bigl(U_{n,m}(M)\bigr),
\]
\end{enumerate}
such that for every $\varphi\in \mathrm{Form}_m$, every $M\in\Mod_n$, and every $p\in\St_n(M)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:conservative-extension}
M,p\Vdash_n \varphi
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
U_{n,m}(M),\pi^M_{n,m}(p)\Vdash_m \varphi.
\end{equation}
\end{definition}

Thus a higher layer may add coordinates or geometric data, but lower-layer formulas are interpreted unchanged after projection.

\begin{definition}[Singleton semantic embedding]\label{def:singleton-semantic-embedding}
Let $\mathbb{L}_0=(\Sigma_0,\mathrm{Form}_0,\Mod_0,\St_0,\Vdash_0,\models_0)$ and $\mathbb{L}_1=(\Sigma_1,\mathrm{Form}_1,\Mod_1,\St_1,\Vdash_1,\models_1)$. We say that $\mathbb{L}_0$ \emph{embeds into $\mathbb{L}_1$ by a singleton semantic embedding}, written $\mathbb{L}_0\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}_1$, if there exist
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item a language embedding $e_{0,1}:\Sigma_0\hookrightarrow\Sigma_1$,
\item a forgetful functor $U_{1,0}:\Mod_1\to\Mod_0$,
\item for every $M\in\Mod_1$ and every pointwise state $\rho\in\St_0(U_{1,0}(M))$, a canonical singleton state
\[
\iota_{0,1}^M(\rho)\in\St_1(M)
\]
\end{enumerate}
such that for every $\varphi\in\mathrm{Form}_0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:singleton-semantic-embedding}
M,\iota_{0,1}^M(\rho)\Vdash_1\varphi
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
U_{1,0}(M),\rho\Vdash_0\varphi.
\end{equation}
\end{definition}

A singleton semantic embedding is weaker than a conservative extension: it compares only singleton states, and no projection from arbitrary information states to pointwise states is required.

\begin{proposition}[Composition of conservative extensions]\label{prop:conservative-composition}
If $\mathbb{L}_\ell\preceq\mathbb{L}_m$ and $\mathbb{L}_m\preceq\mathbb{L}_n$, then $\mathbb{L}_\ell\preceq\mathbb{L}_n$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Compose the language embeddings and forgetful functors:
\[
e_{\ell,n}:=e_{m,n}\circ e_{\ell,m},
\qquad
U_{n,\ell}:=U_{m,\ell}\circ U_{n,m}.
\]
For every $M\in\Mod_n$, define
\[
\pi^M_{n,\ell}:=
\pi^{\,U_{n,m}(M)}_{m,\ell}\circ \pi^M_{n,m}.
\]
If $\varphi\in\mathrm{Form}_\ell$ and $p\in\St_n(M)$, then
\[
M,p\Vdash_n\varphi
\Longleftrightarrow
U_{n,m}(M),\pi^M_{n,m}(p)\Vdash_m\varphi
\Longleftrightarrow
U_{n,\ell}(M),\pi^M_{n,\ell}(p)\Vdash_\ell\varphi,
\]
by two applications of \Cref{def:conservative-extension}. This is exactly the required conservativity condition.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Finite-chain invariance]\label{cor:whole-chain-invariance}
If
\[
\mathbb{L}_0\preceq\mathbb{L}_1\preceq\cdots\preceq\mathbb{L}_n,
\]
then every formula belonging to any lower layer keeps the same forcing behavior when interpreted in any later layer and projected back down.
\leanverified{paper_conservative_extension_whole_chain_invariance_apal}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Apply \Cref{prop:conservative-composition} repeatedly along the chain.
\end{proof}

The semantic layers considered later fit into the chain
\[
\mathbb{L}_0
\xhookrightarrow{\iota}
\mathbb{L}_1
\preceq
\mathbb{L}_2
\preceq
\mathbb{L}_3
\preceq
\mathbb{L}_4,
\]
where the hooked arrow $\xhookrightarrow{\iota}$ denotes a singleton semantic embedding (\Cref{def:singleton-semantic-embedding}) and each $\preceq$ denotes a conservative extension in the sense of \Cref{def:conservative-extension}. Here $\mathbb{L}_0$ is the underlying pointwise semantics, $\mathbb{L}_1$ is the information-state forcing layer with reference sorts, $\mathbb{L}_2$ adds local-object predicates and site structure, $\mathbb{L}_3$ adds structural absence and obstruction data, and $\mathbb{L}_4$ adds refinement dynamics. By \Cref{cor:whole-chain-invariance}, the passage from $\mathbb{L}_1$ upward is cumulative. The first step is weaker: pointwise states embed faithfully as singleton information states, but arbitrary multi-realization states need not project back to single assignments.

The model classes of the higher layers are used below as generated
expansion classes.
Precisely, an expansion from $\mathbb L_m$ to $\mathbb L_n$ is admissible when
the newly adjoined symbols of $\Sigma_n\setminus\Sigma_m$ are interpreted by
the kind of data named for that layer, the reduct under $U_{n,m}$ is the given
lower model, and the lower atomic forcing clauses are read through that
reduct.  No existence theorem for gluing-sensitive data is being smuggled into
this convention: the convention says only that, once such data are explicitly
constructed or assumed and satisfy the stated typing axioms, the resulting
structure is a member of the relevant higher model class.

Accordingly, the realization theorems in later sections are internal
construction results under this generated expansion convention. They first
construct typed finite sites, presheaves, prestacks, stackifications, and
cohomology classes satisfying the displayed hypotheses, and then invoke the
expansion convention to regard those data as an \(\mathbb L_2\)- or
\(\mathbb L_3\)-model. They should not be read as closure theorems for an
independently specified semantic model class.

\begin{lemma}[Flat reduct induction for a displayed layer extension]\label{lem:layer-reduct-induction}
Let $1\le m<n\le 4$. Suppose that $\Sigma_n$ is obtained from $\Sigma_m$ by adjoining a disjoint block of new symbols, that $\Mod_n$ consists of expansions of $\Sigma_m$-reducts through the forgetful functor
\[
U_{n,m}:\Mod_n\to\Mod_m,
\]
and that the state projection
\[
\pi^M_{n,m}:\St_n(M)\to\St_m(U_{n,m}(M))
\]
forgets only the new layer coordinates. Assume also that every atomic forcing clause whose atom belongs to $\Sigma_m$ is interpreted through the reduct:
\[
M,p\Vdash_n \alpha
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
U_{n,m}(M),\pi^M_{n,m}(p)\Vdash_m \alpha
\]
for every $\Sigma_m$-atomic formula $\alpha$. Then the same equivalence holds for every formula $\varphi\in\mathrm{Form}_m$:
\[
M,p\Vdash_n \varphi
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
U_{n,m}(M),\pi^M_{n,m}(p)\Vdash_m \varphi.
\]
Consequently $\mathbb L_m\preceq\mathbb L_n$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For every retained formula \(\varphi\in\mathrm{Form}_m\), forcing is the
flat information-state satisfaction of \Cref{def:state-forcing}: it means
ordinary pointwise satisfaction of \(\varphi\) by every realization in the
state. The reduct functor \(U_{n,m}\) leaves all retained symbols, retained
sorts, and retained assignments unchanged, while the state projection
\(\pi^M_{n,m}\) forgets only the newly adjoined layer coordinates. Hence, for
every retained assignment \(\rho\) occurring in \(p\),
\[
M,\rho\models \varphi
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
U_{n,m}(M),\rho|_{\Sigma_m}\models \varphi .
\]
This is the ordinary first-order reduct induction on the formula
\(\varphi\): atomic formulas are the displayed hypothesis, Boolean
connectives are immediate, and quantifiers range over the same retained
sort-carriers on both sides. Taking the universal quantifier over the same
projected set of realizations in the state gives
\[
M,p\Vdash_n\varphi
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
U_{n,m}(M),\pi^M_{n,m}(p)\Vdash_m\varphi .
\]
Persistence and decidedness are not additional recursive forcing clauses in
this paper; persistence is a theorem about the flat forcing relation, and
decidedness is the abbreviation from \Cref{def:decidedness}. The displayed
equivalence is exactly \Cref{eq:conservative-extension}, so
\(\mathbb L_m\preceq\mathbb L_n\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Admissible-expansion model-class interface for the layer chain]\label{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}
Let $i\in\{1,2,3\}$ and let $M_i\in\Mod_i$. Suppose a block of new layer data
$D_{i+1}$ is supplied as follows:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item for $i=1$, $D_2$ may be any finite pullback-based local reference site
attached to the given admitted references, with covering families, refinement
relations, pullbacks, and any contravariant local object functors satisfying the
typing clauses of
\Cref{def:reference-classes,def:local-reference-frame,def:local-object-functor};
\item for $i=2$, $D_3$ consists of structural-absence predicates, visible
branches, realization prestacks, banded stackifications or banded
realization-stack extensions, component-gerbe data, and obstruction classes
satisfying the hypotheses explicitly stated where those data are used;
\item for $i=3$, $D_4$ consists of refinement-dynamic axes, update relations,
support data, and rewrite operations satisfying the typing clauses of
\Cref{def:multi-axis-dynamic-structure,def:value-preserving-rewrite}.
\end{enumerate}
Assume in each case that the interpretations of all symbols already belonging
to $\Sigma_i$ are exactly those of $M_i$, and that every $\Sigma_i$-atomic
forcing clause is evaluated through the reduct. Then the expansion
\[
M_{i+1}:=M_i[D_{i+1}]
\]
is an admissible model in $\Mod_{i+1}$, satisfies
\[
U_{i+1,i}(M_{i+1})=M_i,
\]
and, with the state projection forgetting only the newly added layer
coordinates, preserves forcing of every $\mathbb L_i$-formula:
\[
M_{i+1},p\Vdash_{i+1}\varphi
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M_i,\pi^{M_{i+1}}_{i+1,i}(p)\Vdash_i\varphi
\qquad(\varphi\in\mathrm{Form}_i).
\]
Thus arbitrary finite pullback subsites, arbitrary local object functors on
those subsites, and arbitrary supplied banded prestack or stack-extension data
are admissible only in the generated model class defined by this proposition,
once they satisfy the displayed typing and theorem-level hypotheses. This is a
model-class convention for the expanded layers, not a semantic existence or
closure theorem for an independently restricted class of models. The realization
theorems below therefore have two parts: an algebraic construction of typed
site, presheaf, prestack, stackification, and cohomology data, followed by the
admissible-expansion step which declares those well-typed data to be an
\(\mathbb L_{i+1}\)-model without changing lower-layer forcing.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By the expansion convention just fixed, $\Mod_{i+1}$ is exactly the class of
$\Sigma_{i+1}$-expansions of admissible $\Sigma_i$-models by the corresponding
well-typed layer data.  The hypotheses on $D_{i+1}$ therefore put
$M_i[D_{i+1}]$ in $\Mod_{i+1}$ and make its reduct under $U_{i+1,i}$ equal to
$M_i$.  The state projection is the projection specified for the adjacent
layer boundary and forgets only the new layer coordinates.  Since lower atomic
forcing clauses are assumed to be interpreted through the reduct, all
hypotheses of \Cref{lem:layer-reduct-induction} hold for the adjacent pair
$\mathbb L_i\preceq\mathbb L_{i+1}$.  Applying that lemma gives the displayed
forcing equivalence for every $\varphi\in\mathrm{Form}_i$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Concrete instantiation of the finite chain]\label{prop:chain-instantiation}
The chain
\[
\mathbb L_0\xhookrightarrow{\iota} \mathbb L_1\preceq \mathbb L_2\preceq \mathbb L_3\preceq \mathbb L_4
\]
is instantiated as follows.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=3em]
\item \textbf{$\mathbb L_0\hookrightarrow\mathbb L_1$ (pointwise states to information states: singleton semantic embedding).}
The language embedding
\[
e_{0,1}:\Sigma_0\hookrightarrow \Sigma_1
\]
is the inclusion of the underlying many-sorted symbols. In addition, $\Sigma_1$ contains the reference sorts $\mathsf{Ref}_s$ (one for each referenceable sort $s\in S_{\mathrm{ref}}$) together with their equality; no geometric predicates or site structure is imposed at this step. The forgetful functor
\[
U_{1,0}:\Mod_1\to\Mod_0
\]
forgets the information-state presentation and the reference carrier sets, remembering only the underlying many-sorted structure of $\Sigma_0$. A pointwise state $\rho$ in $\mathbb L_0$ is represented inside $\mathbb L_1$ by the singleton information state
\[
\iota_{0,1}^M(\rho):=(\Gamma,\{\rho\}).
\]
By \Cref{prop:singleton-conservativity},
\[
M,\iota_{0,1}^M(\rho)\Vdash_1\varphi
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
U_{1,0}(M),\rho\Vdash_0\varphi
\]
for every $\mathbb L_0$-formula $\varphi$. This is a singleton semantic embedding in the sense of \Cref{def:singleton-semantic-embedding}: it preserves forcing of $\mathbb L_0$-formulas on the canonical singleton image but does not provide a state projection from arbitrary $\mathbb L_1$-states back to $\mathbb L_0$-states.

\item \textbf{$\mathbb L_1\leadsto\mathbb L_2$ (information states to local objects).}
The embedding
\[
e_{1,2}:\Sigma_1\hookrightarrow \Sigma_2
\]
adjoins the admissibility predicates, the local-object predicates $\LocSec_s$, $\CompSec_s$, $\Sec_s$, the site-structure symbols (covers, refinement preorder, pullbacks), and the associated geometric notation. The reference sorts $\mathsf{Ref}_s$ and their equality are already present in $\Sigma_1$; the step from $\mathbb L_1$ to $\mathbb L_2$ equips references with the geometric structure needed to state local-to-global questions. The forgetful functor
\[
U_{2,1}:\Mod_2\to\Mod_1
\]
forgets the local reference sites, covers, and local object functors but retains the reference sorts and their carrier sets. The state projection
\[
\pi^M_{2,1}:\St_2(M)\to \St_1(U_{2,1}(M))
\]
is the identity on the underlying information state $(\Gamma_p,R_p)$. Since the forcing clauses for formulas already in $\mathrm{Form}_1$ never inspect the added local-object data, one has
\[
M,p\Vdash_2\varphi
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
U_{2,1}(M),\pi^M_{2,1}(p)\Vdash_1\varphi
\]
for every $\varphi\in\mathrm{Form}_1$.

\item \textbf{$\mathbb L_2\leadsto\mathbb L_3$ (local objects to obstruction data).}
The embedding
\[
e_{2,3}:\Sigma_2\hookrightarrow \Sigma_3
\]
adjoins the predicates and symbols used to talk about structural absence, visible branches, component gerbes, and obstruction classes. The forgetful functor
\[
U_{3,2}:\Mod_3\to\Mod_2
\]
drops the realization prestack, band, component-gerbe, and obstruction data. The state projection
\[
\pi^M_{3,2}:\St_3(M)\to \St_2(U_{3,2}(M))
\]
again leaves the underlying state unchanged. Formulas from $\mathrm{Form}_2$ are evaluated exactly as before, so
\[
M,p\Vdash_3\varphi
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
U_{3,2}(M),\pi^M_{3,2}(p)\Vdash_2\varphi
\]
for every $\varphi\in\mathrm{Form}_2$.

\item \textbf{$\mathbb L_3\leadsto\mathbb L_4$ (obstruction data to refinement dynamics).}
The embedding
\[
e_{3,4}:\Sigma_3\hookrightarrow \Sigma_4
\]
adjoins the update relations, axis indices, support notions, and rewrite language of the refinement layer. The forgetful functor
\[
U_{4,3}:\Mod_4\to\Mod_3
\]
forgets the dynamic axes, update structure, and rewrite operations. The state projection
\[
\pi^M_{4,3}:\St_4(M)\to \St_3(U_{4,3}(M))
\]
forgets only the new dynamic bookkeeping. Because the forcing clauses for $\mathrm{Form}_3$ do not depend on that added bookkeeping, one has
\[
M,p\Vdash_4\varphi
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
U_{4,3}(M),\pi^M_{4,3}(p)\Vdash_3\varphi
\]
for every $\varphi\in\mathrm{Form}_3$.
\end{enumerate}
The base boundary $\mathbb L_0\hookrightarrow\mathbb L_1$ is a singleton semantic embedding: pointwise states embed faithfully into information states, preserving the forcing of all $\mathbb L_0$-formulas on that canonical image. The three higher boundaries $\mathbb L_1\preceq\mathbb L_2\preceq\mathbb L_3\preceq\mathbb L_4$ are conservative extensions in the sense of \Cref{def:conservative-extension}, each with an explicit forgetful functor and state projection.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For the first boundary, the data displayed in \textup{(i)} give the language embedding
\[
e_{0,1}:\Sigma_0\hookrightarrow\Sigma_1,
\]
the forgetful functor $U_{1,0}$, and for each pointwise state $\rho$ the canonical singleton information state
\[
\iota_{0,1}^M(\rho)=(\Gamma,\{\rho\}).
\]
\Cref{prop:singleton-conservativity} gives, for every $\mathbb L_0$-formula $\varphi$,
\[
M,\iota_{0,1}^M(\rho)\Vdash_1\varphi
\Longleftrightarrow
U_{1,0}(M),\rho\Vdash_0\varphi.
\]
These are exactly the three pieces of structure and the preservation condition required in \Cref{def:singleton-semantic-embedding}. Notice that the verification uses only singleton information states; it does not assert a projection from arbitrary $\mathbb L_1$-states to pointwise states.

For $\mathbb L_1\preceq\mathbb L_2$, the displayed clauses fix the object language of the extension: $\Sigma_2$ is the disjoint expansion of $\Sigma_1$ by the local-object and site symbols listed in \textup{(ii)}, $\Mod_2$ is the class of $\Sigma_2$-expansions whose $\Sigma_1$-reduct is obtained by $U_{2,1}$, and $\pi^M_{2,1}$ is the identity on the underlying information state. Equivalently, this is the local-object instance of the admissible expansion interface of \Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}. Atomic $\Sigma_1$-formulas use only symbols retained by $U_{2,1}$. Thus \Cref{lem:layer-reduct-induction} gives, for every $\varphi\in\mathrm{Form}_1$,
\[
M,p\Vdash_2\varphi
\Longleftrightarrow
U_{2,1}(M),\pi^M_{2,1}(p)\Vdash_1\varphi,
\]
which is \Cref{def:conservative-extension}.

The same lemma applies to $\mathbb L_2\preceq\mathbb L_3$. Here $\Sigma_3$ is the disjoint expansion of $\Sigma_2$ by the realization-prestack, band, component-gerbe, structural-absence, and obstruction-class symbols listed in \textup{(iii)}. The admissible models are precisely the corresponding well-typed expansions singled out in \Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}. The functor $U_{3,2}$ forgets precisely those symbols, while $\pi^M_{3,2}$ leaves the underlying state unchanged. Atomic $\Sigma_2$-formulas contain no symbols for the forgotten gerbe or obstruction data, so \Cref{lem:layer-reduct-induction} gives
\[
M,p\Vdash_3\varphi
\Longleftrightarrow
U_{3,2}(M),\pi^M_{3,2}(p)\Vdash_2\varphi
\qquad(\varphi\in\mathrm{Form}_2).
\]
Thus $\mathbb L_2\preceq\mathbb L_3$.

Finally, $\Sigma_4$ is obtained from $\Sigma_3$ by adjoining only the refinement-dynamic axes, update structure, support notions, and rewrite operations listed in \textup{(iv)}. This is the refinement-dynamic instance of \Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}. The functor $U_{4,3}$ forgets exactly that dynamic structure, and $\pi^M_{4,3}$ forgets only the corresponding state bookkeeping. Atomic $\Sigma_3$-formulas are interpreted through the reduct, so \Cref{lem:layer-reduct-induction} yields
\[
M,p\Vdash_4\varphi
\Longleftrightarrow
U_{4,3}(M),\pi^M_{4,3}(p)\Vdash_3\varphi
\qquad(\varphi\in\mathrm{Form}_3).
\]
Consequently the three upper steps are conservative extensions, and the displayed finite chain has exactly the asserted form.
\end{proof}

Hence the genuinely conservative part of the chain begins at $\mathbb L_1$.

\section{Information states and forcing}\label{sec:information-states}

We now recall the forcing semantics on information states.

\subsection{States, refinement, and forcing}

Fix a many-sorted first-order signature
\[
\Sigma=(S,F,R).
\]
For a model $M$, write $D_s^M$ for the interpretation of a sort $s\in S$. A \emph{context} is a finite typed list
\[
\Gamma=(x_1:s_1,\dots,x_n:s_n),
\]
and its realization set in $M$ is
\[
\Val_M(\Gamma):=\{\rho\mid \rho(x_i)\in D_{s_i}^M\text{ for }1\le i\le n\}.
\]
If $\Gamma\preceq\Delta$, every $\sigma\in\Val_M(\Delta)$ restricts to an element $\sigma|_{\Gamma}\in\Val_M(\Gamma)$.

\begin{definition}[Information state]\label{def:information-state}
An \emph{information state} in $M$ is a pair
\[
p=(\Gamma_p,R_p)
\]
such that $\Gamma_p$ is a context and $\varnothing\neq R_p\subseteq \Val_M(\Gamma_p)$. The set of information states in $M$ is denoted by $\St(M)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Refinement]\label{def:refinement}
For states $p=(\Gamma_p,R_p)$ and $q=(\Gamma_q,R_q)$ in the same model, write $q\le p$ if
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $\Gamma_p\preceq\Gamma_q$,
\item every $\sigma\in R_q$ satisfies $\sigma|_{\Gamma_p}\in R_p$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

Refinement enlarges the context and may shrink the set of admissible realizations.

\begin{definition}[State forcing]\label{def:state-forcing}
Let $p=(\Gamma_p,R_p)\in\St(M)$ and let $\varphi$ be a formula well formed in context $\Gamma_p$. We define
\[
M,p\Vdash\varphi
\quad:\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\forall \rho\in R_p\;(M,\rho\models\varphi).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Flat forcing convention]\label{rem:flat-forcing-convention}
The word ``forcing'' in this paper means the flat, team-style universal
satisfaction relation of \Cref{def:state-forcing}. It is not
Kripke--Joyal forcing: there are no recursive forcing clauses for logical
connectives or quantifiers, and persistence below is a theorem about
universal satisfaction under refinement rather than a defining clause.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[State theory]\label{def:state-theory}
For $p\in\St(M)$, define
\[
\Th_M(p):=\{\varphi\mid M,p\Vdash\varphi\}.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Decidedness]\label{def:decidedness}
A formula $\varphi$ is \emph{decided} at $p$, written $M,p\Downarrow\varphi$, if
\[
M,p\Vdash\varphi
\qquad\text{or}\qquad
M,p\Vdash\neg\varphi.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Persistence of forcing]\label{prop:forcing-persistence}
