If $q\le p$ and $M,p\Vdash\varphi$, then $M,q\Vdash\varphi^\uparrow$, where $\varphi^\uparrow$ is the same formula viewed in the larger context $\Gamma_q$.
\leanverified{paper_information_state_forcing_persistence_seeds}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $\sigma\in R_q$. By \Cref{def:refinement}, its restriction $\sigma|_{\Gamma_p}$ lies in $R_p$. Since $M,p\Vdash\varphi$, we have $M,\sigma|_{\Gamma_p}\models\varphi$. The formula $\varphi$ mentions only variables from $\Gamma_p$, so the same assignment viewed in the larger context yields $M,\sigma\models\varphi^\uparrow$. As $\sigma$ was arbitrary, $M,q\Vdash\varphi^\uparrow$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Monotone growth of state theories]\label{cor:state-theory-monotonicity}
If $q\le p$, then $\Th_M(p)\subseteq \Th_M(q)$.
\leanverified{paper_information_state_theory_monotonicity_seeds}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Immediate from \Cref{prop:forcing-persistence}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Singleton conservativity]\label{prop:singleton-conservativity}
Let $\rho\in\Val_M(\Gamma)$ and put $p_\rho=(\Gamma,\{\rho\})$. Then for every $\Gamma$-formula $\varphi$,
\[
M,p_\rho\Vdash\varphi
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M,\rho\models\varphi.
\]
\leanverified{paper_information_state_singleton_conservativity_apal}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since $R_{p_\rho}=\{\rho\}$, the forcing clause in \Cref{def:state-forcing} reduces immediately to ordinary satisfaction at $\rho$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Lifted consequence}

\begin{definition}[State semantic consequence]\label{def:state-semantic-consequence}
For formulas $\Phi\cup\{\varphi\}$, define
\[
\Phi\models_{\mathrm{S}}\varphi
\]
to mean that for every model $M$ and every state $p\in\St(M)$,
\[
M,p\Vdash\Phi
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
M,p\Vdash\varphi.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Lifted soundness of pointwise reasoning]\label{prop:lifted-pointwise-soundness}
Let $\vdash_*$ be any derivability relation on formulas that is sound for ordinary many-sorted satisfaction. Then
\[
\Gamma\vdash_*\varphi
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\Gamma\models_{\mathrm{S}}\varphi.
\]
\leanverified{paper_information_state_lifted_pointwise_soundness_seeds}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Assume $\Gamma\vdash_*\varphi$, and let $M$ and $p=(\Gamma_p,R_p)$ be such that $M,p\Vdash\Gamma$. By definition, every realization $\rho\in R_p$ satisfies every formula in $\Gamma$ pointwise. Since $\vdash_*$ is sound for ordinary satisfaction, each such $\rho$ also satisfies $\varphi$. Therefore $M,p\Vdash\varphi$. As $M$ and $p$ were arbitrary, $\Gamma\models_{\mathrm{S}}\varphi$.
\end{proof}


 succeeded in 159ms:
\section{Homological visibility and finite-nerve-class quotients}
\label{sec:homological-visibility}

We now fix one visible branch together with a finite nerve presentation. The quotient attached to a chosen cocycle still depends on that presentation, so in order to isolate the part determined by the cohomology class itself we pass to the homological evaluation map. Throughout the section we work with one fixed finite cover and the comparison maps coming from the identification of \v{C}ech cochains with simplicial cochains on its finite nerve. The resulting hidden subgroup and visible quotient are finite-nerve-class invariants only relative to the homology-equivalent changes of presentation singled out below.

We also assume that the chosen cover $\mathcal U$ trivializes the band, so that the coefficients may be viewed as a constant finite abelian group $A$ on the nerve. The case of nontrivial monodromy, which would require local coefficients, is not considered here.

\subsection{Finite nerve presentations}

\begin{definition}[Finite nerve presentation of the obstruction class]\label{def:finite-nerve-presentation}
Fix an admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$, and assume conditions \textup{(i)}--\textup{(iv)} of \Cref{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}, with the ordered \v{C}ech convention of \Cref{def:ordered-cech-convention}. A \emph{finite nerve presentation} of the branch obstruction class consists of:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item a finite covering family
\[
\mathcal U=\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I},
\qquad
a=[r]_p,
\]
whose \v{C}ech nerve $N(\mathcal U)$ is finite;
\item a trivialization of the band on $\mathcal U$, so that the band may be viewed as a constant coefficient finite abelian group $A$ on $N(\mathcal U)$;
\item the induced finite-nerve class
\[
\omega\in H^2(N(\mathcal U),A),
\]
obtained by identifying \v{C}ech cochains on $\mathcal U$ with simplicial cochains on the finite nerve, transporting the branch obstruction cocycle through that identification, and writing
\[
\omega:=\omega_v
\]
for the chosen visible branch. When the class is also viewed as a derived
$H^2$-class, the presentation includes either the
\v{C}ech--derived comparison package of
\Cref{def:cech-derived-comparison-package} or the finite good-cover Leray
comparison of \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}. Without one of
these comparison inputs, \(\omega\) is only the selected finite-nerve
\v{C}ech/simplicial class.
\end{enumerate}
Write
\[
\eta_A:H^2(N(\mathcal U),A)\longrightarrow \mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A)
\]
for the map in the universal coefficient exact sequence
\[
0\to \operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A)\to H^2(N(\mathcal U),A)\xrightarrow{\eta_A}\mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A)\to 0
\]
\cite[Sec.~3.1]{Hatcher2002}, and define the \emph{homological evaluation map}
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}:=\eta_A(\omega).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Finite nerve comparison groupoid]\label{def:finite-nerve-comparison-relation}
For finite nerve presentations of a fixed branch obstruction class, an
\emph{elementary comparison} is one of the three operations listed in
\Cref{prop:invariance-visible-quotients}: homology-equivalent refinement,
cohomologous cocycle replacement, or re-trivialization of the constant band.
The \emph{finite nerve comparison groupoid} has these presentations as
objects and finite zigzags of elementary comparisons and formal inverses as
morphisms, with composition given by concatenation of zigzags. We say that two
presentations are comparison-equivalent when they lie in the same connected
component of this groupoid. Thus the comparison data carried by a finite
zigzag are part of the pseudofunctorial assignment; no path independence
between distinct zigzags is asserted unless an additional coherence
hypothesis is imposed.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Pseudofunctorial assignment of $K_\omega$ and $A_{\mathrm{vis}}^\omega$]\label{prop:invariance-visible-quotients}
Let $(\mathcal U,A,\omega)$ and $(\mathcal U',A',\omega')$ be finite nerve presentations of the same branch obstruction class. Suppose first that the two presentations differ by one of the following elementary comparisons:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item refinement of the cover by a simplicial map
\[
f:N(\mathcal U')\to N(\mathcal U)
\]
from the refined nerve to the original nerve, such that
\[
\omega'=f^*\omega
\]
and
\[
f_*:H_2(N(\mathcal U'),\mathbb Z)\xrightarrow{\sim} H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)
\]
is an isomorphism, with the coefficient group fixed;
\item replacement of the \v{C}ech cocycle by a cohomologous cocycle in $H^2(N(\mathcal U),A)$;
\item re-trivialization of the band by an isomorphism of coefficient groups
\[
\theta:A\xrightarrow{\sim} A',
\]
so that
\[
\omega'=\theta_*\omega.
\]
\end{enumerate}
Then there exists a specified coefficient comparison isomorphism $\phi:A\to A'$---the identity in \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)}, and $\theta$ in \textup{(iii)}---such that
\[
\phi\bigl(K_{\omega}\bigr)=K_{\omega'}
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\bar\phi:A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\omega}=A/K_\omega\xrightarrow{\sim}A'/K_{\omega'}=A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\omega'}
\]
is the induced quotient isomorphism. In particular, both $K_{\omega}$ and $A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\omega}$ are transported by the three elementary comparison operations listed above, with the refinement direction fixed by the displayed pullback map.

More generally, if the two presentations are joined by a morphism $\Gamma$ in
the finite nerve comparison groupoid of
\Cref{def:finite-nerve-comparison-relation}, then the chosen finite comparison
zigzag $\Gamma$ determines, by composition of the elementary coefficient
comparisons and their inverses, an isomorphism
\[
\phi_\Gamma:A\xrightarrow{\sim} A'
\]
such that
\[
\phi_\Gamma(K_\omega)=K_{\omega'}
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
A/K_\omega\xrightarrow{\sim}A'/K_{\omega'}
\]
is the corresponding composite quotient isomorphism. Hence
$(\mathcal U,A,\omega)\mapsto (K_\omega\le A)$ and
$(\mathcal U,A,\omega)\mapsto A_{\mathrm{vis}}^\omega$ define a pseudofunctorial assignment over the
finite nerve comparison groupoid. They are presentation-object canonical:
once a finite nerve, coefficient trivialization, and comparison package have
been fixed, the subgroup and quotient are determined by that object. Along a
chosen comparison zigzag they are transported functorially by the displayed
composite isomorphism. The statement does not assert path independence, and
it does not identify the isomorphisms attached to two different zigzags with
the same endpoints.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Typical refinements]\label{rem:refinement-iso}
The refinements used later are simplicial homotopy equivalences on the chosen nerves---for example, barycentric subdivisions and the standard refinements attached to good covers---and therefore satisfy the hypothesis of case \textup{(i)} of \Cref{prop:invariance-visible-quotients}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Necessity of homology equivalences and chosen paths]\label{rem:homology-equality}
The equalities in \Cref{prop:invariance-visible-quotients} rely on the assumption that the refinement map induces an isomorphism on $H_2$. Without that hypothesis, for a refinement \(f:N(\mathcal U')\to N(\mathcal U)\) with \(\omega'=f^*\omega\), naturality gives
\[
K_{\omega'}=\operatorname{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}\circ f_*)\subseteq K_{\omega}.
\]
Hence one obtains \(K_{\omega'}\subseteq K_{\omega}\), and therefore a comparison map
\[
A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\omega'}=A/K_{\omega'}\twoheadrightarrow
A/K_{\omega}=A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\omega},
\]
but not equality. The term ``finite-nerve-class visible quotient'' is therefore reserved for refinements and presentations that are homology-equivalent in this precise sense.

For presentations connected by several finite zigzags, the comparison
isomorphism is part of the finite nerve comparison groupoid. A different
zigzag may compose the re-trivialization automorphisms of the coefficient band
in a different way, and the paper does not quotient by that possible holonomy.
Thus the assignment is pseudofunctorial over the finite nerve comparison groupoid rather than a path-independent quotient
on the set of comparison-equivalence classes.
\end{remark}

\begin{proof}
Case \textup{(ii)} is immediate because cohomologous cocycles define the same element of $H^2(N(\mathcal U),A)$ and hence have the same image under $\eta_A$. Here $\phi=\mathrm{id}_A$, so the quotient map is the identity on $A/K_\omega$.

In case \textup{(i)}, the assumption on $f$ gives an isomorphism on $H_2$. Since $\omega'=f^*(\omega)$ and the universal coefficient map is natural, one has
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega'}=\eta_A\bigl(f^*(\omega)\bigr)=\eta_A(\omega)\circ f_*,
\]
where $f_*:H_2(N(\mathcal U'),\mathbb Z)\to H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)$ is induced on homology and is an isomorphism by hypothesis. Hence $\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega'})=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_\omega)$, so $K_{\omega'}=K_{\omega}$ and $A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\omega'}\cong A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\omega}$.
The comparison is canonical because the refinement operation supplies the direction of $f$ and hence the specified isomorphism $f_*$; replacing $f_*$ by its inverse would compare the evaluation maps in the wrong direction. Thus the subgroup equality occurs inside the same coefficient group $A$, and the quotient isomorphism is induced by $\phi=\mathrm{id}_A$.

In case \textup{(iii)}, the coefficient isomorphism $\theta$ identifies the two cohomology groups, and $\omega'=\theta_*(\omega)$. Naturality gives the coefficient-isomorphism square
\[
\begin{array}{ccc}
H^2(N(\mathcal U),A) & \xrightarrow{\eta_A} &
\mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A)\\
\theta_*\downarrow\phantom{\theta_*} && \phantom{\theta\circ(-)}\downarrow\theta\circ(-)\\
H^2(N(\mathcal U),A') & \xrightarrow{\eta_{A'}} &
\mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A'),
\end{array}
\]
and hence
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega'}=\eta_{A'}(\omega')=\eta_{A'}\bigl(\theta_*(\omega)\bigr)=\theta\circ \eta_A(\omega)=\theta\circ \mathrm{ev}_\omega.
\]
Therefore $K_{\omega'}=\theta(K_{\omega})$, and $\theta$ induces an isomorphism between $A_{\mathrm{vis}}^\omega$ and $A_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\omega'}$.

In all three cases the induced quotient map is well defined because the chosen coefficient comparison $\phi$ sends $K_\omega$ onto $K_{\omega'}$. Its inverse is induced by the inverse coefficient comparison: the identity in cases \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)}, and $\theta^{-1}$ in case \textup{(iii)}. This proves functoriality of the specified elementary comparison and invariance of the quotient for that elementary morphism.

For a finite zigzag in the comparison groupoid, apply the elementary
case at each step. If a step is traversed in the opposite direction, use the
inverse coefficient and quotient isomorphisms just constructed. Composition of
the finitely many subgroup identifications gives
\[
\phi_\Gamma(K_\omega)=K_{\omega'},
\]
and the universal property of quotients gives the displayed quotient
isomorphism. Concatenating two zigzags concatenates the corresponding composite
coefficient maps, so these assignments respect identity zigzags and composition
of zigzags. Thus finite composites of the three comparison operations preserve
the finite-nerve-class hidden subgroup and visible quotient in the stated
groupoid-indexed sense.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finite nerve comparison isomorphism]\label{prop:finite-nerve-comparison}
Work in one finite nerve presentation
$(\mathcal U,A,\omega)$ of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}; in
particular, the band has been trivialized on $\mathcal U$ and is being
viewed as the constant finite abelian group $A$ on the selected finite
nerve. Use the total order on the finite index set $I$ already fixed in
\Cref{def:ordered-cech-convention} for
\[
\mathcal U=\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I}.
\]
Use the ordered \v{C}ech convention in which $\check C^n(\mathcal U,A)$
is indexed by tuples
\[
i_0<\cdots<i_n
\]
with nonempty overlap, and use the corresponding ordered oriented
simplices of $N(\mathcal U)$ for simplicial cochains. For each $n\ge 0$,
each nonempty ordered $(n{+}1)$-fold overlap $a_{i_0\cdots i_n}$
determines the oriented $n$-simplex $(i_0,\dots,i_n)$ of the selected
nerve, and the assignment
\[
\Theta_{\mathcal U}^n:\check C^n(\mathcal U,A)\to C^n(N(\mathcal U),A),\qquad
(\alpha_{i_0\cdots i_n})\longmapsto\bigl((i_0,\dots,i_n)\mapsto \alpha_{i_0\cdots i_n}\bigr)
\]
is an isomorphism of cochain groups. Under this correspondence,
\[
\delta\leftrightarrow d,\qquad
B^n\leftrightarrow B^n,\qquad
Z^n\leftrightarrow Z^n.
\]
Hence the \v{C}ech cohomology of this selected ordered finite nerve
presentation with constant coefficients $A$ is identified with the
simplicial cohomology of $N(\mathcal U)$ with coefficients in $A$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The hypotheses are part of the finite nerve presentation: the cover is
finite, the coefficient sheaf has been trivialized to the constant group
$A$ on this cover, and the comparison is being made on the chosen finite
nerve rather than on an arbitrary refinement system. With the fixed order
on $I$, the $n$-cochains on $\mathcal U$ are families of coefficients
indexed by nonempty ordered $(n{+}1)$-fold overlaps
$a_{i_0\cdots i_n}$ with $i_0<\cdots<i_n$, while the simplicial
$n$-cochains on $N(\mathcal U)$ are coefficient functions on the
corresponding ordered oriented $n$-simplices $(i_0,\dots,i_n)$. By
definition of the selected nerve, these two index sets agree, so
$\Theta_{\mathcal U}^n$ is a bijection.

The \v{C}ech coboundary of $(\alpha_{i_0\cdots i_n})$ is the alternating
sum obtained by omitting the $k$th ordered index, and the simplicial
coboundary of $\Theta_{\mathcal U}^n(\alpha)$ uses the same ordered face
map with the same sign $(-1)^k$. Since the coefficients are constant
under the chosen trivialization, the restriction maps on coefficients are
the identity maps of $A$. Because the face maps of $N(\mathcal U)$ are
induced by omitting one ordered index, the two alternating sums coincide
term by term. Hence
\[
\Theta_{\mathcal U}^{n+1}(\delta\alpha)=d(\Theta_{\mathcal U}^n(\alpha)).
\]
Therefore $\Theta_{\mathcal U}^\bullet$ is an isomorphism of cochain
complexes, and it induces the stated identifications on cocycles,
coboundaries, and cohomology. This is only the finite ordered
constant-coefficient comparison used in \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation};
promotion of a filtered \v{C}ech system to derived cohomology remains the
separate \textup{(H4)} assumption of \Cref{rem:cech-vs-derived}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Strict versus finite-nerve-class hidden subgroups]\label{prop:strict-vs-intrinsic-hidden-subgroups}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}, let $\alpha\in Z^2(N(\mathcal U),A)$ be a simplicial cocycle representing $\omega$, and let
\[
\widetilde\alpha:C_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)\to A
\]
be the chain-level evaluation map obtained by linear extension from the oriented $2$-simplices. Then
\[
H_\alpha=\operatorname{Im}(\widetilde\alpha)
\]
and
\[
K_{\omega}:=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}).
\]
Thus $H_\alpha$ records presentation-level chain visibility: it is generated by the values of the chosen cocycle on all $2$-chains. By contrast, $K_{\omega}$ records cycle-level class visibility: it is the image that survives after passage to $2$-homology. In particular,
\[
K_{\omega}\subseteq H_\alpha.
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The image of $\widetilde\alpha$ is the subgroup generated by the values of $\alpha$ on the oriented $2$-simplices, equivalently by the values of the original \v{C}ech cocycle on the nonempty triple overlaps of $\mathcal U$. This is exactly the strict subgroup attached to the chosen presentation. The second equality is the definition of $K_\omega$. Since $H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)$ is represented by $2$-cycles inside $C_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)$, every homological value counted by $\mathrm{ev}_\omega$ is, a fortiori, a chain-level value of $\widetilde\alpha$, so $K_\omega\subseteq H_\alpha$.
\end{proof}



 succeeded in 159ms:

\subsection{Visible quotients and strictification}

We now fix one visible branch and a finite obstruction presentation. The quotient $A/H_\alpha$ records what remains visible at the level of the chosen cocycle; the finite-nerve-class quotient considered in the next section removes the dependence on the representative within the specified finite nerve comparison groupoid.

\begin{definition}[Ordered \v{C}ech convention]\label{def:ordered-cech-convention}
For every finite cover
\[
\mathcal U=\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
\]
used in a finite obstruction presentation, fix a total order on $I$. The
group $\check C^n(\mathcal U,A)$ is indexed by strictly increasing
nondegenerate tuples
\[
i_0<\cdots<i_n
\]
whose overlap $a_{i_0\cdots i_n}$ is nonempty. If a value is written on an
arbitrary ordered tuple, it denotes the alternating extension from the
increasing tuple: permuting pairwise distinct indices multiplies the value
by the sign of the permutation, and a tuple with a repeated index has value
$0$. In degree $2$, the cocycle identity on every ordered nondegenerate
quadruple is
\[
\alpha_{jkl}-\alpha_{ikl}+\alpha_{ijl}-\alpha_{ijk}=0.
\]
Thus reversing the orientation of a nondegenerate triple replaces
$\alpha_{ijk}$ by $-\alpha_{ijk}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Finite obstruction presentation]\label{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}
Fix an admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$, and assume:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item the hypotheses of \Cref{def:cech-gluing-obstruction} hold;
\item a visible branch
\[
v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)
\]
has been chosen;
\item the associated branch gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ is banded by a finite abelian group $A$,
\item a finite covering family
\[
\mathcal U=\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I},
\qquad
a=[r]_p,
\]
has been chosen together with a \v{C}ech $2$-cocycle
\[
\alpha=(\alpha_{ijk})\in Z^2(\mathcal U,A)
\]
representing the branch obstruction class $\omega_v$, written in the ordered
convention of \Cref{def:ordered-cech-convention}.
\end{enumerate}
Define the \emph{obstruction subgroup}
\[
H_\alpha:=\langle \alpha_{ijk}\mid i<j<k\text{ and }a_{ijk}\neq\varnothing\rangle\le A,
\]
and the associated \emph{visible quotient}
\[
A_{\mathrm{vis}}:=A/H_\alpha.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Orientation independence of the generated subgroup]\label{rem:strict-subgroup-orientation}
The definition of $H_\alpha$ is independent of whether one generates from
increasing triples or from all oriented nondegenerate triples. Indeed, any
orientation change replaces a generator $\alpha_{ijk}$ by
$-\alpha_{ijk}$, and the subgroup generated by a subset of an abelian group
is unchanged after replacing any generator by its negative. Degenerate
triples contribute $0$ by \Cref{def:ordered-cech-convention}.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Algebraic quotient generated by a chosen obstruction cocycle]\label{thm:initial-visible-quotient}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}, let
\[
\pi:A\twoheadrightarrow A_{\mathrm{vis}}
\]
be the canonical quotient map. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item the pushed-forward cocycle $\pi_*(\alpha)$ is pointwise zero on every nonempty triple overlap;
\item if $\varphi:A\to B$ is any homomorphism into an abelian group $B$ such that $\varphi(\alpha_{ijk})=0$ for every ordered nondegenerate triple overlap, then there exists a unique homomorphism
\[
\bar\varphi:A_{\mathrm{vis}}\to B
\]
with $\varphi=\bar\varphi\circ\pi$.
\end{enumerate}
Hence \(A_{\mathrm{vis}}\) is the elementary quotient of \(A\) by the
subgroup generated by the displayed cocycle values; equivalently, it is the
initial coefficient quotient through which this chosen obstruction
presentation becomes strictly trivial.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By construction and \Cref{rem:strict-subgroup-orientation}, every oriented
cocycle value $\alpha_{ijk}$ lies in the subgroup $H_\alpha$. Therefore
$\pi(\alpha_{ijk})=0$ for every ordered nondegenerate triple overlap, which
proves (i).

For (ii), the hypothesis $\varphi(\alpha_{ijk})=0$ on every ordered
nondegenerate triple overlap implies that every generator of $H_\alpha$
lies in $\ker\varphi$. Hence $H_\alpha\subseteq\ker\varphi$. By the
universal property of the quotient by $H_\alpha$, there exists a unique
homomorphism $\bar\varphi:A/H_\alpha\to B$ satisfying
$\varphi=\bar\varphi\circ\pi$.
\end{proof}

The strict quotient $A/H_\alpha$ depends on the chosen cocycle representative $\alpha$, not only on the cohomology class $\omega_v$. The next section removes this dependence: the homological evaluation map $\mathrm{ev}_\omega$ is defined from the class itself, and its cokernel $A_{\mathrm{vis}}^\omega$ is canonical within the homology-equivalent comparison class isolated there. The comparison between the two quotients is given by \Cref{cor:strict-to-intrinsic-visible} and \Cref{prop:chain-vs-homological-visibility}.

\begin{definition}[Coboundary action and presentation-invariant character channels]\label{def:gauge-invariance-character}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}, the \v{C}ech $1$-cochain group $C^1(\mathcal U,A)$ acts on cocycles by coboundary translation: a cochain
\[
h=(h_{ij})\in C^1(\mathcal U,A)
\]
sends a cocycle $\alpha$ to the cohomologous cocycle
\[
(h\cdot\alpha)_{ijk}:=\alpha_{ijk}+(\delta h)_{ijk}.
\]
A character $\chi\in\widehat A$ is called \emph{presentation-invariant} for the chosen obstruction presentation if it kills the obstruction subgroup $H_\alpha$, equivalently if it factors through the quotient
\[
\pi:A\twoheadrightarrow A_{\mathrm{vis}}=A/H_\alpha.
\]
In that case the pushed-forward class $\chi_*[\alpha]$ depends only on the visible quotient and not on the chosen local trivializations.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Character channels and the presentation-level visible quotient]\label{thm:character-definability-barrier}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}, let
\[
\widehat A:=\mathrm{Hom}(A,\mathbb T),
\]
where $\mathbb T\subseteq\mathbb C^\times$ is the unit circle, and define
\[
H_\alpha^\perp:=\{\chi\in\widehat A\mid \chi(h)=1\text{ for every }h\in H_\alpha\}.
\]
Call a character $\chi\in\widehat A$ \emph{globally admissible} for the obstruction presentation if
\[
\chi(\alpha_{ijk})=1
\]
for every nonempty triple overlap of $\mathcal U$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item a character $\chi\in\widehat A$ is globally admissible if and only if $\chi\in H_\alpha^\perp$;
\item the quotient map $\pi:A\twoheadrightarrow A_{\mathrm{vis}}$ induces a natural isomorphism
\[
\widehat{A_{\mathrm{vis}}}\cong H_\alpha^\perp;
\]
\item consequently, every globally admissible character channel factors uniquely through $A_{\mathrm{vis}}$, and a character is presentation-invariant for the chosen obstruction presentation if and only if it lies in $H_\alpha^\perp$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Since $H_\alpha$ is generated by the cocycle values $\alpha_{ijk}$, a character $\chi$ is trivial on $H_\alpha$ if and only if it is trivial on each generator $\alpha_{ijk}$. This proves (i).

For (ii), the quotient map $\pi$ induces by precomposition an injective homomorphism
\[
\pi^*:\widehat{A_{\mathrm{vis}}}\hookrightarrow \widehat A.
\]
Its image consists exactly of the characters on $A$ that are trivial on $\ker\pi=H_\alpha$, namely $H_\alpha^\perp$. Thus $\pi^*$ identifies $\widehat{A_{\mathrm{vis}}}$ with $H_\alpha^\perp$.

Clause \textup{(iii)} is just \Cref{def:gauge-invariance-character} rewritten using \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The visible quotient is exactly the character-visible quotient]\label{cor:visible-quotient-character-separation}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}, for elements $x,y\in A$ the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $\pi(x)=\pi(y)$ in $A_{\mathrm{vis}}$;
\item $\chi(x)=\chi(y)$ for every globally admissible character $\chi\in\widehat A$.
\end{enumerate}
Equivalently, $A_{\mathrm{vis}}$ is the quotient of $A$ obtained by identifying exactly those elements that cannot be separated by globally admissible character channels.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Assume first that $\pi(x)=\pi(y)$. By \Cref{thm:character-definability-barrier}(ii), every globally admissible character $\chi$ factors as
\[
\chi=\bar\chi\circ\pi
\]
for a unique character $\bar\chi\in\widehat{A_{\mathrm{vis}}}$. Hence
\[
\chi(x)=\bar\chi(\pi(x))=\bar\chi(\pi(y))=\chi(y).
\]

Conversely, suppose $\pi(x)\neq\pi(y)$. Then
\[
g:=\pi(x)-\pi(y)\neq 0
\]
in the finite abelian group $A_{\mathrm{vis}}$. By the structure theorem for finite abelian groups \cite{DummitFoote2004}, there is an isomorphism
\[
A_{\mathrm{vis}}\cong \mathbb Z/n_1\mathbb Z\oplus\cdots\oplus \mathbb Z/n_t\mathbb Z
\]
under which the image of $g$ has some nonzero coordinate, say the $k$th. The coordinate character
\[
\psi(z_1,\dots,z_t):=\exp(2\pi i z_k/n_k)
\]
therefore satisfies $\psi(g)\neq 1$. By \Cref{thm:character-definability-barrier}(ii), the character
\[
\chi:=\psi\circ\pi
\]
is globally admissible. Since
\[
\chi(x)\chi(y)^{-1}=\psi(\pi(x)-\pi(y))=\psi(g)\neq 1,
\]
we have $\chi(x)\neq\chi(y)$, contradicting (ii). Thus $\pi(x)=\pi(y)$.
\end{proof}
\leanverified{paper_gluing_failure_visible_quotient_character_separation}

\begin{definition}[Auxiliary strictification code]\label{def:auxiliary-strictification-code}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}, an \emph{auxiliary strictification code} consists of a finite set $R$ and an injective map
\[
\iota:A\hookrightarrow A_{\mathrm{vis}}\times R
\]
such that the first projection recovers the canonical quotient:
\[
\mathrm{pr}_1\circ\iota=\pi.
\]
Equivalently, the visible quotient is kept fixed, while the auxiliary register $R$ resolves the hidden information inside the fibers of $\pi$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Fiber-size cost of strictification]\label{thm:minimal-information-cost-of-strictification}
In the setting of \Cref{def:auxiliary-strictification-code}, every auxiliary strictification code satisfies
\[
|R|\ge |H_\alpha|.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\log_2 |R|\ge \log_2 |H_\alpha|.
\]
Moreover, this lower bound is sharp.
\leanverified{paper_gluing_failure_minimal_information_cost_of_strictification}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $\bar a\in A_{\mathrm{vis}}$. The fiber $\pi^{-1}(\bar a)$ is a coset of $\ker\pi=H_\alpha$, hence has cardinality $|H_\alpha|$. Since $\iota$ is injective and $\mathrm{pr}_1\circ\iota=\pi$, the restriction of $\iota$ to $\pi^{-1}(\bar a)$ lands inside the slice $\{\bar a\}\times R$ and remains injective. Therefore
\[
|H_\alpha|=|\pi^{-1}(\bar a)|\le |\{\bar a\}\times R|=|R|.
\]
This proves the lower bound.

To see that it is sharp, take $R:=H_\alpha$. Choose a set-theoretic section $s:A_{\mathrm{vis}}\to A$ of $\pi$. For each $x\in A$, the difference
\[
h(x):=x-s(\pi(x))
\]
lies in $H_\alpha$. Define
\[
\iota(x):=(\pi(x),h(x))\in A_{\mathrm{vis}}\times H_\alpha.
\]
Then $\mathrm{pr}_1\circ\iota=\pi$. If $\iota(x)=\iota(y)$, then $\pi(x)=\pi(y)$ and $h(x)=h(y)$, hence
\[
x=s(\pi(x))+h(x)=s(\pi(y))+h(y)=y.
\]
Thus $\iota$ is injective and $|R|=|H_\alpha|$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Conservative recovery requires hidden-state budget]\label{cor:conservative-recovery-budget}
For a finite obstruction presentation, no conservative enrichment that leaves the visible quotient unchanged can restore full \(A\)-resolution with fewer than \(\log_2|H_\alpha|\) bits of auxiliary local state. This is the elementary fiber-counting consequence of quotienting by the obstruction subgroup \(H_\alpha\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Any such enrichment induces an auxiliary strictification code in the sense of \Cref{def:auxiliary-strictification-code}. Apply \Cref{thm:minimal-information-cost-of-strictification}.
\end{proof}
\leanverified{paper_conservative_extension_conservative_recovery_budget}

\subsection{A worked finite example}

\begin{example}[A one-bit hidden obstruction]\label{ex:one-bit-hidden-obstruction}
Assume an admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ together with a three-member cover
\[
\mathcal U=\{a_0,a_1,a_2\to a\},
\qquad
a=[r]_p,
\]
and let the band be the additive group
\[
A=\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z.
\]
Suppose the chosen obstruction presentation is represented by a cocycle whose only nonzero value on a nonempty triple overlap is
\[
\alpha_{012}=2\in \mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z.
\]
Then
\[
H_\alpha=\langle 2\rangle=\{0,2\},
\qquad
A_{\mathrm{vis}}=A/H_\alpha\cong \mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z.
\]

Thus the visible quotient identifies $0$ with $2$ and identifies $1$ with $3$. By \Cref{thm:initial-visible-quotient}, this is the initial coefficient quotient through which the chosen obstruction presentation becomes trivial.

For $m\in\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z$, define a character
\[
\chi_m(x):=\exp(2\pi i mx/4).
\]
Then
\[
\chi_m(\alpha_{012})=\chi_m(2)=(-1)^m,
\]
so \Cref{thm:character-definability-barrier} implies that the globally admissible characters are exactly
\[
\chi_0
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\chi_2.
\]
Hence the only surviving linear detection is parity: $\chi_2$ distinguishes the two cosets of $H_\alpha$, while the phase-sensitive channels $\chi_1$ and $\chi_3$ do not factor through the visible quotient. By \Cref{cor:visible-quotient-character-separation}, the visible quotient is therefore exactly the quotient detected by all globally admissible character channels.

Finally, \Cref{thm:minimal-information-cost-of-strictification} yields
\[
|R|\ge |H_\alpha|=2,
\]
so at least one auxiliary bit is required to recover the full $\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z$-resolution while keeping the visible quotient fixed. This lower bound is attained by the code
\[
0\mapsto (\overline{0},0),\qquad
2\mapsto (\overline{0},1),\qquad
1\mapsto (\overline{1},0),\qquad
3\mapsto (\overline{1},1),
\]
where $\overline{0},\overline{1}\in A_{\mathrm{vis}}\cong \mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z$ and the second component is a one-bit register.
\end{example}


 succeeded in 208ms:
\section{Local objects and gluing failure}\label{sec:null-decomposition}

We study the local-object problem attached to an admitted reference, extending the meet-semilattice formulation of the companion paper \cite{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure} to the pullback-based sites used here. The basic distinction is between local sectionability, compatible local sectionability, and global sectionability.

The following standing hypothesis will be used throughout the section and cited explicitly when needed.

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(RC)},nosep,leftmargin=3em]
\item\label{hyp:refinement-conservation} \emph{Refinement conservation.} If $q\le p$, then admitted references, covers, and compatible local families at $q$ restrict functorially to those at $p$, and a visible value class already uniquely determined at $p$ is not split by further refinement.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{remark}[Relation to the companion paper]
The companion paper \cite{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure} develops the same local-object and visible-value framework on meet-semilattice sites. In this section those constructions are recast on the pullback-based sites of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame}, so the sheafification criterion, split realization prestacks, stackification comparison, terminal-fibre conservativity, and visible value classes are all stated without assuming that overlaps arise from semilattice meets.
\end{remark}

\subsection{References and local objects}

Fix a family of referenceable sorts
\[
S_{\mathrm{ref}}\subseteq S.
\]
For each $s\in S_{\mathrm{ref}}$, the language contains a reference sort $\mathsf{Ref}_s$ and an admissibility predicate $\Adm_s(\cdot)$.
Layer placement is fixed as follows. The sort \(\mathsf{Ref}_s\) and its
equality belong to \(\Sigma_1\); the admissibility predicate
\(\Adm_s\), together with \(\LocSec_s,\CompSec_s,\Sec_s\) and the
site-structure symbols, belongs to \(\Sigma_2\setminus\Sigma_1\). Thus a
lower-fragment formula may quantify over references but cannot define or
mention admission unless the statement explicitly restricts attention to the
admitted subdomain.

\begin{definition}[Reference classes]\label{def:reference-classes}
For each state $p$ and each referenceable sort $s$, fix a set
\[
\mathcal R_s(p)
\]
and a surjective assignment
\[
r\longmapsto [r]_p\in \mathcal R_s(p)
\]
defined for the admitted references $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at $p$. The element $[r]_p$ is called the \emph{reference class} of $r$ at $p$. The later local reference site is built on these classes; no further quotient structure is needed here.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Local reference site]\label{def:local-reference-frame}
For a state $p$ and a referenceable sort $s$, a \emph{local reference site} consists of the following data.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item A reflexive, transitive refinement relation $\sqsubseteq_{p,s}$ on $\mathcal R_s(p)$.
\item The associated small thin category $\mathcal B_{p,s}$ whose objects are the elements of $\mathcal R_s(p)$ and in which there is a unique morphism
\[
b\longrightarrow a
\]
if and only if $b\sqsubseteq_{p,s} a$.
\item A Grothendieck pretopology $\Cov_{p,s}$ on $\mathcal B_{p,s}$ satisfying the identity, pullback-stability, and transitivity axioms.
\item The standing pullback hypothesis that, for every covering family
\[
\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I}\in \Cov_{p,s}(a)
\]
and every pair $(i,j)$, the fibre product
\[
a_i\times_a a_j
\]
exists in $\mathcal B_{p,s}$.
\end{enumerate}
The fibre product clause is a condition on the site, not on a later
\v{C}ech cochain display: if two members of a covering family are
disjoint in a concrete presentation, their pullback must still be
represented by an object of the site, typically a strict initial object.
One may omit such empty pullback objects from a nonzero \v{C}ech nerve or
from a cochain complex whose coefficient sheaf has only the zero section
there, but not from the local reference site itself. Because
$\mathcal B_{p,s}$ is thin, the fibre product $a_i\times_a a_j$ is the
meet of $a_i$ and $a_j$ over $a$ whenever that meet exists. We write
\[
\mathcal C_{p,s}:=(\mathcal B_{p,s},\Cov_{p,s})
\]
for the resulting site.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Local object functor]\label{def:local-object-functor}
A \emph{local object functor} on $(p,s)$ is a contravariant functor
\[
F_{p,s}:\mathcal{B}_{p,s}^{op}\to\mathsf{Set}.
\]
If $b\sqsubseteq_{p,s} a$, the induced restriction map is denoted by
\[
\res_b^a:F_{p,s}(a)\to F_{p,s}(b).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Three existence levels]\label{def:existence-levels}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ and assume $M,p\Vdash \Adm_s(r)$. Write $a=[r]_p$.
In the pullback-based local reference site of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame}, overlaps are checked on the fibre products $a_i\times_a a_j$ supplied there, rather than on semilattice meets.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $M,p\Vdash \LocSec_s(r)$ if some cover $\mathcal U=\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I}\in\Cov_{p,s}(a)$ has $F_{p,s}(a_i)\neq\varnothing$ for every $i\in I$.
\item $M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)$ if some cover $\mathcal U=\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I}\in\Cov_{p,s}(a)$ carries a matching family $(\sigma_i)_{i\in I}$ with each $\sigma_i\in F_{p,s}(a_i)$ and
\[
\res_{a_i\times_a a_j}^{a_i}(\sigma_i)=\res_{a_i\times_a a_j}^{a_j}(\sigma_j).
\]
\item $M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)$ if the fibre $F_{p,s}(a)$ is nonempty.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Global sections yield compatible local sections]\label{prop:global-implies-compatible-local}
In the pullback-based site of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame}, so without assuming a meet-semilattice presentation of overlaps, if $M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)$, then
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
M,p\Vdash \LocSec_s(r).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $a=[r]_p$ and choose $\sigma\in F_{p,s}(a)$. The identity axiom for the pretopology gives the trivial cover $\{a\to a\}$. The singleton family $(\sigma)$ is therefore a compatible local family, so both compatible local sectionability and local sectionability follow immediately.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Sheafification and gluing failure}

For fixed $(p,s)$, the local reference site of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame} determines a presheaf
\[
F_{p,s}\in\mathbf{PSh}(\mathcal{B}_{p,s}).
\]
Write
\[
a_{p,s}:\mathbf{PSh}(\mathcal{B}_{p,s})\to\mathbf{Sh}(\mathcal{C}_{p,s})
\]
for the associated sheafification functor.

\begin{definition}[Separatedness at an object]\label{def:separated-at-a}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a site, let $F$ be a presheaf on $\mathcal C$, and let $a$ be an object of $\mathcal C$. We say that $F$ is \emph{separated at $a$} if for every covering family
\[
\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
\]
and every pair of sections $\sigma,\tau\in F(a)$, the equalities
\[
\res_{a_i}^{a}(\sigma)=\res_{a_i}^{a}(\tau)
\qquad
(\forall i\in I)
\]
imply $\sigma=\tau$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Separatedness on the reference slice]\label{def:slice-separated-local-object}
For an admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at $p$, write
\[
a=[r]_p,
\qquad
\mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a.
\]
The restricted local-object presheaf $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is \emph{slice-separated} if it is separated at every object of $\mathcal C_a$.
Equivalently, separatedness is required not only at the terminal object
$a$, but also at every cover member, every pullback overlap, and every
object introduced by a refinement inside the slice. This distinction is
used essentially below: equality in a sheafification value over a cover
member gives equality of the original presheaf sections only after a
further cover of that cover member, and the upgrade back to equality over
the cover member itself requires separatedness at that object.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Explicit plus--plus normal form for sheafification]\label{lem:strict-sheafification-representatives}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a site with pullbacks of covering families, let
$F$ be a presheaf of sets on $\mathcal C$, and let
$u:F\to aF$ be the sheafification unit. For every object $X$ and every
section $\xi\in(aF)(X)$ there is a covering family
\[
\{X_i\to X\}_{i\in I}
\]
and sections $\sigma_i\in F(X_i)$ such that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item for every pair $(i,j)$, the restrictions of $\sigma_i$ and
$\sigma_j$ to the pullback $X_i\times_X X_j$ agree after a covering
refinement of that pullback object;
\item the family $(u(\sigma_i))_i$ glues in the sheaf $aF$ to $\xi$;
\end{enumerate}
No strict matching family of $F$ is asserted in this first conclusion.
For an arbitrary presheaf, the compatibility obtained from a section of
\((aF)(X)\) is only compatibility after refinements of the pairwise
overlaps. This is exactly the loss of information caused by the two
successive plus-construction quotient relations. In particular, the data
in \textup{(i)} cannot be used as cover-level identity descent data unless
the refined equalities have first been upgraded to equalities on the
original pullback overlaps.

If $F$ is separated at every object of the slice $\mathcal C/X$, then the
equalities after covering refinements in \textup{(i)} upgrade to
equalities in $F(X_i\times_X X_j)$; in that case the displayed data are a
strict matching family in $F$ on the cover of $X$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We use the usual plus construction
\cite[III.5]{MacLaneMoerdijk1994}, \cite[Tags 00W1, 00W3]{StacksProject},
but we record the flattening explicitly. For an arbitrary presheaf, a
section of \(aF\) is not a matching family of \(F\)-sections on one cover;
it is a matching family of \(F^+\)-sections, and each of those
\(F^+\)-sections is itself represented by \(F\)-sections only up to a
local equivalence relation.

For an object \(T\), a section of \(F^+(T)\) is an equivalence class of
strict \(F\)-matching data
\[
\{T_\alpha\to T\}_{\alpha\in A},
\qquad
s_\alpha\in F(T_\alpha),
\]
whose restrictions agree strictly on each pullback
\(T_\alpha\times_T T_\beta\). Two such matching families define the same
element of \(F^+(T)\) precisely when their pullbacks to a common covering
refinement agree in \(F\). More explicitly, the common refinement is taken
after pulling the two covers back to each \(T_\alpha\times_TT'_\beta\);
there is no assertion that the original sections agree on
\(T_\alpha\times_TT'_\beta\) itself. In particular, if two elements of
\(F^+(T)\) are
represented by matching families
\[
\{T_\alpha\to T,s_\alpha\}_{\alpha\in A},
\qquad
\{T'_\beta\to T,t_\beta\}_{\beta\in B},
\]
then their equality in $F^+(T)$ means that, for every fibre product
$T_\alpha\times_TT'_\beta$, there is a covering family of that fibre
product on which the two pulled-back $F$-sections become equal. This is
the only equality criterion used below.

Since \(aF=(F^+)^+\), a section \(\xi\in(aF)(X)\) is represented by a
covering family
$\{Y_\ell\to X\}_{\ell\in L}$ and sections
\[
\eta_\ell\in F^+(Y_\ell)
\]
which form a strict \(F^+\)-matching family. The word ``strict'' here
refers only to equality of the \(\eta_\ell\) in \(F^+\) on the overlaps
\(Y_\ell\times_XY_{\ell'}\); after expanding the \(\eta_\ell\) to
\(F\)-data, that equality again means equality only after a further cover.
Choose, for each $\ell$, a
plus representative
\[
\{Y_{\ell m}\to Y_\ell\}_{m\in M_\ell},
\qquad
\sigma_{\ell m}\in F(Y_{\ell m})
\]
of $\eta_\ell$. By pullback stability and transitivity, the composites
$Y_{\ell m}\to X$ form a covering family of $X$.

Fix two composites $Y_{\ell m}\to X$ and $Y_{\ell' m'}\to X$, and write
\[
T:=Y_{\ell m}\times_XY_{\ell' m'}.
\]
The strict matching condition in \((F^+)^+\) says that the two restrictions
of \(\eta_\ell\) and \(\eta_{\ell'}\) to
\(Y_\ell\times_XY_{\ell'}\) are equal as elements of \(F^+\). Pull this
equality back to \(T\). Applying the just stated equality criterion in
\(F^+\) to the two chosen plus representatives gives a cover
$\{T_q\to T\}_q$ on which the restrictions of the two $F$-sections
$\sigma_{\ell m}$ and $\sigma_{\ell' m'}$ are equal in $F(T_q)$. These
are covers of the already formed pullback overlap \(T\), not replacements
for \(T\) in the \v{C}ech nerve. They give exactly the local-on-overlaps
compatibility in \textup{(i)} and nothing stronger without a separatedness
hypothesis. The image of the family
$(u(\sigma_{\ell m}))_{\ell,m}$ in $(F^+)^+(X)$ is the original
representative $(\eta_\ell)_\ell$, hence glues to $\xi$; this proves
\textup{(ii)}.

If $F$ is separated at every object of $\mathcal C/X$, then in particular
it is separated at each pullback overlap \(X_i\times_XX_j\). Two sections
over such an overlap that agree after a covering of that overlap are
therefore already equal by separatedness at the overlap object. This
converts the local-on-overlaps compatibility into strict equality on every
$X_i\times_X X_j$. Notice that no cover has been replaced at this last
step: the output is a genuine matching family on the original cover of
\(X\), not a matching family only after changing the \v{C}ech nerve.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Strictification of sheafified component representatives]\label{lem:strictify-component-representatives}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a site with pullbacks of covering families, let
$F$ be a presheaf of sets on $\mathcal C$, and let \(X\) be an object.
Assume that \(F\) is separated at every object of the slice
\(\mathcal C/X\). If \(\xi\in(aF)(X)\), then there is a cover
\[
\{X_i\to X\}_{i\in I}
\]
and sections \(\sigma_i\in F(X_i)\) which form a strict matching family on
the original pullback overlaps \(X_i\times_X X_j\), and whose images under
the sheafification unit glue to \(\xi\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Apply \Cref{lem:strict-sheafification-representatives}. This gives a
cover and \(F\)-sections whose restrictions to each original pullback
overlap agree after a further cover of that overlap. Since each overlap is
an object of \(\mathcal C/X\), the slice-separated hypothesis lets
\Cref{lem:slice-separated-refined-equality} upgrade all refined overlap
equalities to equalities over the original overlaps. The same
plus--plus representative still glues under the unit to \(\xi\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Slice-separated descent of refined equality]\label{lem:slice-separated-refined-equality}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a site with pullbacks of covering families, let $F$
be a presheaf of sets on $\mathcal C$, and let $X$ be an object. Suppose
that $F$ is separated at every object of the slice $\mathcal C/X$. If
\[
\{X_i\to X\}_{i\in I}
\]
is a cover and sections $\sigma_i\in F(X_i)$ satisfy the following
refined-overlap condition: for every pair $(i,j)$, writing
$X_{ij}:=X_i\times_X X_j$, the two restrictions of $\sigma_i$ and
$\sigma_j$ to $X_{ij}$ become equal after some covering family of
$X_{ij}$, then the family $(\sigma_i)_i$ is a strict matching family:
\[
\res_{X_{ij}}^{X_i}(\sigma_i)=\res_{X_{ij}}^{X_j}(\sigma_j)
\qquad(\forall i,j).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For each pair $(i,j)$ the overlap $X_{ij}$ is an object of the slice
$\mathcal C/X$. By hypothesis $F$ is separated at $X_{ij}$. The two
sections
\[
\res_{X_{ij}}^{X_i}(\sigma_i),
\qquad
\res_{X_{ij}}^{X_j}(\sigma_j)
\]
agree after a cover of $X_{ij}$, so separatedness at $X_{ij}$ forces
their equality in $F(X_{ij})$. This is exactly the strict matching
condition.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Equality in the unit is local equality]\label{lem:sheafification-unit-equality-local}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a site with pullbacks of covering families, let
$F$ be a presheaf of sets on $\mathcal C$, and let
$u:F\to aF$ be the sheafification unit. If
\[
u_X(\sigma)=u_X(\tau)
\qquad
(\sigma,\tau\in F(X)),
\]
then there is a covering family $\{X_i\to X\}_{i\in I}$ such that
\[
\res_{X_i}^{X}(\sigma)=\res_{X_i}^{X}(\tau)
\qquad(\forall i\in I).
\]
Conversely, equality after such a cover implies
$u_X(\sigma)=u_X(\tau)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For the plus construction, the two unit representatives of
$\sigma,\tau\in F(X)$ are represented by the identity cover of $X$. Their
images in $F^+(X)$ are equal exactly when, after a covering refinement of
that identity cover, the restrictions of $\sigma$ and $\tau$ agree in
$F$. Applying the same statement to the unit
$F^+\to(F^+)^+=aF$, equality of $u_X(\sigma)$ and $u_X(\tau)$ gives a cover
$\{Y_j\to X\}$ on which the two corresponding sections of $F^+$ agree.
For each $j$, equality in $F^+(Y_j)$ gives a further cover
$\{X_{ji}\to Y_j\}_i$ on which the restrictions of $\sigma$ and $\tau$
agree in $F$. By transitivity, the composites $X_{ji}\to X$ form the
required cover. The converse follows because $aF$ is a sheaf: if the two
unit sections agree after a cover, separatedness of the sheaf $aF$ forces
them to agree over $X$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Compatible local sectionability gives a sheafified section]\label{prop:compsec-to-sheafification}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admitted at $p$, and write $a=[r]_p$. Then, in the pullback-based thin site $\mathcal C_{p,s}$ of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame},
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\neq\varnothing.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let
\[
G:=a_{p,s}F_{p,s}.
\]
By \Cref{def:existence-levels}(ii), a proof of $M,p\Vdash\CompSec_s(r)$
supplies a cover $\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I}$ and local sections
$\sigma_i\in F_{p,s}(a_i)$ satisfying the displayed matching condition on
the pairwise pullbacks. Applying the sheafification unit on each $a_i$
gives a matching family in the sheaf $G$; the sheaf gluing axiom for $G$
therefore gives a section of $G(a)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Sheafification characterizes compatible local sectionability on slice-separated slices]\label{thm:sheafification-characterization}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admitted at $p$, write $a=[r]_p$, and assume that
$F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is slice-separated in the sense of
\Cref{def:slice-separated-local-object}. Then, in the pullback-based thin
site $\mathcal C_{p,s}$ of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame},
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\neq\varnothing.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The forward implication is \Cref{prop:compsec-to-sheafification}.
Conversely, suppose
\[
\xi\in \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a).
\]
Since sheafification is the plus--plus construction for an arbitrary
presheaf, one cannot read $\xi$ directly as a strict $F$-matching family
on the first cover that appears in a plus representative. The needed
strict representative is exactly the conclusion of
\Cref{lem:strictify-component-representatives}, applied to the restricted
presheaf \(F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\) on the slice. This gives a covering family
\[
\{b_\ell\to a\}_{\ell\in L}
\]
and sections $\tau_\ell\in F_{p,s}(b_\ell)$ satisfying the strict
equalities
\[
\res_{b_\ell\times_a b_m}^{b_\ell}(\tau_\ell)
=
\res_{b_\ell\times_a b_m}^{b_m}(\tau_m)
\qquad(\ell,m\in L).
\]
This is precisely the compatibility requirement in
\Cref{def:existence-levels}(ii). Thus the refined strict representative
exhibits
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r).
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Sheaf condition at a reference]\label{cor:sheaf-local-to-global}
In the pullback-based thin site of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame}, assume
$F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is slice-separated. If $F_{p,s}$ is already a
sheaf at $a=[r]_p$, then global and compatible-local realizability coincide:
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
If $F_{p,s}$ is already a sheaf at $a$, then the unit $F_{p,s}\to a_{p,s}F_{p,s}$ is an isomorphism at $a$. The claim therefore follows from \Cref{thm:sheafification-characterization}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Sheafification removes gluing failure]\label{cor:sheafification-removes-glue}
Let $\Sec_s^{\sharp}(r)$ denote global sectionability computed with the
sheafified local object functor $a_{p,s}F_{p,s}$. In the pullback-based
thin site of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame}, assume that
$F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is slice-separated. Then
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s^{\sharp}(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)
\]
In particular, no admitted reference remains in gluing failure after sheafification.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By definition,
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s^{\sharp}(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\neq\varnothing .
\]
The direct equivalence of
\Cref{thm:sheafification-characterization} identifies the right-hand side
with \(M,p\Vdash\CompSec_s(r)\). This avoids any need to extract strict
compatible local sections from the already sheafified functor.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Realization prestacks and connected components}

The presheaf $F_{p,s}$ records only isomorphism classes of local realizations. To recover gerbes and cohomology one needs a groupoid-valued lift whose connected components give back $F_{p,s}$ after sheafification. We use the standard prestack, stackification, and banded-gerbe conventions of \cite{Giraud1971,MacLaneMoerdijk1994,Moerdijk2002,StacksProject}. Once an abelian band has been fixed, split lifts always exist; the substantive issue is whether a chosen lift is gluing-sensitive at the terminal fibre.

\begin{definition}[Realization prestack and stackification]\label{def:realization-prestack}
Fix an admitted reference $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$, and write
\[
a=[r]_p,
\qquad
\mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a.
\]
A \emph{realization prestack} for $r$ at $p$ over the pullback-based slice site is a category fibred in groupoids
\[
\mathfrak P_r\to \mathcal C_a
\]
such that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item the pre-stackification connected-component presheaf is the restriction of the local object functor,
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)\cong F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a};
\]
\item for every object $U$ of $\mathcal C_a$ and every $x\in \mathfrak P_r(U)$, the automorphism sheaf
\[
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}(x)
\]
on $\mathcal C_a/U$ is functorially identified with the restriction of a fixed abelian sheaf $\Band$ on $\mathcal C_a$.
\end{enumerate}
Write
\[
\mathfrak L_r:=a(\mathfrak P_r)
\]
for the stackification of $\mathfrak P_r$ in groupoids \cite[Tag 02ZP]{StacksProject}.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Separated split realization prestack]\label{prop:split-realization-prestack}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admitted at $p$, write
\[
a=[r]_p,\qquad \mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,
\]
and let $\Band$ be an abelian sheaf on $\mathcal C_a$. There is a canonical split realization prestack
\[
\mathfrak P_r^{\mathrm{spl}}\to\mathcal C_a
\]
with band $\Band$ and connected-component presheaf
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r^{\mathrm{spl}})
\cong F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}.
\]
Explicitly, for every object $U$ of $\mathcal C_a$, the groupoid
$\mathfrak P_r^{\mathrm{spl}}(U)$ has object set $F_{p,s}(U)$ and
\[
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathfrak P_r^{\mathrm{spl}}(U)}(\sigma,\tau)=
\begin{cases}
\Band(U),& \sigma=\tau,\\
\varnothing,& \sigma\ne\tau,
\end{cases}
\]
with composition given by addition in $\Band(U)$. For a morphism
$f:V\to U$ in $\mathcal C_a$, restriction sends $\sigma$ to
$\res_V^U(\sigma)$ and sends an automorphism $b\in\Band(U)$ to its
restriction $b|_V\in\Band(V)$.

This split prestack is separated as a prestack in groupoids, in the
sense that every isomorphism presheaf is a sheaf, if and only if the
restricted local-object presheaf $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is
slice-separated in the sense of \Cref{def:slice-separated-local-object}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The displayed rule defines a contravariant pseudofunctor from
$\mathcal C_a$ to groupoids. Since $\mathcal C_a$ is thin, there are no
nontrivial coherence choices to make: functoriality of the object maps is
the functoriality of $F_{p,s}$, and functoriality on automorphisms is the
restriction functoriality of the sheaf $\Band$. The associated category
over $\mathcal C_a$ is therefore fibred in groupoids. Its connected
components over $U$ are exactly the objects $F_{p,s}(U)$, because
distinct objects have no morphisms between them and each automorphism
group is connected to its own object only. Hence
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r^{\mathrm{spl}})(U)\cong F_{p,s}(U)
\]
naturally in $U$.

For an object $\sigma\in F_{p,s}(U)$, the automorphism presheaf on
$\mathcal C_a/U$ is
\[
V\longmapsto \operatorname{Aut}(\sigma|_V)=\Band(V),
\]
so its sheafification is already the restriction $\Band|_{\mathcal C_a/U}$.
Thus $\mathfrak P_r^{\mathrm{spl}}$ is a realization prestack with band
$\Band$.

It remains to prove the separatedness criterion. Suppose first that
$F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is slice-separated. Let $\sigma,\tau\in
F_{p,s}(U)$ and let $\{U_i\to U\}_{i\in I}$ be a cover. A matching
family of local isomorphisms
\[
\alpha_i:\sigma|_{U_i}\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau|_{U_i}
\]
can exist only when
\[
\sigma|_{U_i}=\tau|_{U_i}
\qquad(\forall i).
\]
Slice-separatedness then gives $\sigma=\tau$. The local isomorphisms
$\alpha_i$ are therefore sections of the sheaf $\Band$ over the $U_i$,
and their matching condition is exactly the sheaf matching condition for
$\Band$. Since $\Band$ is a sheaf, the $\alpha_i$ glue uniquely to a
global element of $\Band(U)$, hence to a unique global isomorphism
$\sigma\to\tau$. Thus every isomorphism presheaf is a sheaf.

Conversely, assume that $\mathfrak P_r^{\mathrm{spl}}$ is separated. Let
$\sigma,\tau\in F_{p,s}(U)$ have equal restrictions on a cover
$\{U_i\to U\}_{i\in I}$. The identity element of $\Band(U_i)$ gives an
isomorphism
\[
\sigma|_{U_i}\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau|_{U_i}
\]
for every $i$, and these identities form a matching family. Separatedness
of the isomorphism presheaf glues them to a global isomorphism
$\sigma\to\tau$ in the split groupoid. Such a global isomorphism exists
only when $\sigma=\tau$. Therefore $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is separated
at every $U$, which is slice-separatedness.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Consequences of a chosen gluing-sensitive realization datum]\label{thm:gluing-sensitive-realization}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admitted at a state $p$, and suppose $r$ is equipped with a gluing-sensitive realization datum in the sense of \Cref{def:gluing-sensitive-realization}. Then the later branch-obstruction theory depends on this extra datum, rather than on the split prestack of \Cref{prop:split-realization-prestack}, as follows:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item the visible value classes of $r$ are identified with $\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a)$ via \Cref{thm:visible-value-components};
\item if the cofinal gerbe-adapted-cover hypothesis of \Cref{def:cech-gluing-obstruction} is added, then each visible branch determines a component gerbe as in \Cref{thm:component-gerbe-decomposition};
\item if, in addition, the terminal-fibre essential surjectivity above is used as global conservativity at $a$, and the reference slice is slice-separated when the strict predicate \(\CompSec_s\) is invoked, then the comparison between global sections and neutral component gerbes is exactly the one proved later in \Cref{prop:global-sections-vs-neutral-components} and \Cref{thm:gerbe-null-semantics}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Clause \textup{(ii)} of \Cref{def:gluing-sensitive-realization} identifies the connected components of the chosen stack $\mathfrak L_r$ with the sheafification of the local object functor. Combined with \Cref{def:realization-prestack}(ii), this is the stack-theoretic input used later to define visible branches and their component gerbes. Global conservativity at $a$ supplies the comparison between stack-global sections and actual global realizations via \Cref{prop:no-new-global-objects}. Finally, the acyclic cover data in \Cref{def:gluing-sensitive-realization} are exactly the hypotheses invoked in \Cref{def:cech-gluing-obstruction}. Together these assumptions are precisely what is needed for the later gerbe and obstruction arguments.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Components of the stackification]\label{prop:stackification-components}
In the setting of \Cref{def:realization-prestack} over the pullback-based slice site $\mathcal C_a$, so without assuming an ambient meet-semilattice presentation, the sheaf of connected components of $\mathfrak L_r$ is canonically identified with the sheafification of the restricted local object functor $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By stackification, the morphism presheaves of $\mathfrak P_r$ are sheafified and every object of $\mathfrak L_r$ is locally in the essential image of $\mathfrak P_r\to \mathfrak L_r$ \cite[Tag 02ZP]{StacksProject}. Passing to isomorphism classes therefore shows that $\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)$ is the sheafification of $\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)$. Since stacks in setoids are precisely sheaves of sets \cite[Tag 042Y]{StacksProject}, we obtain
\[
\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)\cong a\bigl(\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)\bigr).
\]
Using \Cref{def:realization-prestack}(i), this becomes
\[
\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)\cong a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}.
\qedhere
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Naturality of the stackification component map]\label{prop:stackification-component-unit-naturality}
In the setting of \Cref{def:realization-prestack}, the map of component
sheaves induced by the stackification morphism
\[
\iota_r:\mathfrak P_r\to a(\mathfrak P_r)=\mathfrak L_r
\]
is, under the identification
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)\cong F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}
\quad\text{and}\quad
\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)\cong a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a},
\]
exactly the sheafification unit
\[
u:F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\longrightarrow
a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}.
\]
Equivalently, for every object \(U\) of the slice and every
\(y\in\mathfrak P_r(U)\),
\[
[\iota_r(y)]=u_U([y])
\]
in \(\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(U)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\Cref{prop:stackification-components} identifies
\(\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)\) with the sheafification of
\(\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)\). This identification is induced by
the universal morphism from a presheaf to its sheafification: applying
\(\pi_0\) to \(\iota_r\) sends a prestack object to the same local
component, now viewed in the sheaf of components. After the defining
identification
\(\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)\cong F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\), this
universal morphism is precisely \(u\).
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Global conservativity at the terminal fibre]\label{def:global-conservativity}
In the setting of \Cref{def:realization-prestack}, now over a pullback-based slice site $\mathcal C_a$, let
\[
\iota_r:\mathfrak P_r\to \mathfrak L_r:=a(\mathfrak P_r)
\]
be the stackification morphism. We say that $\mathfrak P_r$ is \emph{globally conservative at $a$} if every object of the terminal stack fibre $(\mathfrak L_r)_a$ is isomorphic to the image of some object of the terminal prestack fibre $(\mathfrak P_r)_a$; equivalently, the induced functor
\[
(\iota_r)_a:(\mathfrak P_r)_a\to (\mathfrak L_r)_a
\]
is essentially surjective.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Terminal component effectivity]\label{def:terminal-component-effectivity}
In the setting of \Cref{def:realization-prestack}, the stackification
$\mathfrak L_r$ has \emph{effective terminal components} if the canonical
map from isomorphism classes of terminal-fibre objects to terminal sections
of the component sheaf,
\[
\pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak L_r(a)\bigr)\longrightarrow \pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a),
\]
is surjective. Equivalently, every section of the sheaf of connected
components over $a$ is represented by an actual object of
$\mathfrak L_r(a)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[No new global objects at $a$]\label{prop:no-new-global-objects}
Assume global conservativity at $a$ in the sense of \Cref{def:global-conservativity} for a realization prestack on the pullback-based slice site $\mathcal C_a$. Then
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak L_r(a)\neq\varnothing.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{def:existence-levels} and \Cref{def:realization-prestack}(i),
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
F_{p,s}(a)\neq\varnothing
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)(a)\neq\varnothing
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
(\mathfrak P_r)_a\neq\varnothing.
\]
If $(\mathfrak P_r)_a\neq\varnothing$, choose $y\in (\mathfrak P_r)_a$. Then $\iota_r(y)\in \mathfrak L_r(a)$, so $\mathfrak L_r(a)\neq\varnothing$. Conversely, if $x\in \mathfrak L_r(a)$, essential surjectivity of $(\iota_r)_a$ yields $y\in (\mathfrak P_r)_a$ with $\iota_r(y)\cong x$, so $(\mathfrak P_r)_a\neq\varnothing$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Representative-rigid lift]\label{def:representative-rigid-lift}
In the setting of \Cref{def:realization-prestack} over the pullback-based slice site $\mathcal C_a$, a realization prestack
\[
\mathfrak P_r\to \mathcal C_a
\]
is called \emph{representative-rigid} if for every object $U$ of $\mathcal C_a$ there is a map
\[
s_U:F_{p,s}(U)\to \Ob(\mathfrak P_r(U))
\]
such that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item the image of $s_U(\sigma)$ in
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)(U)\cong F_{p,s}(U)
\]
is $\sigma$;
\item for every morphism $f:V\to U$ in $\mathcal C_a$ and every $\sigma\in F_{p,s}(U)$,
\[
f^*s_U(\sigma)=s_V(\res_V^U(\sigma)).
\]
\end{enumerate}
Here \(f^*\) is computed with respect to a fixed split cleavage of the
fibred groupoid \(\mathfrak P_r\), so pullbacks compose on the nose. Thus
the isomorphism classes of local objects admit a strict functorial choice of
representatives. Without this split-cleavage convention the same condition
would have to be stated with coherent cartesian isomorphisms, and the proof

  176: v|_U.
  177: \]

  181: \]

  190: v|_U,
  191: \]
  192: so $y$ lies locally in $\mathfrak E_r[v]$. Hence $\Phi_v$ is an equivalence of stacks. The automorphism-sheaf maps of $\Phi_v$ are the restrictions of the automorphism-sheaf maps of $\Phi$, so the band is transported by the same isomorphism $\theta$.
  193: \end{proof}

  195: \begin{lemma}[Transport of Giraud classes under banded equivalence]\label{lem:giraud-class-transport}
  196: Let $\mathfrak G$ be a $\Band$-banded gerbe and let $\mathfrak G'$ be a $\Band'$-banded gerbe on the same site $\mathcal C$. Suppose
  197: \[
  198: \Phi:\mathfrak G\to\mathfrak G'
  199: \]
  200: is an equivalence of stacks whose induced maps on automorphism sheaves identify the bands through an isomorphism
  201: \[
  202: \theta:\Band\xrightarrow{\sim}\Band'.
  203: \]
  204: Then
  205: \[
  206: \theta_*[\mathfrak G]=[\mathfrak G']
  207: \qquad\text{in}\qquad
  208: H^2(\mathcal C,\Band').
  209: \]
  210: \end{lemma}
  211: 
  212: \begin{proof}
  213: Choose a cover $\{U_i\to U\}$ which locally trivializes $\mathfrak G$, objects $x_i\in\mathfrak G(U_i)$, and descent isomorphisms
  214: \[
  215: \varphi_{ij}:x_i|_{U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim}x_j|_{U_{ij}}.
  216: \]
  217: The usual Giraud cocycle $c_{ijk}\in\Band(U_{ijk})$ is defined by
  218: \[
  219: \varphi_{ik}^{-1}\varphi_{jk}\varphi_{ij}=c_{ijk}\in\operatorname{Aut}(x_i|_{U_{ijk}})\cong\Band(U_{ijk}).
  220: \]
  221: Applying $\Phi$ gives local objects $\Phi(x_i)$ of $\mathfrak G'$ and comparison isomorphisms $\Phi(\varphi_{ij})$. The corresponding defect is
  222: \[
  223: \Phi(\varphi_{ik})^{-1}\Phi(\varphi_{jk})\Phi(\varphi_{ij})
  224: =
  225: \Phi(c_{ijk}),
  226: \]
  227: which is identified with $\theta(c_{ijk})$ under the band isomorphism. Thus the transported local trivialization of $\mathfrak G'$ has cocycle $\theta_*(c)$. Refining the cover or changing the local objects changes both cocycles by the corresponding coboundary, exactly as in the standard local-trivialization classification of banded gerbes \cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}, \cite[Tag 06NY]{StacksProject}. Hence the cohomology class of $\mathfrak G'$ is $\theta_*[\mathfrak G]$.
  228: 
  229: The functoriality invoked here is the usual naturality of Giraud's classification with respect to a morphism of bands. Equivalently, the assignment
  230: \[
  231: (\mathfrak G,\Band)\longmapsto [\mathfrak G]\in H^2(\mathcal C,\Band)
  232: \]
  233: is functorial for banded equivalences: an equivalence of gerbes whose automorphism sheaves are identified by $\theta$ transports a local object--isomorphism--defect cocycle to its pointwise image under $\theta$, and this construction is independent of all choices after passage to cohomology.
  234: \end{proof}

id               : http://arxiv.org/abs/2308.06336v1
title            : A bundle perspective on contextuality: Empirical models and simplicial distributions on bundle 
                   scenarios
updated          : 2023-08-11T18:19:49Z
link             : {link, link}
summary          : This paper provides a bundle perspective to contextuality by introducing new categories of 
                   contextuality scenarios based on bundles of simplicial complexes and simplicial sets. The former 
                   approach generalizes earlier work on the sheaf-theoretic perspective on contextuality, and the 
                   latter extends simplicial distributions, a more recent approach to contextuality formulated in the 
                   language of simplicial sets. After constructing our bundle categories, we also construct functors 
                   that relate them and natural isomorphisms that allow us to compare the notions of contextuality 
                   formulated in two languages. We are motivated by applications to the resource theory of 
                   contextuality, captured by the morphisms in these categories. In this paper, we develop the main 
                   formalism and leave applications to future work.
category         : {category, category, category}
published        : 2023-08-11T18:19:49Z
comment          : 50 pages, 2 figures
primary_category : primary_category
author           : {Rui Soares Barbosa, Aziz Kharoof, Cihan Okay}

codex
1. **HEADLINE RESULT.** On the chosen good-cover site of \(\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta}S^2\), a nonzero finite abelian group \(G\) occurs as a pure two-branch resolution kernel exactly when \(d(G)\le 2\beta\) and \(G\) is not a cyclic \(p\)-group. **Theorem `thm:bouquet-two-branch-resolution`.**

2. **IS IT ACTUALLY NEW? REPACKAGING.** Abramsky–Mansfield–Barbosa, *The Cohomology of Non-Locality and Contextuality* (2012), already encode failure of global sections by Čech classes; Giraud’s *Cohomologie non abélienne* classifies banded gerbes by \(H^2\), while Okay–Raussendorf develop the corresponding homotopical contextuality framework. The displayed classification results from freely installing prescribed maps \(\mathbb Z^\beta\to G\) and applying the standard structure theorem for finite abelian groups, so the terminology is new but the mathematical content is not.

3. **DEPTH OF THE MATHEMATICS.** Mostly bookkeeping: stackification, Giraud \(H^2\), the universal coefficient theorem, character annihilators, quotient identities, and finite-abelian decomposition are assembled carefully, but no new obstruction mechanism or hard argument is introduced.

4. **TOP-JOURNAL VERDICT. TIER-4.** The manuscript is too conditional and synthetically engineered for TIER-3: its strongest examples arise in a model class explicitly defined to admit arbitrary prescribed local-object and gerbe data.

5. **WHAT WOULD MOVE IT UP ONE TIER.** Prove that every finite Abramsky–Brandenburger empirical model \(E\) canonically and refinement-functorially determines a banded realization stack \(\mathfrak E_E\), independent of covers and presentations, with strong contextuality equivalent to nonvanishing of its branch obstruction classes and with the resulting visible quotient a complete invariant of gluing failure.

6. **WEAKEST LINK.** Lemma `lem:generated-subsite-model-interface`, especially clause (iv): contractibility in the ambient good-cover realization does not by itself establish the asserted derived-cohomology calculation for the bespoke finite formal site, yet that calculation supports every realization and bouquet theorem.

**Verification note.** No manuscript-local `artifacts/`, verification scripts, outputs, or `oracle_sprint_*.md` files exist in the directory, archive, or repository history; `PIPELINE.md` reports \(0/13\) claims formally verified.
